# Ore decisions (settled 2026-10-09)

Started as a two-round design interview; every line since is one of Tyler's picks. A decision is a pick for its case, not a law (Tyler, 2026-11-20). Follow it by default. When evidence says it is wrong for the case at hand, raise it with him (the rule, the evidence, what you would do instead) instead of designing around it; it changes only with his yes, and the change is recorded here.

How a new decision is written:
- Say **why** in its own words, and when it rests on a number or a limit, what would reopen it ("revisit if…"), so a later thread can tell when the reason no longer holds.
- Absolute words ("never", "only", "no others", "nowhere else") are for licences, the safety of his data and the learning science. A taste call or a number says what it is for now.
- A one-off fix is a test, not a decision. It becomes a rule here or in `CLAUDE.md` only when the same mistake happens twice, or Tyler asks for one.
- A decision that keeps collecting exceptions or amendments is rewritten around its reason.

Old scope and build-order lines that no longer steer anything are at the end, under **Archive**.

## Purpose
- D1. Optimize for *explaining concepts in my own words* and *applying them in my work*, not fact recall. Primary review unit is an open-ended prompt graded against a rubric. Atomic cards exist for facts and terms, secondary.
- D2. Standalone product. No engram (Tyler never used it; it felt awkward). Hard break from NotionRPG: no Notion, no shared schema. Ideas only, filtered through the evidence brief.
- D3. Mac first. Phone later only if free. Never trade Mac UX for portability. (Amended 2026-10-27: a full iPhone app is being built alongside, D78.)
- D4. Personal tool, single user, local only, no accounts, no sync, no API keys. (Amended 2026-10-27: sync between his Mac and iPhone is planned, D78; still no user API keys.) (Amended 2026-11-06: Ore is open source, D120; the old ban on AGPL/GPL dependencies is lifted on the Mac.)

## Data
- D5. Files are canonical, in a library folder outside the code repo (default `~/Ore`), under local git, not pushed anywhere. SQLite under `.index/` is a rebuildable index. iCloud only after a phone client exists. (2026-10-27: the phone client exists and the files are laid out for two devices, D78.)
- D6. Agents (Claude Code, Codex) read and write the library directly, guided by the library `AGENTS.md`, and use the `ore` CLI for anything with invariants (IDs, review log, anchoring). MCP server later, as a thin CLI wrapper, only if a chat app without a shell needs it.

## AI
- D7. AI runs through the `claude` and `codex` CLIs on Tyler's subscriptions. No in-app API calls. One adapter so the vendor is swappable.
- D8. Deterministic work (ingest, transcripts, indexing, scheduling) runs in the app or CLI with no model. AI enrichment (prequestions, skill and topic tags, card drafts, topic merges, FSRS optimizer) runs immediately on capture in the background, with a nightly idempotent catch-up as a Claude Code Desktop scheduled task. Design for "runs when awake, catches up once".
- D9. Err toward trusting AI for convenience. Drafts go live without an approval inbox; Tyler fixes mistakes on the fly (edit the card, or chat about it). Never describe AI generation as learning.

## Stack
- D10. Tauri 2 shell. TypeScript core on Bun, shared by the CLI and the app. React UI. One FSRS implementation: ts-fsrs (FSRS-6, defaults until ~1,000 reviews, optimizer via the native binding, run monthly).
- D11. Native needs that Tauri lacks (read selected text from another app via Accessibility; later, screenshot OCR via Vision) are tiny standalone Swift CLI helpers compiled with `swiftc`. No Xcode project.

## Capture
- D15. (Research folded into Question and the placeholders became "Note…" on 2026-10-28, D91.) Four highlight intents: **Keep**, **Card**, **Question**, **Research**. Every intent accepts an optional one-line note. Selecting text highlights instantly as Keep; keys 1–4 (Keep, Card, Question, Research) change intent (replaced K/C/Q/R on 2026-10-21); the popup appears only for the note. The note field shows a generative placeholder per intent, causal rather than relevance or rating: Card "This works because… / connects to…" (2026-10-23, `docs/research/brief-notetaking.md` check), Question "My question… my guess:", Keep and Research "In my words…" ("why it matters" dropped 2026-10-22, `docs/research/brief-notetaking.md`). It is focused only when the intent is Card. "How I will use this" is a note, not a button. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the types are now a plain highlight (1), Key point (2), Question (3) and Try it (4), D118, D127.)
- D17. **Research** creates a topic with status `wanted`; AI finds 3–5 sources and presents them; one key ingests any. Never auto-ingest. (2026-10-28: there is no Research highlight any more, D91; research starts from a question's **Begin research**, D75, and returns sources only, with no topic written, D95.)

## Learning loop
- D18. Prequestions: on capture, AI writes 2–4 broad questions on the document's central ideas. Shown at the top of the reader, one-line answers or "no idea", stored, unscored. Re-asked closed-book when finishing the source (text hidden), followed by a short free recall ("anything else you remember?"), then AI feedback. Feedback always comes after the attempt. After the recall, one optional line asks "Where would you use this, and what does it connect to?" (Adler and Ahrens; practitioner-backed, not evidence-backed; replaced "use or dispute" 2026-10-23). The finish and chapter feedback also says in one line whether his section lines (D32) state main ideas in his own words (2026-10-23). EPUBs also get a short closed-book recall with AI feedback at each chapter marker; the book end keeps the full finish (both 2026-10-22). The finish and chapter recalls accept typed free recall or a map drawn from memory (D35), never both in one recall (2026-10-23).
- D19. Two review item kinds, both FSRS-scheduled: `card` (atomic Q/A or cloze, facts and terms) and `prompt` (open-ended explain/apply, 2–4 rubric points). Only Card-intent highlights, My notes (D31), maps (D35) and, after the finish, the prequestions produce items; items drafted from My notes are credited "from your notes" (2026-10-22), and items drafted from relationships drawn in a map "from your map" (2026-10-23). Each prequestion becomes a `prompt` (rubric from the document and the ideas he missed) so central ideas get spaced retrieval (2026-10-23). Keep/Question/Research never do by themselves. (2026-10-28, as D91 records: a Keep with a note is also drafted from when the note marks a key point to remember, prompt `enrich`; Research is gone.)
- D20. Card finishing: AI drafts from every Card-intent highlight. The draft's first appearance in review is the finishing step: answer cold → see AI draft answer → edit if needed → live. No approval queue.
- D21. Grading: confidence tap before reveal; Tyler types an answer; AI grades against the rubric and proposes a rating 1–4; one key accepts, any other overrides. Tyler's rating is what FSRS records. A miss triggers one relearn pass in the same session. (Amended 2026-10-24: Fact cards are flipped, not typed, D59.) (Amended 2026-11-11, Tyler: Review's key bar names the rating ↵ accepts as its button does, "↵ Hard", never "↵ Suggested".)
- D22. Skill priority never touches FSRS math or reorders by importance alone. Priority maps to desired retention (P1 0.92, P2 0.88, P3 0.80, lowered from 0.95/0.90/0.85 on 2026-10-25, D65; a card takes the max of its skills) and breaks ties in the daily queue under overload.
- D23. No quota, no streaks, no XP, no daily cap. Review is open-ended; an optional 10-minute time box is a later UI option. Display fairly basic stats at the skill level (items read, items live, reviews, retention using the Anki-adapted metric; never show a trend arrow at small n). Track session starts. (Amended 2026-10-24: a daily time goal and a new-card limit, D60; motivation rules, D62.)
- D24. Interleave topics; place confusable concepts adjacent; keep definitions grouped by source. Vary application prompts rather than rewording cards. (Built 2026-10-25 as remixed cards, D66.)
- D25. Skills (owned by Tyler, 9): AI P1, Learning P1, Teaching P1, Storytelling & Writing P2, Creator Business P2, Creativity P2, Habits P3, Focus & Attention P3, Self-Regulation & Motivation P3. Topics are AI-created and AI-merged. Enrich assigns only skills that obviously fit and never stretches one; when none does, it records a `suggested_skill` on the item, shown as a dashed chip. One click creates it (P2) and files the item, its cards and topics under it; AI never creates a skill itself (2026-10-22).

## UI
- D26. Dark Raycast-style: compact, keyboard-first, command palette, no chrome. Optional light reading surface. Add features only when necessary. (2026-10-24: made delightful within D62.) (2026-10-28: the light surface is built: Settings › Reading page sets the source pane to Light, Apple Books' White; sidebar and side panel stay dark. Text size ⌘+ ⌘− scales only the reading text and keeps the top paragraph in place. ⌘0 stays Go to Home, so Default text size is in Settings and ⌘K.)
- D27. Reader: text column 55–70 characters per line; notes in a right margin anchored to their passage; prequestion strip at top, collapsible; popup above selection. No reading-speed metrics or progress gamification.
- D28. Navigation: the sources list has three states, Inbox (unread), Reading (in progress) and Done, shown as a segmented control at the top of the list. The sidebar holds skill filters and, once they exist, the other places: Library (by skill and topic, AI-organized; wanted topics live here) and Review (due). The app reopens on the last state and skill filter, Reading on first run. Everything else via the command palette. (Amended 2026-10-22: states moved from the sidebar to top tabs.) (Amended 2026-10-24, Tyler's pick: two tabs, **To read** and **Done**, ⌘1 and ⌘2. To read lists started sources first with a filled dot, then the ones not opened yet with a hollow dot and a dimmer title. The stored statuses stay `inbox`, `reading` and `done`; opening a source still marks it `reading`, which is what lights its dot. Sources added with no skill show under All sources. The app reopens on To read on first run.) (Amended 2026-10-24: Home is the default page, D58.) (Amended 2026-10-28, Tyler: the dots are gone; a source not opened yet keeps only its dimmer title.) (Amended 2026-10-25: the tabs became the list title's view menu, D74.) The sidebar's Library place is dropped (D95); topics stay in the files, where they order review (D24). (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: unopened sources are not dimmed, D134.)

## v1 build (2026-10-21)
- D29. The first app build covers sources only: add (URL, file picker, drag and drop), Inbox/Reading/Done lists, the reader with highlights and notes, prequestion attempts, and the finish step (D18). Review, Library, stats, the hotkey capture panel and margin notes come later. The UI shows skills only; topics stay in the files but are not shown yet. Never add a "review your highlights" screen or show an AI summary of a source in place of Tyler's own notes. (Since built: Review D59, skill strength D63 and D90, margin notes. Still not built: Library and the hotkey capture panel.)
- D30. The app runs the installed `ore` CLI (`bun link`) with the login-shell PATH, as the Raycast extension does, rather than a bundled sidecar. One code path for writes, no compile step; the app needs the checkout and Bun present, which is true for a single-user Mac. (Amended 2026-10-27: on iPhone the same command code runs inside the app, D78. Amended 2026-11-08, Tyler: the installed app runs its own copy of the CLI, compiled to bytecode by `app:install` from the checkout, because each call from source spent ~50 ms loading modules; at 240 sources that made Home 30%, opening a source 40% and starting review 55% slower. Same command code and one write path; the reinstall hook rebuilds it when `packages/` changes, and `tauri dev`, the terminal and the `/ore` skill keep running the linked checkout.)

## Active note-taking (2026-10-22)
Evidence and rationale: `docs/research/brief-notetaking.md`.
- D31. Each source has one **My notes** area: Markdown, Tyler's writing only, editable any time with the source open, shown with the reader. The finish recall (D18), each chapter recall and the use-and-connections line are saved into it as the first draft; Tyler refines it with the text open.
- D32. At each heading (or about once per screen of headingless text) the margin shows one optional line: "Main point of this section, in your words". One key dismisses it; no counter, no nag. (Removed 2026-11-03, Tyler: it was the only uninvited prompt in the reader, sat between the text and the note as a second place to write, and open-book summarizing is the weakest learning move there; chapter recall and Finish carry the own-words step. Lines he writes under `## ` headings in the source note are still read back by the feedback.)
- D33. Deferred: one note linked to several highlights ("one idea, many passages"). Revisit once My notes is in use, scoped as one idea linked across several sources (Ahrens).
- D34. Note guardrails:
  1. No notes archive, notes-review screen or canvas for arranging notes or highlights. The one drawing surface is the map drawn from memory (D35, 2026-10-23). Outside the reader, notes and maps appear only inside retrieval items after an attempt, and in search.
  2. No AI in the highlight popup, note fields or My notes: no prefilled or suggested note text, and no "copy highlight into note" action.
  3. Nothing from AI is shown before Tyler's attempt: prequestions, section lines, the finish step, chapter recalls, and any future "explain this passage".
  4. The finish step and chapter recalls hide the text, his highlights and notes, My notes and the map. In review, a card's source note or highlight appears only after he answers.
  5. Notes stay optional: one key dismisses any prompt, and nothing counts un-noted highlights.
  6. Highlight notes live in the right margin, anchored to their passage (D27).
  7. No handwriting or input-medium settings.
  8. Judge note features on retention days later, not on how a session feels. Stats report retention for items drafted from his own words (a highlight note or My notes) vs from highlights without a note, as plain numbers, null below 10 reviews (D23).
  9. Card drafts from opinion or contested sources say "According to <author>, …". A central idea gets a pair of items (definition and application), not one orphan card.

## Maps (2026-10-23)
Evidence and rationale: `docs/research/brief-diagramming.md`. Deferred (D95): basics first. Diagrams in notes (D40) are built.
- D35. Each source can have one **map**: a freehand whiteboard drawing (Excalidraw, MIT; tldraw excluded for its license key), stored as `map.excalidraw` beside `notes.md`, fonts bundled, no network.
  1. It starts from memory: at the finish and chapter recalls he chooses typed recall or a map, never both (D18). Mapping with the text open lost to plain recall, and a map before a recall added nothing.
  2. After that it is his, like My notes (D31): editable any time with the source open, hidden during recalls (D34.4), no AI drawing or text in it (D34.2).
  3. After his attempt, AI shows its own map beside his with comparison prompts ("what does it have that yours lacks? which link differs?") and names missing ideas and wrong links. It never edits his map or sets a rating (D21, D34.3).
  4. Relationships he drew can seed `prompt` items credited "from your map" (D19).
  5. No map gallery or map-review screen (D34.1). Stats report retention for items from map recalls vs typed recalls, null below 10 reviews (D34.8).
  6. Freehand was chosen over typed boxes and arrows although an on-screen drawing tool erased drawing's benefit in one study (d = 0.87 on paper vs −0.16; F&M ch04). Retention (item 5) decides whether it stays.
  7. Deferred: a cross-source map (D33), a compare-and-contrast matrix.

## Sources and skills in the app (2026-10-23)
- D36. Skills can carry an icon (one emoji, or a MingCute icon stored as `mc:<name>` (Phosphor `ph:<name>` icons picked before still draw) and tinted with the skill's color, picked in a Notion-style picker with Emoji and Icons tabs, search, recents, random and remove), a named color (gray, red, orange, yellow, green, teal, blue, purple, pink) and a **collection** (one per skill), all optional, stored in `skills.yaml`. Collections only group skills in the sidebar, in the order they first appear, with the rest under "Other"; they never touch priority, retention or the queue (D22). Right-clicking a skill, or "Edit skill" in the palette while a skill filter is on, edits these through `ore skills set`. (Amended 2026-10-24: a skill also drags into another sidebar section to change its collection, and the palette has **New skill…** and **New collection…**; a collection is created with its first skill, since an empty one cannot be stored.) (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: no emoji: pixel icons only, D157.) (Amended 2026-11-20, Tyler: on the Mac, ⌘K's **New skill…** opens the sidebar's own name field instead of naming the skill in the palette, and the Mac sidebar lists the skills in no collection first, above the collections, with no heading over them when there are collections (he found "Other" unnecessary there; they can't be folded, and a new skill among them comes from ⌘K or a right-click on the sidebar). Why: the palette filed a new skill under the collection of the page he happened to be on, with no way to change it; a blank row at the top guesses nothing, is in sight however long the list is, and is dragged (or "Move to") into its collection from there. Home and the iPhone still list "Other" last. Revisit if unfiled skills pile up at the top, or if he wants Home and the phone to match.) (Amended 2026-11-21, Tyler's pick from the round 8 list: the iPhone's drawer follows the Mac's sidebar, the skills in no collection first with no heading, so a skill sits in the same place on both. One function gives both lists their order, `sidebarGroups`. Home still lists "Other" last, for now.)
- D37. The reader header shows where a source came from: the site's domain for a web source, "Original PDF/EPUB/…" for a file (the copy kept at capture as `source.*`). **O** opens it (the page in the browser, the file in its default app); **⇧⌘R** reveals it in Finder. Both work on the selected row in the sources list, both are rebindable, and both are off during the finish step and chapter recalls (D34.4). ⌘O stays "Open file" (add). Adding or changing a source's URL is deferred. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: it opens with ⇧⌘O, D232.)

## Notes editor, links and skill notes (proposed 2026-10-23)
Tyler accepted D38, D39, D41 and D42 as written on 2026-10-23; D40 is built with the D35 map. Spike result (D38): Milkdown, which kept toggles and links as written where TipTap rewrote 9 lines and dropped toggles and callouts. Links are stored as `[text](ore:item/<id>)` and `[text](ore:skill/<id>)`: the ID plus the text, in plain Markdown. Evidence: `docs/research/brief-notetaking.md`, its Fiorella & Mayer and Ahrens checks, and `brief-diagramming.md`. Build order: D38, D39, D40 (after the D35 map), D41. Each stays only if retention says so (D34.8).
- D38. **Block editor for notes.** My notes (D31) and skill notes (D41) get a Notion-style editor: a `/` menu, Markdown shortcuts (`#`–`###` headings, lists, `[ ]` checkboxes, `>` quotes, code), toggles, callouts, images and diagram blocks (D40). The file stays plain Markdown and canonical (D5), so only blocks Markdown can store: toggles as `<details><summary>`, callouts as `> [!note]` quotes. `## ` headings stay real headings, since recall feedback reads section lines from them (D32). Saves still go through `ore notes --set` (D30). The library is MIT and round-trips Markdown (Milkdown or TipTap); a one-hour spike picks it by loading and saving a real notes.md without changing lines he did not edit. BlockNote is out (GPL extension packages, D4). No AI in the editor and hidden during recalls, as before (D34.2, D34.4). Refines D31. (Amended 2026-11-06, Tyler: Milkdown stays and gets Notion's layer on top: a ⋮⋮ block handle to drag and a block menu (Turn into, Duplicate, Move, Delete), a format bar over selected text, and text colours and highlights in the skill palette, stored as `<span data-color>` and `<mark>` HTML that GitHub, Obsidian and an AI still read. Tables are GFM tables (a header row and at least one row, plain text cells); one wider than the note scrolls sideways in place, and the skill note has a widen button that gives the note the whole page until clicked again (Tyler, 2026-11-06, option C). Columns and nested pages wait. Each piece also ships for the iPhone: there a bar over the keyboard does what keys and the ⋮⋮ do on the Mac (+ for the / menu, to-do, bullet, heading, [[ link, indent, outdent, ⋮⋮ for move, duplicate and delete), turns into the format bar over selected text, and the skill note opens full screen from a row on the skill page (Tyler, 2026-11-06, option B). Dragging a block by touch is left out: iOS keeps long-press for selecting text. (Amended 2026-11-08: screenshots and images pasted or dropped on a note go into it, copied into `images/` beside it, not into the library, as in Notion.) (Amended 2026-11-08: equations, Notion's /equation and /math, typed `$$x$$` too, stored as the reader reads them: `$$…$$` inline and a `$$` block, a single `$` staying a dollar; drawn with KaTeX, a click opens the TeX.) Evidence: a load-and-save test where Milkdown changed 0 of 31 lines, TipTap 5 and BlockNote 12, BlockNote dropping `[[` link targets.)
- D39. **Links and backlinks.** In any note, `[[` opens a picker over sources, skills and skill notes and inserts a link that stores the target's ID plus its display text, so a rename never breaks it. Each source and skill note shows a "Linked from" list: the linking note, its heading and the line with the link. No link types: the sentence around the link says how the two relate. AI never adds links (D34.2), but a link and its sentence can seed a `prompt` ("How does X in A relate to Y in B?") credited "from your links". Links are searchable; `ore doctor` reports links that no longer resolve. Replaces the D33 deferral with its cross-source scope; amends D19 (new item source) and D34.8 (stats add retention for items from links).
- D40. **Diagram block.** A note can embed Excalidraw drawings, stored as `diagrams/<slug>.excalidraw` beside the note and referenced with a Markdown image link, written through the CLI like the map (D30). In the note a diagram shows as a static preview; click or Enter opens the full editor in an overlay, Esc saves and closes. No canvas that stays interactive inside the scrolling note, because it captures scroll and keys. The D35 map keeps its role: only a map drawn from memory at a recall counts as a recall attempt and gets the AI comparison (D35.1, D35.3). Other diagrams are ordinary notes: enrich reads their text and arrows with the note, nothing compares them. Hidden during recalls (D34.4). Built on the D35 setup (bundled fonts, CSP). Amends D35 (a source has its map plus any diagrams in notes) and D34.1 (the map is no longer the only drawing surface). (Tyler, 2026-11-06: in the reader the editor opens beside the source, over the side panel's sheet, so he can draw while he reads; ⤢ or ⇧⌘↵ swaps it with the full window, remembered. A new diagram is linked into the note at its first save, not at Done. Skill notes and the phone keep the full window.)
- D41. **Skill notes.** Each skill can have one note, `skills/<skill-id>/notes.md` with its diagrams beside it: his writing only, in the D38 editor. Opening an empty skill note first offers a closed-book recall ("What do you know about <skill>, across what you've read?") with sources and notes hidden (D34.4); one key skips it (D34.5). The recall is saved as the first draft, then he refines it with sources and links open. It seeds items credited "from your notes", like My notes (D19). No AI feedback on the skill recall at first; add it only if retention earns it. Opened from the skill in the sidebar or palette; no "notes due", no reminders to revisit (D23). Stats report retention for items from skill notes separately (D34.8). Amends D34.1 (a writing surface outside the reader) and D19 (new item source).
- D42. **Not doing:** freeform notes tied to no source or skill (a stray thought goes in the skill note it belongs to); a graph view; a browse-all-notes screen (D34.1 otherwise stands); Notion blocks Markdown cannot store (columns, databases, synced blocks); AI-written or AI-suggested links.

## Reader and skill page (2026-10-23)
Tyler accepted D43 to D47 as written on 2026-10-23, and kept a browse-all-notes view out (D34.1, D42). Questions are listed with `ore questions [--skill s]`.
- D43. **Names.** The app and docs say: **source** (the captured text, "source content" only where it could be confused), **highlights**, **source note** (was "My notes", D31; the file stays `notes.md`), **skill note** (D41), **questions** (Question-intent highlights, D15) and **cards** for every review item. The two kinds show as **Fact** (`card`) and **Explain** (`prompt`); file fields, the CLI and the Card intent keep their names.
- D44. **Source note as a split screen.** The source note opens as a second pane beside the source, not a narrow sidebar. A divider between them drags to resize (from 280 px to 60% of the window), double-click resets it, and the width is remembered. The source column keeps 55 to 70 characters per line, centered in its pane (D27), and highlight notes stay in the source's margin. With the note closed, a button at the top right of the reader shows **+ Note** when the source note is empty and **Note** with a dot when it has text; it and the existing key both toggle the pane. Hidden during recalls (D34.4). Amends D27 and D31.
- D45. **Position bar and outline.** A 2 px bar under the reader header fills with how far down the source you are: no percent, no time left, no reading speed (D27 stands). A floating outline sits at the source's right edge, like Notion's: one short line per heading (H1 to H3, or EPUB chapters), the current one brighter. Hover expands it to heading titles, click jumps. No headings, no outline. Both are hidden during recalls (D34.4). Amends D27 (position, not progress gamification).
- D46. **Skill page.** Clicking a skill in the sidebar opens its page instead of only filtering the list. Top: one quiet row of the D23 stats (sources done, cards live, reviews, retention, "—" below 10 reviews, no trends). Below, three sections: the skill note (D41, edited in place), questions (D47), and the skill's sources with the Inbox/Reading/Done tabs, which replace the sidebar filter (D28). A source row shows a dot when it has a source note and opens the reader. No combined view of all source notes on the page (D34.1, D42 stand). Amends D28 and D41 (the skill note is opened from this page). (Amended since: the stats row is gone, D72; Find sources is Begin research, D75; the tabs are the list title's view menu, D74.)
  - 2026-10-24, Tyler OK'd: two columns. Left is the skill note under a short encouraging line (never a streak or target, D23), with a + that shows ⇧⌘N on hover and, while the note is empty, a "Warm up from memory" card that opens the D41 recall as a popup. Right is a side panel with the four stats as tiles, Questions, and Sources, each foldable, with a summary (open count, inbox/reading/done) in the header.
  - 2026-10-24 follow-up: less text, with explanations in ⓘ hover hints. Questions and Sources each have a + in their header. A typed question is saved as a question snippet, as Raycast's Capture Question does. Sources' + opens the ⌘N panel, and anything added from a skill page is filed under that skill. The panel's divider resizes it like the source note: 440px by default, from 300px up to 60% of the window, remembered.
  - 2026-10-24, Tyler picked layout A of three mockups: the side panel felt secondary, and a long note must not bury questions and sources. Below, two equal columns that each scroll on their own: the note card on the left, the Questions and Sources cards stacked on the right (each foldable, with its + in the header). A bar between the columns drags the split from 30% to 70%, remembered; double-click resets it to half. Under 1000px the columns stack and the page scrolls. No "Up next" card for now.
- D47. **Questions.** Built from what exists, no new item type: the skill's Question-intent highlights (D15), each with its passage, his note and a jump to the passage, open ones first, answered ones collapsed. Two actions per question: **Find sources** (now **Begin research**, D75) runs D17 research (3 to 5 sources, one key adds one, never auto-add), and **Answered** sets the highlight's status to `done`. Answering means writing it in a source or skill note; the app does not ask for proof. Questions never become cards by themselves (D19), and nothing counts or badges open questions (D23). Deferred: a question typed on the skill page with no passage behind it.

## Video sources (2026-10-24)
Tyler accepted D48 to D51 on 2026-10-24 with the edits below (YouTube embed instead of a local copy, speed up to 2x, timestamps in a left gutter, "Start from here"). Pattern: the Eleven Reader iOS player (his screen recording, 2026-10-23). The YouTube ingest keeps the raw captions as `source.vtt`; auto-captions carry per-word times, manual subtitles only per-line times.
- D48. **Player above the transcript.** A YouTube source opens in the reader with the video pinned at the top of the column, 16:9, at most 40% of the window height; the title scrolls away and the transcript scrolls under it. The D44 split pane, highlights, notes and the D18 prequestions work as for any source. One control row under the video: play/pause, back 15 s, forward 30 s, a scrubber with elapsed and total time, and a speed button. Clicking the video plays or pauses it; YouTube's own controls are off. The D45 position bar is hidden for video, since the scrubber shows position; a transcript has no headings, so no outline. The video is hidden with the text during the finish step and chapter recalls (D34.4). Playback is the YouTube embed (needs the network, speeds up to 2x); no local copy, since a download is slow to start and needs cleaning up.
- D49. **Spoken-text highlight and follow.** Once a video has started, text already spoken is at full brightness and text not yet spoken is dimmed, moving word by word (auto-captions) or by evenly spread words within each caption line (manual subtitles). Times come from `source.vtt` at read time; `body.md` and highlight anchors are unchanged. The transcript follows playback, keeping the current line about a third of the way down. Any scroll by Tyler (trackpad, wheel, keys, scrollbar) turns following off. When the current word is off screen, a **Back to current** pill appears under the video or at the bottom of the column with an arrow pointing toward it; clicking it scrolls back and turns following on. Seeking (the skip buttons, the scrubber, a timestamp, Start from here) also turns it back on. Each paragraph's `[mm:ss]` shows in a muted gutter to the left of the text, never inside it, and seeks on click; ⌥-click on a word seeks to it. The highlight popup on a video source has **Start from here**, which plays from the selection's first word; on a fresh selection it also drops the Keep highlight the selection made (D15), on an existing highlight it keeps it.
- D50. **Speed, keys and resume.** The speed button opens a popover with a slider from 0.5x to 2x in 0.05 steps and presets 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.25, 1.5 and 2; a change applies at once. One rate for every video: changing it in one video changes it for all. Keys (rebindable, D37): Space play/pause, ← back 15 s, → forward 30 s, `[` and `]` speed down and up by 0.25. The playback position is saved every few seconds and on pause or close, the way the reader keeps scroll position, and the source reopens paused at that point with following on; a video played to its end reopens at the start, paused (Tyler, 2026-11-07). At the end the control row offers Finish (D18); nothing starts by itself.
- D51. **Not doing (video):** a local copy, speeds above 2x, a sleep timer, bookmarks, voices, a mini or picture-in-picture player, per-video speed, watch-time stats or "time left" (D23, D27), and AI summaries of the video (D29). Local video and audio files and podcasts need on-device transcription and come later.

## In-reader quiz (2026-10-24)
Tyler accepted D52 to D57 on 2026-10-24 (mockup: the "In-reader quiz" canvas). Evidence: `docs/research/brief-learning-science.md` §3 (retrieval g ≈ 0.5, feedback adds ~30%), §7 (confidence before reveal, hypercorrection), §9 (contiguity g = 0.74, static cues). The left sidebar also floats out on hover while collapsed (200 ms in, 300 ms out, like Notion).
- D52. **Quiz panel.** The finish step (D18) and EPUB chapter recalls open as a panel floating at the right of the reader (392 px, raised, rounded, shadow). The source stays in place but is blurred and dimmed past reading, with highlight colors, margin notes, the outline, the video and the source note hidden (D34.4); a chip says "Source hidden while you answer". Opened from Finish (⇧⌘↵ and a top-bar button since D106) and the chapter marker as before; no hover trigger. Esc closes it and keeps typed text as a draft. A segmented bar shows the steps (recall, then each question): progress through this quiz only, no score or XP (D23). Replaces the full-screen finish page.
- D53. **Order and checking.** Free recall comes first ("everything you remember"), so later passages cannot leak into it (inference from the same mechanism). Then each prequestion: answer, a confidence tap (Guess, Think so, Sure), and **Check**, or **No idea**. Check reveals the source for that question only; the next question blurs it again. "Where would you use this, and what does it connect to?" closes the quiz with the recall feedback. Changes the D18 order.
- D54. **Result per question.** A verdict line in plain encouraging words ("You got it", "Partly there", "Not yet. Here's where it is"; green, amber, neutral, never red), his answer, and the question's 2 to 4 key points, each got or missed, missed ones first when stepping. A miss he marked Sure adds "You were sure about this one. Worth a careful look." Unscored and nothing goes to FSRS (D18, D21); verdict and marks are AI output shown after the attempt (D34.3).
- D55. **Stepping through passages.** "In the source · 2 of 3" with up and down buttons scrolls the reader to each supporting passage like find (⌘G): the current one tinted and ringed, the others lightly tinted, static color only. Clicking a key point jumps to its passage. ↓ next, ↑ previous (rebindable, hover hints), ⌘↵ next question. The reader scrolls freely while checking; no new highlights from the quiz.
- D56. **Key points and passages.** When the quiz opens, one AI call writes each prequestion's 2 to 4 key points with the exact sentences that support each, while he writes the free recall; stored on the prequestion (`points`), reused next time. Quotes are anchored like highlights and one that does not anchor is dropped, so an invented citation never shows. After Check, the AI marks which points his answer covered (`check`); the marks arrive a few seconds after the passages. A tap flips a mark, and his flip is what is stored. Prompts `key-points` and `check`.
- D57. **Not doing:** a quiz on hover or any time mid-read (testing pays off after a delay; chapter recalls cover books), a session score or percent correct, showing the source behind a card in review before the answer (when Review is built it reuses this panel, source after Check). The float-out animation (150 to 200 ms, off under Reduce Motion) comes later. (Amended 2026-10-24: a celebration when the daily goal closes is in, D62. Amended 2026-11-20: "sounds or confetti" is struck from this list, since D153, D277 and D336 overturned it.)

## Home, review and motivation (2026-10-24)
Tyler accepted D58 to D62 on 2026-10-24 (plan: the "Review: place, process and in-app AI" doc). Feel: Raycast speed and keyboard-first, made delightful and a bit gamified so review happens every day; social comes later. Evidence: `docs/research/brief-learning-science.md` §5 (late is cheap, cramming costly), §7 (confidence, calibration), §10 (habit cue, forgiving streaks, informational feedback) and its "not supported" list. Amends D21, D23, D26, D28, D29 and D57 as stated in each line.
- D58. **Home.** The app opens on Home, which replaces All sources as the default page (All sources stays in the sidebar). Top: **Today**, a ring for the daily goal (D60), what is due, **Start review** (↵) and a row of skill chips. Then **Skills**, grouped by collection, each with its icon, its strength (D62) and its cards due today; ↵ opens the skill page (D46). Then **Continue reading**: the 3 to 5 sources read most recently, with their position, ↵ resumes. Skill chips are all on by default; a click toggles one, a click on a collection name toggles its skills, ⌥-click solos one (as in Final Cut and Logic) and ⌥-click again turns all back on. The choice lasts the day. Leaving a skill out only makes its cards wait; FSRS order is unchanged (D22). Amends D28 and D29 (Review is no longer "later"). 2026-10-28: Continue reading moves above Skills, in its own card, so it never reads as another collection. (Amended: skill tiles show only strength, D70; the skill chips moved into Start review's picker, D80.)
- D59. **Review mode.** A full-window focus mode: one card at a time in the reading column, a segmented bar for this session only (as D52), a footer of key hints. Entry points: Start review on Home, ⌘R anywhere, the skill page header (that skill pre-selected) and Raycast's Review Due, which opens the app into review. Esc leaves at any point; every rating is already logged. (Amended 2026-10-25: no entry point from the finish step, and a session ends when the goal closes, D65.) (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the session bar is gone and the session no longer ends at the goal: the mole says it is met and Review flows on until Esc or Done, D246.)
  1. **Explain** (`prompt`): type the answer (⌘⌫ "No idea"; ⌥↵ "In my head" on cards he has passed twice, D68), tap confidence 1 Guess, 2 Think so, 3 Sure, then ⌘↵ Check. The model answer and its 2 to 4 rubric points show at once; the AI's marks (got / missed) arrive a few seconds later and a click flips one; the AI proposes a rating, ↵ accepts and 1 to 4 overrides. His rating is what FSRS records (D21).
  2. **Fact** (`card`): recall in his head; 1, 2 or 3 records confidence and reveals the answer in one key; then 1 to 4, or ↵ for Good. No typing and no AI grading for facts. Amends D21 (typed answer on every card).
  3. A miss comes back once about three cards later (D21); a miss marked Sure adds "You were sure about this one. Worth a careful look." A new draft shows **New**; after the answer, E edits it in place and ⇧⌘⌫ retires it, through the CLI (D20). (⌘⌫ is No idea.)
  4. A card drafted from a highlight shows its passage at the right only after the answer (D34.4, D55, D57).
- D60. **Daily goal.** A goal in minutes of active review a day, 10 by default, set on Home (in Settings › Review since 2026-10-25), with a "Weekdays only" switch under which Saturday and Sunday neither count nor break anything. Time, not a card count, so a slow careful answer gets full credit. Active time is each review's `elapsed_ms`, at most 3 minutes per card. When no due or new card is left, today counts as met even short of the goal ("All caught up, today counts"), so the goal never pushes reviews of cards not yet due. Nothing resets to zero after a missed day: Home shows this week's met days and the total. Up to 10 new drafts enter review a day (`new_per_day` in `skills.yaml`), freshest source first; reviews are never capped. Amends D23 (no quota, no daily cap). (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: his own exercises come in first, not the freshest source, D176; new exercises take a fixed share, D144.)
- D61. **Ask about this.** After he answers a card, / opens a chat that sees the card, its rubric, his answer and its passage; it can explain, give an example or propose a better card, applied with one click through the CLI. Never before the answer (D34.3) and never sets a rating (D21). It runs on his plans through the vendors' own programs: one `claude -p` process per chat in stream-json mode, or `codex app-server` over stdio (D7). Never the Agent SDK, and the app never reads a login token: Anthropic allows the unmodified Claude Code binary signed in with his own plan and forbids subscription tokens in other tools (code.claude.com legal and compliance, checked 2026-10-24). (Built 2026-10-24 as `ore ask`, one vendor call per message rather than one long process: `claude -p --session-id`, then `--resume`; `codex exec`, then `exec resume <thread>`. Replies arrive whole, in about 5 to 10 seconds. If that feels slow, the next step is one streaming process per chat, which needs a Tauri pipe for the events. Chats are saved as Claude Code or Codex sessions in the library folder.)
- D62. **Motivation.** Reward what he remembers and the effort he spends, never volume. Now: motion (the card springs in, rubric marks tick in one at a time, keys respond, the session bar fills; all off under Reduce Motion); a short moment when the goal ring closes; an end-of-session line such as "18 memories kept, 4 rescued just before you'd have forgotten them" (rescued: recall chance under 85% at review, from FSRS); and **skill strength**, the count of a skill's solid memories (live cards with FSRS stability of 21 days or more), with a growth line on the skill page and levels at thresholds. Next: calibration ("When you say Sure, you're right 86% of the time", built 2026-10-25, D69) and one daily macOS notification while the goal is unmet (built 2026-10-25, D67). Later: social, private by default. Never: XP or badges for cards or minutes, hard-reset streaks, random rewards, leaderboards, guilt copy. Sound stays off unless Tyler asks for it. Amends D23, D26 and D57 (celebration and motion are now in; a session score or percent correct stays out). (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the card comes in on ease-out, no spring: the spring is kept for moments, D185.)

## Naming
- Repo `ore`, CLI `ore`, `/ore` skill, `@ore/core`, `ORE_*` env vars, library internals `.ore/`, library folder `~/Ore`, app "Ore". Renamed from Omn-Learn on 2026-10-12. Short name changed from `omn` to `ore` on 2026-10-21.

Tyler accepted D63 on 2026-10-24 (research: [`research/skill-strength.md`](research/skill-strength.md)). Evidence: gamification helps learning but barely moves felt competence unless feedback tracks real skill (Sailer 2019, Li 2024); measuring an activity can make it feel like work (Etkin 2016); goal gradient and the midpoint slump (Kivetz 2006, Bonezzi 2011); hard tasks in a run or at the end raise quitting (Kang 2022; about task order, not streak resets); writing how material connects to your life raises interest and grades (Hulleman 2010, 2017); self-chosen goals get sustained effort (Sheldon & Elliot 1999). Amends D62.
- D63. **Strength, growth and meaning.** Strength stays the count of solid memories (D62). A skill's level comes from the most solid memories it ever had, so it never drops; a memory that was solid and is now below 21 days shows as "to win back". The level line reads "N since level k" until halfway and "N to level k+1" after. The skill page gets a Strength card: the count, the level line, one bar split into Solid, Growing (live, below 21 days) and New (drafts), the 12-week growth line (hidden until two weeks differ), and "You can now explain", his solid Explain cards, latest first. Under the skill's name, the skill's `hook` is his one line on why he is learning it (at most 140 characters), edited in place; Enter saves, Esc or clicking away keeps what was there. Strength never shows during a card, and skills are never ranked by it. Rejected as the headline: sources finished, reviews or minutes (volume), current recall chance (falls every day away, like Duolingo's cracked skills), retention (flat under FSRS). (2026-10-28: shown as gems, with the level line and "to win back" moved into hover hints, D90.) (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: a click away saves the field; Esc cancels, D211.)

Tyler picked the floating pill on 2026-10-24 (mocks: the pill vs a tray under the source note). Amends D27 and D47 as stated.
- D64. **Collected in this source.** A pill at the bottom center of the reader shows one chip per highlight type he has used in this source (Keep, Card, Question; Research until D91), each with its icon, colour and count. A new highlight, or one changed to another type, flies a dot of its colour from the passage into its chip, which pops and shows "+1" (off under Reduce Motion). Click a chip, press C or pick "Collected highlights" in ⌘K to open the list: a search field on top (Raycast-style: a chip opens it with that type as a token, Backspace on the empty field drops it, every typed word must appear in the passage or note, Esc clears then closes), type filters below it, rows in reading order with the passage and his note, ↑ ↓ ↵ to jump, which opens the highlight's popup there. Counts are this source's inventory only: no totals across sources, no levels, nothing on Home (D62 "never volume"). Hidden until the first highlight, during the quiz (D52) and while the video's "Back to current" pill shows. Amends D27 (the reader's one piece of collection feedback) and D47 (open questions are counted here, as part of the source's inventory, never as a badge elsewhere).

Tyler said "go" on 2026-10-25 to the science audit's first four fixes (review: [`reviews/2026-10-24-science-audit.md`](reviews/2026-10-24-science-audit.md)). Evidence: workload rises steeply above 0.90 retention and a small, predictable daily load keeps review going (`research/brief-learning-science.md` §5, §10; hard tasks in a run or at the end raise quitting, Kang 2022); a delayed first retrieval beats an immediate one (Karpicke & Roediger 2007); highlight counts as progress are unsupported and heavy highlighting hurts (Ponce 2022). A ts-fsrs simulation of the old defaults gave about 96 minutes of review a day by day 90 against a 10-minute goal. Amends D22, D58, D59, D60 and D64 as stated.
- D65. **A daily load that fits the goal.**
  1. Desired retention by priority is P1 0.92, P2 0.88, P3 0.80 (D22).
  2. New drafts enter review only while today's goal has room after the cards already due, using his typical time per Fact and Explain card (20 s and 90 s until 50 reviews of a kind). `new_per_day` stays as the upper limit. Due cards are never capped (D60). (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: new exercises take a fixed share of every session, D144.)
  3. A draft first comes up the day after it was written, so its first review is spaced from the reading and the finish quiz. Drafts of a source finished today no longer jump the queue, and the planned "Review its N new cards" step after the finish is dropped (D59).
  4. Home shows today's slice: "About N min of your most urgent cards", with the full due count and time in a hover hint; Start review and Keep going carry no count. *Amended (Tyler, 2026-11-09):* the line is gone, since "N of 10 min" beside the ring already says what is left; the due count and total time moved to Start review's hover hint. A review session ends with the end screen when today's goal closes; Keep going goes on (D58, D59). (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the session no longer ends at the goal, D246.)
  5. The collected pill (D64) flies and pops only for a new Card or Question, a highlight changed to one, or a note written on any highlight. A plain Keep is still listed and counted, quietly.

Tyler accepted card remixing on 2026-10-25 (design: the "Remixing Review Cards" doc, https://claude.ai/artifact/RWgbpbKHV3aZyxMDBvLqpR). Evidence: answering about new examples beat repeating the same one on new examples later (Butler 2017); same-structure items with new surface details stop answering from memory (Shappell 2022); practice tests transfer best to application questions (Pan & Rickard 2018, d = 0.40) and application quizzes help definition questions too (McDaniel 2013); variety pays only once the base idea is in place (Foss 2023). Amends D24 and D59 as stated.
- D66. **Remixed cards.** Once he knows a card it comes back as a variant: the same idea asked on a case he hasn't seen.
  1. **Which cards.** Explain cards about a concept: new case, predict, diagnose (find the one mistake) or choose and justify, always written, never ticked. Procedures: new case only. Definitions: "name it from a case" (he names the term and says why), typed and graded like an Explain card. Facts and cloze cards stay as written, and remixing never creates a card.
  2. **The boundary.** Each variant key point maps to one of the original's key points (its answer, for a card without any); it is answerable from the original answer and passage alone; it keeps the source's claim and caveats. At least one point is tied to the new case, so the memorized answer earns partial credit at most.
  3. **Checks.** The writer (prompt `variant`) gets the card, its passage and note, his skills' hooks for settings, and earlier variants with why any were rejected. Code then checks 2 to 4 points with real mappings, an allowed type that differs from the last, a new setting, and no run of 5 words shared with the original question or answer (one retry, then dropped). A separate AI call (prompt `variant-check`) says yes or no to scope, answerability, same claim, and giving the answer away; a no is kept with its reason.
  4. **When.** Remixing starts after his last two reviews of the original since the last miss were Good or Easy, then rotates variant, variant, original. A miss goes back to the original until two passes again. Each variant is shown at most twice, never twice in a row. Two are written ahead in the background after a review, at session start, or when the switch turns on; never during review, and with none ready he sees the original.
  5. **One schedule.** FSRS sees one card; every version's review updates it and records `variant`. Strength counts cards, not versions (D62).
  6. **In review.** No label. After the answer: the variant's answer and key points, then "Same idea as: <original question>", which expands to the original answer. B (Bad version) drops the variant without logging and shows the original, with Undo; E edits the variant; ⇧⌘⌫ retires the card; Ask about this sees both versions.
  7. **Switch.** "Remix cards" in Settings, Review (on by default; `ore goal --remix`). Off shows only originals and keeps the variants written.
  8. **Drift is ours to watch.** `ore stats --json` reports `remix`, the pass rates on variants and on originals over remixed cards. When variants run well below the originals we fix the prompts; the app never shows it.
  9. **Not yet:** math remixes (same-structure problems with the answer computed in code) wait for math cards, which need math display and exact answers.

Tyler said "go" on 2026-10-25 to the audit's daily cue (review: [`reviews/2026-10-24-science-audit.md`](reviews/2026-10-24-science-audit.md)). Evidence: implementation intentions, d ≈ 0.65 on goal attainment (Gollwitzer & Sheeran 2006); habit forms around the cue that starts the behaviour (Gardner 2022); tuning a reminder's wording gains little (Yancey & Settles 2020), so one plain reminder is enough. Builds D62's "Next" notification.
- D67. **A daily plan and one reminder.**
  1. **Plan.** In Settings › Review he writes his cue: "After [my morning coffee], I review." Enter saves, Esc or clicking away keeps what was there. Home shows it under today's slice; a click opens Settings › Review. With none, the line reads "Plan when you review". (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: a click away saves the field; Esc cancels, D211.)
  2. **Reminder.** A time menu beside it (Off, or every half hour from 6:00 to 22:00). At that time one macOS notification, titled with his plan ("After my morning coffee, I review.", or "Time for today's review" without one) and Home's slice line as the body. It fires only when today counts, the goal is unmet and cards are waiting. One a day, no follow-ups, no streak talk (D62, D23).
  3. **Mechanism.** A LaunchAgent (`com.tylerpoelking.ore.reminder`) runs the app's own binary with `--remind`, so it fires with the app closed and on wake if the Mac slept through the time. That run asks `ore remind` whether to notify, posts the notification as Ore (a click opens the app) and exits without a window. Setting the time installs or removes the agent; `review.plan` and `review.reminder` live in `skills.yaml`.

Tyler said "go" on 2026-10-25 to the audit's fifth fix. Evidence: retrieving silently helped retention as much as writing the answer (Smith, Roediger & Karpicke 2013, word lists; one study found a small edge for typed answers at a week, Jönsson et al. 2014); applying it to explanations is an inference. The known risk is a fluency illusion when he marks himself (Bjork 2013), so missed cards go back to typing and calibration watches it. Amends D59.1.
- D68. **Answer older Explain cards in your head.** Once an Explain card's last two reviews of the original since its last miss were Good or Easy, review offers **In my head** (⌥↵) beside No idea. He still picks how sure he is first, then sees the model answer and its key points unmarked ("Key points · which did you get?"). He marks the ones he got; the suggested rating follows his marks (all Good, some Hard, none Again) and nothing is suggested until he marks one. No AI grading. The review records `in_head: true`. New and missed cards and remixed versions (D66) stay typed. About 30 s instead of 90 s per card.

Tyler said "go" on 2026-10-25 to the audit's eighth fix. Evidence: delayed, cue-only confidence judgments are the accurate kind (Rhodes & Tauber 2011); showing calibration counters the fluency illusion that makes people drop methods that work (`research/brief-learning-science.md` §7). Builds D62's "Next".
- D69. **Calibration.** "When you say Sure, you're right N% of the time" (now three bars, D165). Measured on Explain answers he typed and marked Sure in the last 12 weeks, counted right when the AI's key-point check rated them Good or Easy. That check is independent of his own rating. Fact cards don't count (their confidence key also reveals the answer, so it is pressed out of habit), and nor do in-head answers (D68). Needs 20 answers. It shows on the end screen of the week's first session with reviews, at most weekly and never during a card; the hover hint gives the count. `ore stats --json` has it too.
- D70. **One number per meaning (2026-10-25, Tyler, critique round 2).** Home shows each count once: chips carry cards due, the sidebar hides its per-skill source counts on Home, and skill tiles show only strength. Before the first level a skill says "N of 5 solid memories to level 1" instead of "Lv 0", and the skill page says strength once (the folded header repeats it only when folded). A session's "rescued" counts a card only when its recall chance fell under 85% and at least 5 points under its own target retention (D22), so on-time P3 reviews no longer count.
- D71. **Closing card (2026-10-25, Tyler picked B over a toast).** Finishing a source always ends on the quiz panel's closing card, "Just mark done" included: "Source finished", "New cards tomorrow" (no count, since cards are drafted after it closes), what he made (recall saved, highlights, questions waiting on the skill page), the optional "where would you use this" field, and Done. Leaving it any way (button, ⌘↵, Esc, ×) drafts the cards. No toast on top, and never an immediate review (D65).
- D72. **Skill page header (2026-10-25, Tyler, critique round 3).** The four stat chips (done, cards, reviews, retention) and the "N sources finished. Nice going." line are gone: they count volume (D62), and Strength already says how a skill is going. Strength moves into the header's right side as one button: the solid count, its one line ("N of 5 solid memories to level 1", or the level and what's next) and the level meter (the same as on Home tiles). A click opens the solid/growing/new split, the growth line and "You can now explain". A skill with no cards yet shows no Strength. Below, the note on the left and Sources then Questions on the right (layout A of three mockups; amends D46's layout note). Home tiles say nothing under the meter on day one (0 solid). Retention stays in `ore stats`.
- D73. **Questions aren't sources; review returns you (2026-10-25, Tyler).** A snippet that only carries a question (typed on a skill page, or captured with Question intent) lives under Questions and is left out of `ore ls`, so it never shows in To read, Done or the counts. Leaving review puts him back in the source he was reading, at the same place.
- D74. **Source lists at scale (2026-10-25, Tyler, option A of three mockups on a 240-source demo library).** A source list's title is its view menu (To read, Done, and Archived once there is any; ⌘1/⌘2), Linear-style (amended 2026-11-11, Tyler: "We don't need Command-1 and Command-2 to work on simple drop-down menus like this": the menu shows no shortcuts; ⌘1/⌘2 still go to the lists from anywhere and stay in the menu bar's Go menu. A menu's tick is its own smaller drawing, `Tick`, ten dots wide, about as tall as the label's capitals, centred on the row); the To read / Done / Archived tabs and their counts are gone, and so is the folded Sources summary. A skill page lists its first 8 sources, then "Show all N", so Questions stays in sight. All sources has a filter (⌘F): titles that contain it first, then sources whose text, highlights or notes match (`ore search`) with the matching line. Rows are one line with no date: the title, then the kind (article, video, pdf, book, text, snippet) and the other skills, muted. Any source can be pinned (`pinned_at`, `ore pin`): pinned ones list first, in the order pinned (amended 2026-11-07, Tyler: or his own order, by dragging a pinned row or Move up/down in its menu; `ore pin --at` sets the time they list by), on a faint tint (amended 2026-10-28, Tyler: no pin mark; pin and unpin from the right-click or ⋯ menu and ⌘K; amended 2026-10-29, Tyler: the tint, like hover and selection, is neutral grey, not the accent). The mock harness seeds this library with `scripts/mock/seed-big.ts`. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the view menu became section tabs, D226.)

Tyler picked the hybrid on 2026-10-25 (design and evidence: [`design/questions-to-research.md`](design/questions-to-research.md); the side panel was his idea). Precedent: NotionRPG's Question highlight ended Answered or as an Inquiry note, linked many sources by mention, not relation, and kept questions a "menu, not a backlog". Evidence: prequestions help learn what they target (g = 0.54, St. Hilaire 2024); self-generated questions beat rereading (Weinstein 2010; Bugg & McDaniel 2012); curiosity improves memory for the answer (Gruber 2014); writing the answer in his own words is self-explanation. Linking sources doesn't help learning by itself, so links are only for getting back. Amends D19 and D47.
- D75. **Answering questions where the answer turns up.**
  1. **One answer model.** His answer to a question is a list of pieces in his words, each naming the source he wrote it from and the passage he had selected (`answers` on the question highlight). Writing from a source is what links it to the question: no attach step.
  2. **Reader: the Questions tab.** The right panel has two tabs, Source note (⇧⌘N) and Questions (Q); the same key on the open tab closes the panel. Questions lists the open questions of the source's skills: "May be answered here" first (with "Jump to the passage" and "Not answered here"), then the rest under the skills' names, those asked in this source marked "Asked here", 6 before "Show all". A highlight just made or open when he presses Q comes in as the quote. Clicking a question (or ↑ ↓ ↵) opens the answer box: ↵ adds what he wrote and keeps it open, **Answered** closes it, **Answered + card** (⌘↵) closes it and drafts a card. Every save shows a toast with Undo. + asks a new question filed under the source's skills. No right-edge hover (D52).
  3. **Skill page.** A question row has **Answer** (the same box, inline) and **Begin research** (was Find sources, D17); a source added from it is linked to the question. Under the question: his answer so far with each piece's source, and dashed chips for sources that may answer it ("May answer it", or "To read" for one added from research). Mark answered and Reopen stay in ⋯ (and Reopen on answered rows).
  4. **Matching.** When a source is first enriched, the AI checks it against up to 30 open questions of its skills and returns at most 3 it answers, each with one exact sentence; a sentence that isn't in the text drops the match (as D56). Only first enrichment: older sources aren't rechecked when a new question is asked.
  5. **Cards.** A question still never becomes a card by itself (D19), only on **Answered + card**: the next enrich drafts one card from his answer, kept in his words, corrected only where clearly wrong (prompt `enrich` v5). AI never answers for him.
  6. **Menu, not backlog.** No counts of open questions anywhere (D23), no due dates. Future: a small completion animation on Answered (Tyler, after the core workflow).
  CLI: `ore answer <q> --text … [--item --quote] [--done | --card] [--remove <ts>]`, `ore match <source> <q> [--drop]`, `ore questions --item <source>`, `ore add … --for <q>`.

Tyler's idea on 2026-10-25 (built the same night, design checked against D21, D22, D58 to D68 and the purpose rule): fill the minutes spent waiting at the computer with retrieval. Short spaced sessions are what spacing asks for; the risk is a rushed, half-attentive answer, so the quiz keeps every review rule rather than a lighter one.
- D76. **Quick quiz.** A system-wide shortcut (⌃⌥Q by default, set or turned off in Settings › Review, Raycast-style recorder) and an optional menu bar item (off by default) show a small window a third of the way down the screen the pointer is on, over any app, like Spotlight.
  1. **Same review.** It is Review (D59) in a smaller frame: the same `ore due` queue and today's skill pick from Home (D58, D65), Explain cards typed with confidence first and ⌥↵ In my head only where D68 allows it, Fact cards flipped, his rating recorded (D21), a miss back three cards later, edit, retire with Undo, Ask (/), ⌘Z.
  2. **Counts like Review.** Each showing is its own session; its active time counts toward the daily goal (D60) and the card's clock runs only while the window shows. A small goal ring replaces the session bar, since the quiz has no fixed length; its hover says the minutes. When the goal closes the end screen shows (D62); otherwise leaving shows nothing.
  3. **Leaving.** Esc, Done or the shortcut again put it away and return him to the app he was in. A click elsewhere puts it away too and keeps a typed answer for that card. "Open in source" opens the main window there. With nothing due it says "All caught up" and waits for Esc.
  4. Closing the main window now hides it (as in Mail), so the shortcut keeps working; the Dock icon brings it back and ⌘Q quits.

Tyler asked on 2026-10-26 for a Readwise-style author and thumbnail on every source, with author and date added as optional list columns, "starting minimally".
- D77. **Author and thumbnail for every source.**
  1. **Thumbnail.** `thumbnail.<ext>` beside doc.md, made at add from what the source carries: a page's og:image (then twitter:image, image_src, apple-touch-icon; favicons are too small), a video's YouTube poster, an EPUB's cover, a PDF's first page (Quick Look, macOS). Shrunk to 480 px with `sips`. Pasted text and snippets get none. A missing picture never fails an add. `ore backfill` fills older sources.
  2. **Author.** As before from the byline, channel, EPUB creator or PDF Author, now also the PDF's XMP creators (backfilled for PDFs).
  3. **Where.** In every list of sources (All sources, skill page, Home's Continue reading, ⌘K), left of the title, the thumbnail or the kind's icon so titles line up. In the reader, the thumbnail above the title (not for videos, whose player is the picture) and the author first in the row of skill chips and source link.
  4. **Columns.** The list's view menu has Author and Date added (Linear's display properties), off by default, remembered per Mac, at fixed widths on the right so they line up; hidden where the list is too narrow (the skill page panel). No sorting or filter by them yet: ⌘F already finds by title or text.

Tyler said go on the iPhone plan on 2026-10-26 and, after the 2026-10-27 stress test ([`reviews/2026-10-27-stress-test.md`](reviews/2026-10-27-stress-test.md)), to recording it here and making the files safe for two devices.
- D78. **iPhone app, and a library two devices can write.**
  1. **Built.** The iPhone app is the desktop React UI with a phone layout (`apps/desktop/src/phone`), in a Tauri 2 iOS shell (`apps/phone`). Portrait only, dark only, sized for a regular iPhone. It does everything the Mac does except AI.
  2. **Core in the app.** iOS has no CLI to run, so the `ore` commands run in-process (`src/phone/core.ts`) over the same core; D30 stays for the Mac.
  3. **AI on the Mac.** The phone never calls a model and says "AI runs on your Mac for now." The Mac drafts and grades for phone captures once sync exists (D7 unchanged, no user API keys).
  4. **Two-device files.** Each device appends to its own log: `reviews/<yyyy>-<mm>.<device>.jsonl`, `sessions/…`, `annotations.<device>.jsonl`. Readers take the union, including older files without a device part and a synced folder's conflicted copies, and count each record once. An undo appends a record instead of rewriting the log. A draft's finishing review lives only in the log (the card file keeps `draft`), so a card file changes only when its text does.
  5. **Phone data kept.** The phone's library survives reinstalls. Phone data from a new build of the Mac's library is merged in, never reset (the reset before this lost phone reviews).
  6. **Next.** iCloud Drive sync once the $99 Apple account exists (`.index/` and `.ore/` stay per device); then the Mac does the phone's AI.

## Courses (2026-10-28, Tyler picked option B of docs/design/courses-and-live-links.md)
- D79. **A course is its lessons.** Each lesson of a course is its own source (a video source for a lesson with a YouTube video, an article otherwise), so every lesson gets its own prequestions, finish and next-day cards. Lessons carry `course: {id, title, part, url}`; the course has no file of its own.
  1. **Adding.** A Claude Academy course or lesson link, or a YouTube playlist, pasted or typed in ⌘N adds the whole course under the open skill (`ore add <url> --course`); the add box first says "Course · 14 lessons · <title>". Lessons enrich one after another in one background run. A lesson that fails says why; adding the link again adds only the missing lessons.
  2. **Lists.** A course is one row (caret, title, the lesson a click opens, "course"), in To read while any lesson is left and in Done once all are; counted once in the sidebar. One click opens the next lesson; the caret (or → ←) lists the lessons under it, numbered, done ones checked. Menu: Open, Show lessons, Rename…, Open course page, Skills, Archive course; dragging it onto a skill files every lesson. A filter and the Archived tab list lessons one by one.
  3. **Reader.** Above a lesson's title: the course name (a menu of its lessons), "Lesson 3 of 14", and ‹ › (⌥⌘← ⌥⌘→). The closing card (D71) adds **Next lesson** beside Done.
  4. **Home.** Continue reading shows a course's next lesson, with the course name.
  5. **Not doing (yet).** Reordering lessons or moving a source into or out of a course; adding a single lesson on its own from a course link; the course's exercises and takeaways for video lessons (the transcript is the source); grouping on the iPhone's lists; live links (watching a page for changes, docs/design/courses-and-live-links.md).

## Choosing what to review (2026-10-28, Tyler)
- D80. **Start review asks what to review.** The skill chips leave Home. Start review, ↵ on Home, ⌘R and the palette open a short list of the skills with cards due, most due first, each with "N cards ready" and a checkbox; ↵ (or ⌘R again) starts at once, so the usual session costs one extra key. Preselected: his pick from earlier today, else every skill with cards due (the queue already puts the most urgent first, D22). With one skill due it starts without asking. The pick lasts the day, as before; leaving a skill out only makes its cards wait. Amends D58. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: rows show the gems a session could win, not a due count, D249.)

## Find (2026-10-28, Tyler)
- D81. **Find in a source.** ⌘F in the reader opens a find bar at the top right of the source (the Claude app's): count `n/N`, ↵ ↓ ⌘G next, ⇧↵ ↑ ⇧⌘G previous, Esc closes and keeps the query for the next ⌘F. It searches the source and, when open, the side panel (source note or Questions) as one list, source first, so the numbers run left to right as he reads the screen (the Claude app's order). Case, curly quotes and spacing are ignored; a match never runs across two paragraphs. A video's transcript is searched like any text, and a step stops following the speaker. Matches paint orange (D55's "in the source" look), never an intent colour, and are not highlights. Outside a source ⌘F still filters the lists (D74).

## Mac patterns (2026-10-28, Tyler)
- D82. **Undo history.** Every step that offers Undo goes on the window's history: ⌘Z takes back a run of steps, newest first, ⇧⌘Z redoes them, each with a toast naming the step ("Undone: Archived …" · Redo). Steps are inverse calls through `ore`, never library snapshots; the history lasts until relaunch (50 steps). Text fields and the note editor keep their own ⌘Z; Review keeps ⌘Z for its last rating. Amended 2026-11-03 (Tyler: "couldn't undo highlights"): every change he makes is a step, toast or not; small, frequent ones (a new highlight, its type, its note, Card here) go on the history quietly, and ⌘Z names them as it takes them back. A fresh highlight dropped with Esc leaves no step. Then app-wide (Tyler, 2026-11-03, "every change"): renaming and pinning a source, a skill's look and goal, a new skill, feed rename, skills and order, card edits, and deleting a skill (Undo restores it in place, refiled and unarchived, `ore skills restore`). Review ratings join the same history (Review no longer keeps its own ⌘Z); undoing the newest rating while Review is open shows that card again. Amended 2026-11-07 (Tyler): on the end screen too, ⌘Z takes the last rating back and returns to that card in the same session (`ore session reopen`; its next end receipt replaces the first), and ⇧⌘Z gives a rating again (the same rating and answer; while that card is showing, Review moves on). Edit ▸ Undo and Redo in the menu bar are the app's own items (not macOS's), so a click walks the same history; in a text field they undo its typing (`execCommand`), in the note or diagram editor theirs.

## Look Up and Listen (2026-10-28, Tyler ticked them on the missing-patterns page)
- D83. **Look up a word, then keep it.** Apple Books' Look Up, with a way to keep the word.
  1. **Where.** A word or phrase of up to three words gets a Look up button (book icon, ⌃⌘D) in the highlight popup. ⌃⌘D with nothing selected looks up the word under the pointer, as macOS does. Right-click (or ⌃-click) no longer makes a Keep highlight, so the Mac's own menu and its Look Up work on the text; force-click is the webview's own Look Up.
  2. **No stray highlight.** Selecting makes a Keep (D15); Look up drops that Keep when he had not touched it, as Start here does (D49). A highlight he already had stays.
  3. **Entry.** The macOS dictionary (Dictionary Services, offline, instant) in the popup's place: the word, its pronunciation, the entry. One C call from the shell, so no Swift helper (D11 is for Swift-only APIs). Not in the dictionary says so and still offers the card.
  4. **Make card** (2): the word becomes a Card highlight, so its sentence goes to the draft as context and drafting and review run as usual (D20, D21); the popup opens on it with the note field ready. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: 2 marks a Key point, D118; Look up is named Explain, D97.)
  5. **Not yet.** An AI explanation in the source's context; Look Up on the iPhone (iOS's own selection menu has it).
- D85. **Listen to a source.** Readwise Reader's and Books' read-aloud, for learning away from the screen.
  1. **Voice.** The Mac's own speech through the webview (`speechSynthesis`, which is AVSpeechSynthesizer: free, offline, his Spoken Content voice). No Swift helper is needed (D11). Better voices can come later.
  2. **Start and stop.** Listen (L) in ⋯, ⌘K and the menu bar starts from the first sentence in view, so scrolling while stopped picks the place. Stopping leaves the page where the voice was, and the reading position saves as usual. Leaving the source, the finish step or a chapter recall stops it. Not on video sources (they have sound) or the iPhone yet.
  3. **Following.** Spoken a sentence at a time; the sentence is underlined in the accent and kept in view a third of the way down. His own scroll stops the page following, and "Back to current" brings it back, as with a video (D49).
  4. **Controls.** While listening, a small bar sits with the reader tools: pause/play (Space), the video's speed popover ([ ]), stop (L). The speed is remembered apart from a video's. Highlighting with the mouse works while it plays.
  5. **Fails plainly.** No speech on this Mac, or a voice error, stops it with a toast that says what to do.

## Lists (2026-10-28, Tyler)
- D84. **Pick many sources.** In a sources list, ⇧-click and ⇧↑↓ pick a run from the anchor, ⌘-click adds or drops one, ⌘A picks the list, Esc clears (Linear, Finder). A plain click still opens. ⋯, right-click and ⌘K on a picked row act on all of them, named for the count ("Archive 3 sources"): Move to Done or To read, Skills, Archive, or Restore in Archived. Dragging a picked row onto a sidebar skill files the whole pick. Each is one step with one Undo and Redo. Course rows aren't picked; open lessons are. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: ⌘K no longer acts on picked rows, D178.)
- D86. **Highlight from the keyboard** (Readwise Reader). In the reader, ↓ or ↑ shows a reading focus on the block a third of the way down the view (nothing shows until the keyboard moves); further presses step it a block at a time and scroll so it stays a third of the way down, with no animation. ⇧↑ ⇧↓ widen it block by block. H highlights its text as Keep and opens the popup as a mouse selection does (D15); 1 to 3 highlight it as that type at once (D91); Esc in the popup closes it and keeps the highlight. Esc clears the focus, a click hands over to the mouse. Arrows stay with menus, lists, sliders and text fields; the quiz and finish step own the page while open. Not on the phone. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: keys 1 to 4, with Try it, D127.)
- D87. **Copy with the source.** ⌘C with no text selected copies what is selected with where it came from: an open highlight (popup, or the arrowed row in the collected list) as `> quote` + `> — [Title](link)` with his note under it, a source row or the open source as its linked title, picked sources as a list. HTML goes alongside so it pastes rich into Notes or Mail. Selected text still copies plain. Also "Copy as Markdown" in ⋯ and right-click, a copy button in the highlight popup, and a toast saying what was copied.
- D88. **Hover to peek at a link.** Resting the pointer on a `[[link]]` in a note, or a "Linked from" row, for half a second shows a card under it (Notion, Arc Peek): the title, its skills for a source, and the first few lines (a skill link shows its note). It stays while the pointer moves onto it and closes a quarter second after it leaves; one click on the card opens the target, Esc or typing closes it. Lists don't peek: their rows already open on one click.
- D89. **Skills picked as a source is added** (Linear's labels in its new-issue box). The add box (⌘N, + Add) holds the source's skill pills: the open skill page's skill is ticked already, click it, or Tab from the text field (which opens it), to change, add or create one; Esc puts focus back in the text field, and ↵ adds under all of them (several pasted links each get them; a course gets them on every lesson). Paste outside a field, a file drop and ⌘O add at once as before (a drop onto a skill is the pick); one that lands with no skill shows its new row in All sources with the skill picker open (Esc leaves it). He stays where he is: the toast says what went in, with Show (its row, selected) and Undo (archives what that add made; ⌘Z too). A course's skills belong to the whole course: lessons added later join them, and skills picked when the link is added again file every lesson. A link already in the library isn't added twice: it comes back filed under the picked skills too, with "was already here". (Amended 2026-11-22, pointer only: a source added with no skill opens no skill picker, D345.)
- D90. **Gems show a skill's strength** (Duolingo-style metaphor in place of words; research in `docs/research/skill-metrics-motivation.md`, rated by a PM-style review in five rounds, `docs/research/gems-pm-review.md`). A solid memory (D63) is a cut gem, a growing card a rough stone, a growing card whose next Good review makes it solid glows, and a solid one he missed cracks until he wins it back. The level is the gem he's cutting: a ring in the skill colour fills toward the next level, with "Lv N" on it; a dashed ghost arc after the fill shows what today's glowing cards would add, and when they would finish the level the gem breathes and the ring glows. Beside it: the cut count, then the glowing gems due today (the near goal, one meaning on every screen) and the cracked count. Home tiles show the ring, the count and the glowing gems due today only. The end of a review drops in one cut gem per card that became solid, in its skill's colour with its icon ("+N" only past 6). The popover's legend uses the same gems; its growth line is in the skill colour. Words live on hover only. Gems are never spent, earned for anything but memory, or ranked (D23). Art: Recraft vector (style "flat chunky", free plan: check commercial rights before selling), stored as facet paths in `apps/desktop/src/gem-art.ts` and tinted per skill with `color-mix` on `--skill`. Later, not now: the glowing gem as a one-click review of just those cards; an optional About page in Settings with a few gem graphics; and once the art is final, gem-family icons for the section and highlight-type icons where one fits (Your note, To read, Questions), which may mean widening the mining analogy (note as the map, to read as ore, questions as what he's digging for). (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: a cut gem's dots fuse into square pixels, and no gem glows, cracks or breathes; a missed gem comes loose, D236.)
