Tyler picked A on 2026-10-28 (mockups and evidence: `/mnt/project-files/highlight-popup/design.md`). Research did nothing a Keep didn't, and research starts from a question (D75 "Begin research"). Keep and Card stay apart: a Keep with a note already gets a card, and carding every highlight would grow review debt (science audit), while rereading one's own highlights adds nothing (g = −0.06). Own-words notes help (self-explanation g = 0.87), but a placeholder telling him to write in his own words does not (Mueller 2014, Study 2); the strongest own-words steps are the finish recall and Explain answers.
- D91. **Three highlight types, a calmer popup.** Keep, Card and Question (keys 1–3); Research is gone, and older `research` highlights read as questions (the log is not rewritten), so they show under Questions with Begin research. The popup: the three types with their names; the note field, "Note…" (a question keeps "My question… my guess:"); then one row of muted icon tools: Play from here on videos (was the "Start here" button), Look up, Copy with source, and Remove at the right end. The Raycast "Capture as Research" command is removed. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the types are now a plain highlight (1), Key point (2), Question (3) and Try it (4), D118, D127.)
## Pages with a video (2026-10-28, Tyler)
- D92. **A page keeps its text and its video.** A course lesson (Claude Academy) or any article that embeds one YouTube video becomes one source: "## Video transcript" with the timed transcript first (where the video sits on the page), then `<!-- end of video transcript -->` and the page's text. The reader shows the part before that marker as a video card at the top of the text (Notion's embed block, Tyler 2026-10-29): the player, and the transcript in its own scrolling box that follows the speaker, while the page below stays his to read (scrolling it never stops the following; scrolling the box does, with Back to current). The card's bar folds the whole card (pausing the video) or just the transcript, kept per source. Bodies saved with the transcript last (2026-10-28) still read with the pinned player; Reimport rewrites them (`video:` in the frontmatter, `transcript.vtt` beside `source.html`). A page whose text is under ~100 words is a wrapper and stays the bare video; a plain YouTube link stays transcript-only; a page that embeds several videos stays an article. A video found in an article that can't be read (no captions) leaves the article alone; a lesson's video must load, as before. Adding a course again gives lessons added as bare videos their page's text, reusing the transcript they have, keeping their highlights (re-anchored), cards and progress.

## Reimport (2026-10-28, Tyler)
- D93. **Reimport updates a source from its original.** In the source's ⋯ menu, right-click and ⌘K, for a source added from a link, or a file whose original is kept beside it (read again from its `source.*`, D119: a PDF, an EPUB, a recording heard again as D167 says, a Word file, slides, a screenshot, a pasted page read again from its HTML; not plain pasted text, a Markdown file or a snippet, and not once the kept original is gone; Tyler, 2026-11-14, F181). It fetches the link again; if the text is the same nothing changes ("already up to date"). Otherwise the new text replaces the old in the same source (id, title he gave it, skills, status, notes, cards and reviews kept). Highlights whose passage is still there re-anchor to it; ones whose passage is gone are removed, except questions (his own words, listed on the skill page); cards made from a removed highlight stay. The toast says how many were removed, with Undo (⌘Z) and redo: the previous version waits in `.ore/reimport/<id>/`. Not automatic (live links stay deferred, D79.5).
  - The ⋯ menu lost what has its own control or is a keyboard convention; each stays in ⌘K with its shortcut: Skills (the header's skill picker), Find in source (⌘F), Copy as Markdown (⌘C), Open source note (+ Note), Collected highlights (the pill), Highlight focused text (H). The phone keeps Skills and Copy as Markdown, as it has neither ⌘K nor the picker.
  - The side panel's keys match: **N** source note (⇧⌘N still works, also while typing in it), **Q** questions. Main point of section moves from N to **M**.

## Basics first (2026-10-28, Tyler, from the docs drift audit)
- D94. **A note prompt per highlight type** (amends D91). Each type's note field asks for the generative step that fits it, never "in my words" (D91's evidence): Keep "Connects to…" (elaboration: tie the passage to what he already knows), Card "This works because…" (self-explanation, as `research/brief-notetaking-check-fiorella-mayer.md` recommended for Card), Question "My question… my guess:" (a guess before the answer, like a prequestion). Placeholders only, never prefilled (D34.2).
- D95. **Catch up once a day; drop what isn't basic.**
  1. **Catch-up.** Opening Home, starting a review or the reminder agent (D67) starts `ore catch-up` in the background when it hasn't run in 20 hours: it enriches what is still pending (not sources added in the last hour, whose own enrich is running) and, once there are 1,000 reviews, refits FSRS when the last fit is a month old (D8, D10). It replaces the planned Claude Code scheduled task.
  2. **Dropped:** the hotkey capture panel and the watched `inbox/` folder (D14); the Library place and research topics: Begin research returns 3 to 5 sources and writes nothing (D17, D28). Maps (D35) are deferred. Any of these can come back as a new decision once the basics work cleanly.

- D96. **Every content type imports fully, or says why not** (audit and plan: `docs/reviews/2026-10-28-import-coverage.md`). Intake is one pipeline: route by link shape and content type, read with one reader per type, check the result, keep what the reader shows. Nothing is saved that isn't the content: bot checks, login walls, near-empty pages, feeds, audio, playlists and X posts are refused with the next step. Built in three phases, each merged as it passes: (1) the check and new readers in core: Word, PowerPoint, notebooks, subtitles, GitHub, Google Docs and Slides, arXiv's HTML, video sites yt-dlp reads, YouTube chapters and description, PDF titles and headings; (2) the reader: images saved into the source's folder, tables and math (KaTeX) rendered, embedded videos, EPUB illustrations; (3) on the Mac: add a page from the open Safari or Chrome tab (signed-in, paywalled and browser-built pages), audio and podcast transcription (Apple's on-device SpeechAnalyzer; macOS 26+, older macOS says to paste a transcript instead of a whisper.cpp fallback, revised 2026-10-28), text recognition for scans and screenshots (Vision). Every type keeps a saved example under `packages/core/test/fixtures/intake/`; a new type or a fix to one lands with its fixture.

## Side quests (2026-10-28, Tyler picked option B of docs/design/side-quests.md)
- D97. **A term he doesn't know: the gist now, a question with sources for later.** Evidence in `docs/design/side-quests.md`: an unknown key term blocks comprehension, a gloss in place helps, learning the term first pays when the source leans on it, leaving mid-thought costs understanding, curiosity fades fast, and a question carried to the next source makes it stick.
  1. **Explain** (was Look up, D83: the book icon in the highlight popup, ⌃⌘D) on a word or phrase of up to three words: **Here**, what it means in this paragraph, and **In general** (the Mac's dictionary when it has the word, else the AI's), from `ore explain` (prompt `explain`, writes nothing). Under them, when it's true, "Comes up again in Streaming and Tool use" (or "further on"): later sections of this source that use the term, found in the text, not by AI. It's his cue for learning it now. Make card stays.
  2. **Learn this** (↵): the term becomes a Question highlight worded "What is <term>?", editable in place (↵ saves, Esc restores), and research (D17) runs for it with its paragraph as context (prompt `research` v3, which also returns minutes). Up to 5 sources show as rows with a checkbox (the first ticked), the kind's icon and "site · type · N min"; hovering or arrowing onto one shows a peek beside the list with why it was picked (Raycast's detail pane). "into" shows the skills they'll be filed under (the source's; the skill picker changes them, D89).
  3. **Later** (⌘↵) adds the ticked sources to To read, linked to the question (`add --for`, D75), and he keeps reading; **Read now** (↵) does the same and opens the first. The toast has Undo (archives what was new) and Show. Esc closes and keeps the question.
  4. **The side quest.** The opened source shows **Back to <title>** at the bottom left (Books' back-to-page pill), which returns to the passage he left (⌘[ also does), and the Questions tab opens with his question first under "May be answered here", so what he writes there answers it (D75).
  5. **Failures.** No AI or an AI error says so where the text would be, with Retry; the question stays saved. A retry never adds anything.
  6. **Not doing.** A fourth highlight type (D91), Obsidian-style empty pages (no learning effect, and a backlog), counts of terms to learn, AI explanation saved as his answer (D75.5). Not on the iPhone yet. Amends D83.5.
- D98. **The link he just copied is offered in the add box** (Chrome's "Link you copied"; Tyler, 2026-10-28). When ⌘N opens with an empty field and he copied one link or file in another app (the clipboard changed while Ore was in the background, and he came back under 5 minutes ago), it is the box's first row and ↵ adds it with the box's skills; typing replaces it. Only what Ore reads (a link, or a file type it imports; never text, a list, an X post or a podcast show page) and never a link already in the library; each copy is offered once. The clipboard is never polled: its change count is read on focus and blur, and its contents only when the box opens, so macOS's paste prompt, if it asks, comes while he is adding something. Not a chip on return (B): ⌘V outside a text field already adds in one key, and a chip would show for links copied for anything else.
- D99. **Collected tab and Card here** (Tyler, 2026-10-29; design in `docs/design/key-points-and-stopping-early.md`). The side panel's Questions tab becomes **Collected**: his Cards, Questions (with answers and "May be answered here", D75) and Keeps from this source, as collapsible groups in reading order; a row opens its passage and popup (whose ▶ plays a video from there). C opens it (Q too, with the passage as his answer's quote); the bottom pill (D64) opens it instead of its own list. **Card here** (2 with nothing selected, + on Cards, ⌘K) adds a line he types, anchored at the sentence where he is (the word being spoken once a video has played, else the text a third of the way down), with a Card mark in the margin and no tint. It is a Card highlight marked `point`: at enrich the AI drafts one card from his line using the whole source and returns 1 to 4 exact quotes; the app keeps only quotes it finds in the text (a draft with none is retried once) and saves them on the card as `evidence` for review. No fourth highlight type (D97.6). Supersedes D64's popover list. In review, after the answer, a Card here card shows those quotes one at a time with ◂ ▸ (↑ ↓) and the spoken time; Open in source opens the source at that quote. On a New card the retire button reads **Delete** (Undo in the toast). Rewriting a card from typed feedback is Ask (D61): no second button for it.
- D100. **Finish covers what you reached** (Tyler, 2026-10-29; design in `docs/design/key-points-and-stopping-early.md`). The reader keeps how far he got: the furthest word spoken in a video he has played, or in text the last block in view (a body offset, per device). Finish before the last few lines says "Everything up to 15:05" (or the last heading) on the recall, and under it an optional **Leave anything out?** line in his words. Before the questions, `ore scope` leaves out prequestions whose key-point passages all lie past that point, and those the AI (`leave-out` prompt) finds his note rules out; they are not asked and get no card. The point and the note are saved on the source (`reached`, `leave_out` with its AI stamp) and given to the recall feedback and enrich, which keep what they name and draft within them; his own highlights are drafted wherever they are. Finishing again after reading further starts the scope over. Source rows show how far he got as a thin bar under the thumbnail (YouTube), as Home's Continue reading does. Not built: a per-question "Not for me" (the note covers it), a Finish menu, picking sections.
- D101. **The Terminal look (2026-10-29, Tyler, option A of three retro mockups; refs claude.dev, Oxide, Geist Pixel).** The app takes a stylised 8-bit identity on a black-and-white base: Geist for the interface, uppercase Geist Mono labels, square corners, hairline-framed cards, flat white primary buttons, Geist Pixel for big numbers, segmented rings, dithered bars, and pixel-art gems (D90's meaning unchanged). List selection is neutral grey with a white edge bar (the sidebar keeps its open skill tinted in the skill colour, Tyler's sidebar pick C); in Review the picked answer inverts to white (from the Handheld option). No └ tree marks on rows (Tyler: wasted space). Reading stays in the system font. Fonts are bundled (OFL). Lives in `retro.css` loaded after `styles.css`, so it can be tuned or dropped in one place. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: amended by D129, D183, D184: sentence-case labels, round dots, soft corners.)

## Skill goals (2026-10-29, Tyler, the smaller A of `docs/reviews/2026-10-29-skill-goals-stress-test.md`)
- D102. **A goal per skill, tried from memory.** Under the skill's name, in place of the why line (D63), one line: his **goal**, something he could show he can do ("Explain spacing, retrieval and interleaving to a new teacher, from memory"), `goal` in `skills.yaml`, at most 140 characters, edited in place (Enter saves, Esc or clicking away keeps what was there, empty clears). **Try it** beside it opens the skill note's closed-book popup (D41) with the goal as its prompt; the try is saved in the skill note under `## Goal try, <yyyy-mm-dd>`, so his tries sit side by side and he sees the gap. Try it's hover says when he last tried. No card, no schedule, no rating, no reminder or count (D23); the `hook` stays in `skills.yaml` for AI settings (D66) but is no longer shown or edited in the app. Sub-skills ("parts", option C of `docs/design/skill-goals-and-parts.md`) are deferred: parts are written as headings in the skill note for now. Amends D63. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: a click away saves the field; Esc cancels, D211.)
- D103. **Our own diagram editor (Tyler, 2026-10-30).** Excalidraw is replaced by an in-house editor (`apps/desktop/src/draw/`) that keeps Excalidraw's file format (D40's `diagrams/<name>.excalidraw` and links unchanged; old files open, unknown fields kept, no per-edit version fields written). It is keyboard-first and built around the activities the evidence backs (`docs/research/brief-diagram-editor.md`): Tab from a node labels a link and then the next node, placed on free space; M types a map as `A -label-> B` lines or an indented outline (`(A)` ellipse, `{A}` diamond); ⇧M lays out a compare matrix that Tab walks like a spreadsheet. It also has the common tools of mainstream editors with Excalidraw's keys (boxes, diamonds, ellipses, bound arrows with labels and elbow routing, lines, pen, text, eraser, snapping to shapes and grid, groups, align and distribute, layers, lock, copy and paste as Excalidraw clipboard JSON, copy as PNG/SVG/text, find, undo). The look is the Terminal look's (D101): crisp lines on the dark page, square handles, dithered fills, mono labels. Its keys are the editable "Diagram" group of the keymap. Lazy chunk about 96 KB gzip, where Excalidraw loaded 528 KB plus 13 MB of fonts. Left out under the purpose rule: frames, collaboration, libraries, the laser pointer and the hand-drawn look; a map's sections are boxes or groups. Element links (⇧⌘K) take a URL or an `ore:` link.

## Name (2026-10-30, Tyler)
- D104. **The app is Ore, all the way down.** The app (`/Applications/Ore.app`, bundle id `com.tylerpoelking.ore`), windows, menus, notifications, errors, docs, the `ore` CLI, the library (`~/Ore`, its `.ore/` folder, `ore:` note links), `ORE_*` environment variables, the `/ore` skill, packages and the repo all say Ore; nothing keeps the old name. His test library moved over once by hand (skills kept). The Raycast extension is removed for now, to be rebuilt on the app once it settles; Raycast stays a design reference.

## Style (2026-10-30, Tyler)
- D105. **Pixel UI icons, one level of boxes** (icons amended by D129). Every UI icon was a Pixelarticons glyph (MIT, Gerrit Halfmann) in `apps/desktop/src/icons.tsx`, exported under the Phosphor name it replaced; sizes snap to 12, 16 or 24px with crisp edges so the 24 grid stays sharp on Retina; no filled "selected" variants. Skill icons stay MingCute (and old `ph:` icons still draw). Page sections are never framed and nothing is boxed inside a box (DESIGN.md "Style rules"); the skill page's sections are a mono label over a hairline rule (PR #175).

## Finishing a source (2026-10-31, Tyler)
- D106. **A Finish button, and a quiz that doesn't have to finish.** The reader's top bar shows **Finish** beside ⋯ while a source is in To read; it, the ⋯ and ⌘K item, the Source menu and ⇧⌘↵ open the quiz panel (D52). That shortcut does nothing while he types in the source note or a highlight note, where ⌘↵ out of habit used to open the quiz; ⌘↵ stays the quiz's own next step, as Check in Review. The quiz no longer marks the source done on its last question: its closing card asks "Done with this source?" with **Keep reading** (stays in To read, saves the recall and answers, drafts nothing; Esc too) or **Done** (marks it done and drafts its cards, D19). So quizzing partway is the same flow (interpolated testing; D100 scopes it to what he reached) and the next Finish starts a fresh quiz. "Just mark done" on the recall step still finishes at once. Done shows a one-second moment in the panel (the check springs in, "Source finished", "New cards start in tomorrow's review"; still under Reduce Motion), then the list with a toast "Finished “…” · cards start tomorrow" and Undo (⌘Z), which moves it back to To read. Amends D71's "no toast on top". Amends D52, D57 ("no quiz any time mid-read": one he starts himself from Finish is in) and D100.

## Feed (2026-11-03, Tyler)
- D107. **A Feed under Home, nothing saved until he keeps something** (design: `docs/design/feeds-and-newsletters.md`). He follows blogs, newsletters with a feed and YouTube channels by pasting a link in ⌘N (**Follow** beside Add; a link that is itself a feed follows on ↵). The sidebar's **Feed** (⌘3) lists their posts of the last 14 days: **Recent** (newest first, with the feed's name) or **By feed** (each feed's five newest under its name; drag a feed to reorder, or Move up / down), and **Unread** or **All** for both, in the list title's view menu. Opening a post marks it read and reads it as a preview: not listed anywhere, no enrich, "Not saved" where Finish sits. His first highlight or source note line keeps it as a source, filed under the feed's skills, and starts enrich; leaving with neither deletes it (a preview left behind is cleared after a day). U marks a post read or unread, with Undo. No unread counts or badges. Email: no address of Ore's own (it needs a server); email-only newsletters go through an email-to-feed service such as Kill the Newsletter. Checked on the Mac when the Feed opens (at most every 30 minutes) and every 3 hours while the app runs. Before following, ⌘N says what the feed holds ("How I AI · 15 posts, newest today"), and so does the toast, so he knows it is a real feed. A YouTube channel whose feed server fails (it 404s or 500s about half the time, 2026-11) is read through yt-dlp from its Videos tab. No OpenRSS or RSSHub: tested unreliable, they see what he follows, and Folo's code is AGPL. Not on iPhone yet. Amends D13 (newsletters were v3) and D96 (feed links were refused; they still are by Add). (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: Mark read is ⇧⌘U, D234.)

## Row details (2026-11-03, Tyler)
- D108. **Each list picks what its rows show.** A source row's right-click and ⋯ menus (a course's and a multi-pick's too) have **Show ▸** with Type, Author, Site and Date added as checkboxes; ticking one leaves the menu open so he sees the rows change (Linear's Display properties). The list title's view menu holds the same toggles, and ⌘K has "Show/Hide … on rows". Remembered per list on this Mac: each skill's To read, Done and Archived, and All sources', keep their own (`ore.details`); a list never set shows Type plus whatever the old Mac-wide columns (D77) had. Author is whoever the source names (a page's byline, a video's channel, a podcast's show, a PDF, EPUB or Office file's creator, a repo's owner); a web page that names no one shows its site instead, in italics with a hint, unless Site is on beside it; his own text with no author shows nothing. Site is the web address's host, blank for files and text. Amends D74 (no date on rows by default stays) and D77. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the view menu became section tabs, D226.)
- D109. **One source added by hand opens** (Tyler, 2026-11-03; Notion and Linear open what you just made). One link, file or text added from ⌘N, a paste outside a field, ⌘O, the browser tab or a drop opens in the reader as soon as it is in, with the skill picker open on its title if it landed with no skill. It stays put, with the toast and its Show, for a list or several files, a course, Learn this (which has its own Read now, D97), and any add that finishes after he moved on or while he was reading a source: an add never pulls him out of what he is reading. Undo in the toast archives it and closes it. Amends D89 ("he stays where he is"). (Amended 2026-11-22, pointer only: a source added with no skill opens no skill picker, D345.)
- D110. **To read lists the last touched first** (Tyler, 2026-11-03, over newest added first and keeping started first). A source is touched when it is added or opened (`opened_at`, stamped by `ore opened` when the reader opens it, at most once per 10 minutes so flipping between sources doesn't rewrite files). Pinned sources still list first; a source not opened yet keeps its dimmer title. A batch he just added and the source he was just reading are both on top, and sources he never came back to sink. Applies on the phone too. Amends D28 (started first, then not opened). (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: unopened sources are not dimmed, D134.)
- D111. **Remove junk from a source's text** (Tyler, 2026-11-03, picked over a Markdown edit mode). In the highlight popup an eraser (⇧⌫; ⌫ stays Remove highlight) removes every block the selection touches (paragraph, list item, table, code fence, image line), never half a sentence; started in a heading, it removes that heading's section, up to the next heading of the same or a higher level (a site's table of contents, "Related posts", "Share this"). The author's words are never rewritten. Highlights after the cut shift by exactly its length; highlights inside it are removed (their cards stay; questions stay, as on Reimport, D93). The toast says what went, with Undo and Redo on the app history; each cut is kept in `.ore/cuts/<id>/` (`ore cut --undo|--redo`). The original file (`source.*`) is untouched, so Reimport brings the full text back. Not inside a video transcript: its words are timed to the video. A site that keeps leaving junk gets an import fix instead (D96).
- D112. **The highlight popup opens where the selection ends, with its tools in a column** (Tyler, 2026-11-03, picked B over tools beside the types). It used to sit centred above the first line, but the mouse lets go at the end of a selection, so the types were a long move away, across the note. Now, as Hypothesis's adder does, it opens under the last line, centred on where the mouse let go; after an upward drag (the selection runs backward) it opens above the first line; with no room on that side it goes to the other side of the whole passage, so it never covers what he highlighted; a passage taller than the view gets it just under the pointer. The order inside stays the same on both sides (types on top), so the hand learns one place. A click on a highlight opens it under that highlight at the pointer; the keyboard (D86), the margin marks and the Collected tab open it under the passage's last line. The tools leave the note's row for a thin column at the right (Copy with source, Remove text, Remove highlight; Play from here and Explain in a second column when they show), so the note keeps the popup's width and the popup never grows taller. On the phone the tools stay a row under the note (44pt targets). Amends D91.
- D113. **8-bit motion, from RareUI** (Tyler, 2026-11-03, "go for it" on all five of the prototype's picks, built one at a time). RareUI's springs become sprite motion: hard frames on a 2 px grid, a bounce is one frame past the target, and a keyboard move never animates. First the sidebar cursor: a 3×5 pixel arrow in the sidebar's left gutter points at the open row and hops there in five 28 ms frames after a click in the sidebar (Bounce sidebar); a shortcut, the palette, a link elsewhere or reduced motion moves it at once. Next: the reader's outline rail, a toast stack, the working line, rolling pixel numbers. Left out: the delete button (it asks to confirm; D-undo rule), the task list (it reorders his rows), the activity grid (a streak, D23). The position bar shows only for a source without an outline (fewer than two headings): with one, the outline's filled lines already say where he is (Tyler, 2026-11-03; amends D45).

## Source rows (2026-11-03, Tyler)
- D114. **A source row is two lines, its title never squeezed** (Tyler picked D of A–D, mocks in the project's `source-row-meta/`). Line one is the title at full width; line two is the author (D108's byline) and the other skills it is under, as the same skill pills used everywhere else, emoji and name (Tyler, after trying plain text and name-only pills). Date added, Site and Type stay columns on the right, so they line up down the list, and drop out when the list is narrow (the skill page's panel, a small window). The picture grows to 64×36 so a source is known by it; reading progress stays the bar under it (YouTube), the same at every width. Author is on by default and Type off, since the picture says the kind. A lesson keeps one line (its skills are its course's); a search hit's second line is the matching text. Applies to All sources, skill pages and courses; amends D74 (one-line rows), D77 and D108. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: no emoji, D157; a skill on a row is its icon and a muted name, D196.)

## Chapter recall in the source note (2026-11-03, Tyler)
- D115. **A chapter's recall is written in the source note, under the chapter's title** (Tyler, "let's try it" on the thread's mocks in the project's `chapter-recall/`; replaces the margin marker and the quiz-panel chapter recall, amends D18, D34.4, D52). The goal is a source note that reads as his own notes, not a log of recalls.
  1. **The sign.** At the end of each chapter, "Recall this chapter" with a pixel signpost sits in the column, centred in the gap before the next one (mono caps, `faint`; M opens the nearest). Chapters under ~500 words (title pages, contents, a "how to read this" intro) get none, and nor does the last (Finish covers it). A recalled chapter's sign is gone. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the sign is mono in sentence case, no caps, D129.)
  2. **The block.** Clicking it opens the source note with `## <chapter title>` at the end and three prompts on empty lines beneath ("The claim this chapter makes", "One example or piece of evidence", "What it changes for you"). They are placeholders, never text: what he leaves empty disappears. The source is hidden while the block is live (D34.4, his note stays visible since it is where he writes). ⌘↵ ends it: what he wrote under the heading is recorded as `chapter_recalls[n]` (`ore recall --chapter N --text … --heading <title>`, which does not append it twice), then the feedback shows under the note, dismissable, with Retry on failure. Esc ends it without recording: what he typed stays, empty prompts and an empty heading go.
  3. **The eye** (⌥H) beside Finish hides or shows the source while he writes, any time; remembered. The block turns it on and puts it back after. No setting for it: a remembered button is the setting.
  4. **What a chapter is**: a division the author made, never one inferred from headings. Built: EPUB chapters, a YouTube video's own chapter list (its `###` headings, the only headings a transcript has, so it needs no reimport), and a PDF's outline (2026-11-07: each top-level bookmark is a `<!-- chapter N -->` and a `## ` heading where its page has the title; an older PDF gets them on reimport); a course lesson is already its own source. Everything else is one chapter, whose recall is Finish.
  5. Prompts are generic for now; prompts written per chapter by enrich (never answers, D34.3) can come later.


## AI model per provider (2026-11-03, Tyler)
- D116. **He picks the model for each AI provider in Settings › AI, and the list comes from the provider** (Tyler: "Sonnet default for Claude; Codex lists what it offers; new models show up by themselves"). Saved per vendor as `models: {claude, codex}` in skills.yaml (`ore settings --model`, `ore models` lists); `ORE_AI_MODEL` still overrides both. Claude offers Claude Code's aliases (Sonnet, the default; Opus; Fable; Haiku), each of which always means that family's newest model, since `claude` has no command that lists models. Codex offers what `codex debug models` lists as shown in its own picker, in its order (refreshed from OpenAI when signed in, else the catalog the installed Codex ships with), plus "Codex default" (its `config.toml` model). A saved model Codex stops listing stays in the menu, and a failed run names the model problem and points to Settings, never a silent switch. One model per provider: no per-task models or reasoning effort until one has a learning purpose.

## One highlight, cards planned by the AI (2026-11-04, Tyler)
Tyler couldn't tell Keep from Card, and didn't want to decide what is worth a card ("users don't have that knowledge"). His "AI Revolution" source had 26 highlights: 25 Keeps, no Cards, no notes, so the D19/D91 rules would have drafted nothing from them. They were also structural: a heading, a "three reasons" lead-in with its three items, three related definitions. Checked against `research/brief-learning-science.md`: highlighting does little alone (g 0.30, comprehension ns) and pays off when it feeds a second step; learners mark the wrong things (g −0.70) while expert-picked passages help (g 0.44); a small daily load is what keeps review going.
- D118. **One highlight; a ★ Key point mark; the AI plans the cards.** Amends D15, D19, D91 and D99.
  1. **Marks.** Selecting makes a highlight, as before (D15). The popup has two switches, **★ Key point** (2) and **Question** (3); pressing the one that is on goes back to a plain highlight (1). Hover says what each does. Key points take the highlight's gold tint and a star in the margin; plain highlights show no margin icon. Questions are unchanged (D75). Card here (D99) becomes "Add a key point", the same typed, anchored line. Files keep their names: a key point is `intent: card`, a plain highlight `keep` (as D43). (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: a key point is blue, D124, and marked with a gem, D229.)
  2. **Plan.** A key point is drafted as soon as enrich runs, like a Card was. Plain highlights wait for the finish; then enrich gives the AI every one not weighed yet, with the key points, his notes, the ideas the finish recall says he missed, the skill's goal and the questions the skill's cards already ask. It groups the highlights into ideas (a card records the extra highlights as `also`), covers every key point, writes at most 5 drafts from plain highlights alone, skips strays and anything a prequestion or existing card asks, and weighs his own words first. An idea with parts is an Explain card with the parts as rubric points, never "name all three". The weighed highlights are saved on the source (`planned`), so they are not weighed twice; a highlight made later is weighed at the next enrich. Prompt `enrich@9`.
  3. A Keep with a note is no longer drafted before the finish (D91's rule); its note now weighs in the plan.

## Resize a highlight (2026-11-04, Tyler)
- D117. **A highlight's passage can be changed after it is made, two ways** (Tyler picked C of A handles / B select over / C both). An open highlight shows a handle at each edge in its type's colour (Kindle, Books on iPhone): drag either to grow or shrink it; it snaps to whole words, the popup hides while dragging and comes back at the new end, Esc mid-drag puts it back, ⌘Z undoes (`ore annotate <id> --exact … --start N`, `moveHighlight` in core). Selecting past one highlight's edge grows that highlight to cover both (Readwise Reader) instead of stacking a second; a selection inside a bigger one (a snippet) is still its own, and one over two highlights is a new one, so no note is merged away. Same id, type and note; a card already drafted from the old passage is not redrafted.

## PDF layout (2026-11-06, Tyler)
- D119. **A PDF reads as its text, not its page furniture.** Tyler picked fixing our own pdf.js reader over adding Docling (MIT, but a 1.8 GB Python install, ~1 min for 35 pages, still let margin words in and turned em-dashes into hyphens) or OpenDataLoader PDF (Apache-2.0, Java; margin words still glued in, running heads kept); AGPL/GPL tools (PyMuPDF4LLM, MinerU, Marker, Poppler) are out. The reader now drops text set smaller than the body entirely beside the body's column (a textbook's margin glossary terms; two columns of body text are both kept), a page's first or last line set apart from the text when it is a page number, starts or ends with one, or repeats on other pages (running heads and feet), and takes a numbered line ("1.1.1 Acting humanly") set a little larger as a heading. Bullets become list items, each footnote its own paragraph at the page's end, and a "CHAPTER 1" over "INTRODUCTION" opener titles the file "Chapter 1: Introduction" (a TeX file name like `chap01.dvi` never does). Reimport reads a PDF or EPUB file again from its kept copy, so sources added before a reader fix get it.
- D120. **Ore is free and open source under the AGPL-3.0-or-later** (Tyler, 2026-11-06). He builds it for his own learning and may share it; nobody may sell a closed copy, and there are no plans to sell it. Dependencies must be AGPL-compatible (MIT, BSD, ISC, Apache-2.0, MPL-2.0, LGPL, GPL-3.0, AGPL-3.0; model weights' licenses count too); never GPL-2.0-only, proprietary, non-commercial or license-key tools. The iPhone app bundles only permissive code, because App Store terms clash with GPL; GPL tools (PyMuPDF, Marker and the like) run on the Mac, and their results reach the phone through sync, so the phone still adds YouTube links and PDFs on its own. Big tools (Python, ML models) are optional Mac downloads, never needed to add or open a source. Forks must use another name than "Ore". The public repo starts from a fresh history; earlier history and the strategy docs stay private. Lifts the GPL ban in D4 and D119.
- D121. **PDF cleanup rules, scored on 15 PDFs** (Tyler, 2026-11-06). A bake-off of pdf.js against MuPDF on 15 PDFs of 12 kinds (two-column papers, a book, a magazine, tables, Japanese, a report, a form, slides, a scan, a locked file, AIMA) found the engines read the same words in the same order, so Ore keeps pdf.js, which also runs on the iPhone, and improves the rules on top: a small raised number after a word is a footnote's mark and is dropped; after a number, a sign or a lone letter it is a power (10¹¹, 10⁻³, d²). A word broken at a line's end with a hyphen loses it unless the document spells it with one elsewhere (fine-tuned) or the hyphen follows a capital (NP-complete); a word broken with no hyphen in the text is joined when the whole word appears elsewhere. A paragraph that runs onto the next page is finished on the page it starts on, above that page's footnotes. Running heads and page numbers are found by their height on the page, not by where the file writes them (Chromium writes them last). Still gaps for both engines: formulas, tables, and words split with no hyphen that appear only once. The test set and scripts are in the project files (`pdf-bakeoff/`); `files/hazards.pdf` holds one line per hazard.
- D125. **PDFs: pdf.js reads the prose, Docling the tables, formulas and scans** (Tyler, 2026-11-06). A second bake-off scored Docling (IBM; MIT code, Apache models) against the arXiv papers' own LaTeX: display formulas 95%, tables 77% cell by cell, and it reads scanned pages; pdf.js gets none of those, but reads the prose as well (95.8% vs 95.1% of words) and keeps margin notes, footnotes and dashes right where Docling doesn't. Marker (model license caps revenue) and MinerU 4 (sends files to its own service by default, extra license terms) were ruled out. So the two are combined: pdf.js stays the instant reader on the Mac and the iPhone, and Docling comes as an optional Mac download that swaps in only the tables, the display formulas and scanned pages, so highlights on the prose never move. Part 1, in pdf.js: bold and italic from the fonts' names (a face used for most of the text marks nothing; math fonts' italic is notation, not emphasis); a footnote's mark becomes `[^n]` and its note `[^n]: …` when both are found on the page, and the reader shows the note on hover (Mac) or tap (iPhone), Wikipedia and Books style; a paragraph whose first line is indented starts there (a reference's hanging second line doesn't). Part 2, Docling: `ore tables --install` downloads it (pinned uv and docling, its layout, table and formula models, about 2 GB, into `~/Library/Application Support/Ore/docling`; `--remove` deletes it all), and once it is there each PDF added is read in the background (`ore tables <item>`, seconds to minutes a paper). Its reading is kept beside the source as `layout.json`; the lines inside each table or display-formula box are replaced by a GFM table (cell text from pdf.js inside Docling's cell boxes, so bold and powers stay) or a `$$` LaTeX block, and the rest of the text is unchanged, so highlights stay put. A reimport keeps the reading, and Undo takes it back out. Scored on the bake-off papers: formulas 95%, tables 81% (Docling alone 77%). Inline math and the in-app offer are still to come. Amended by D273: the reader is Granite-Docling, not Docling's standard models. Amended by D-new-paddle-pages: the reader is PaddleOCR-VL, on every PDF, and it marks headings too.

## Core promise (2026-11-06, Tyler)
- D122. **Ore's core promise: everything people learn from imports whole, in one format built for learning.** At least the top 80% of what people learn from (web articles, YouTube and other video, podcasts and audio, every kind of PDF including tables and formulas, EPUB books, documents, slides, notebooks, screenshots) imports as robustly as possible, and every type lands as the same Markdown `doc.md`: the content's own title, author and date, its headings as the outline (chunking, chapter recall, D115), its images kept as assets (dual coding), tables as tables, math as KaTeX, code fenced, timestamps for anything that plays (D48), and the original file kept so reimport (D93) can improve it. Because the shape is one, every source is read, highlighted, noted, quizzed and reviewed the same way. Import is the base of the loop, so a broken import is a core bug, not polish. Builds on D13 and D96 (refuse with the next step, never save junk). Open gaps live as `gap:` cells in `packages/core/test/intake-grid.test.ts`; the ranked list Tyler picks from is `docs/reviews/2026-11-06-import-promise.md`. Tyler picked gaps 1 to 3, in order (2026-11-06). Gap 1 built: a video with no captions is heard (its sound downloaded by yt-dlp and transcribed on the Mac in its own language), captions in the video's own language are read when it has no English ones, and video files (mp4, mov, m4v) are heard where they lie, with no size limit; only the transcript is kept. Gap 2 built: PDF figures (pictures on the page, touching ones joined, logos and icons left out; vector-drawn charts not yet), PowerPoint slide pictures, notebook plots and a saved web page's own images come in as assets beside the text they illustrate. Gap 3 built: a Spotify episode link is heard from the same episode in the show's public feed (Spotify's page names it, Apple's podcast search finds the feed); a show only on Spotify says so. Gap 9 built: arXiv papers' authors, damaged zipped files and unshared Google Docs say what they are; the grid's PDF author and TED title and date cells are checked.

## Highlights snap to whole words (2026-11-06, Tyler)
- D123. **Every highlight snaps to whole words** (Kindle, Apple Books). A selection's start inside a word goes back to the word's start; its end inside a word goes on to the word's end and, when the word ends a sentence, takes the full stop (`.` `!` `?` `…`) and any closing quote or bracket around it (`go now."`, `(mostly).`); a comma or a bracket with no sentence end stays out. An edge already between words stays where he put it, so starting on an opening quote keeps it, and never takes the space beside it. Words are the system's (Intl.Segmenter), so Japanese or Chinese snap to its words; code and math snap to nothing. The same rule drives the mouse, the iPhone's selection and the resize handles (D117), across inline marks and around a transcript's timestamps. ⌥ while selecting or dragging keeps exactly what he selected (listed in Shortcuts). `apps/desktop/src/snap.ts`.

## Key points are blue (2026-11-06, Tyler)
- D124. **A ★ Key point is blue; a plain highlight stays gold** (amends D118.1). Tyler read a whole source believing every highlight was a key point, because on the dark page both painted the same gold and the margin star was not enough of a cue. Each state now has one colour everywhere it shows: plain highlight gold (`--keep`), key point blue (`--card`, as the light page already did), question purple; the popup's switch, the margin icon, the Collected chips and the quiz stepper's star take the same hue. The default stays a plain highlight: he keeps not having to judge what is card-worthy (D118), and the colour, not a default, tells the two apart. Making key point the default was rejected: it would draft a card from every highlight again.

## An open note on a plain highlight (2026-11-07, Tyler)
- D126. **A plain highlight's note says "Note…"** (amends D94). "Connects to…" read as a demand to link the passage to another concept, so Tyler stopped writing the thoughts he has while reading. A placeholder nudge has no measured learning effect (D91, Mueller 2014), so the plain highlight's field is open to any thought; the generative prompts stay where a test follows: key point "This works because…", question "My question… my guess:". Enrich (`enrich@10`) treats a plain highlight's note as a sign only when it names or explains an idea in the document; an aside stays his note and never becomes a card by itself.

## Try it: techniques to try on his own work (2026-11-07, Tyler)
- D127. **A third switch, Try it (4), and a To try list on the skill page** (amends D118, D91's "no fourth type"). Adult skill learning is mostly trying and reflecting, so a technique he reads about can be marked to try on his own work (e.g. a storytelling technique for his next video). Evidence: reflecting on a task improves later performance more than spending the same time practising it, and people skip reflection when left to choose (Di Stefano et al., "Learning by thinking", 2015); a short reflection after skills practice improved performance a week later (Thommes et al. 2023, RCT); writing where and when follows D67's implementation intentions.
  1. **Mark.** The popup's third switch, green, a checked box; its note asks "I'll try this on…" (his plan). Stored as `intent: apply`; `reflection` holds how it went. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the mark is a flask, D229.)
  2. **To try.** On the skill page above Questions, shown once he has one: each try with a check box, his plan over the passage (one click opens it), to try first; tried ones fold under "Tried (n)". Checking one off opens "How did it go?" in place: Enter saves, Esc skips. Every step goes on the history (⌘Z), Remove has Undo. Nothing counts or badges them beyond the fold's own "n to try" (D23). CLI: `ore tries`, `ore tried <t> [--text] [--undo]`.
  3. **Card.** A try checked off with a reflection is drafted once at the next enrich (`enrich@11`): one Explain prompt that asks him to use the technique on a new case like his, with the document's steps and the lesson from his try as rubric points. Never a grade of the try itself.
  4. **Not in v1.** Reminders to try, repeat cycles, a practice log. Watch whether he checks tries off first (as D102's Try it).

## The tutor (2026-11-07, Tyler)
- D128. **A tutor chat, grown out of Ask your library** (Tyler's picks on `docs/research/brief-ai-tutor.md`). A bubble at the bottom right, ⌘J or ⌘K opens it; Esc or ⌘J closes it and gives focus back. Docked beside a source and its note, three columns left the page too tight at 1280 px (Tyler, 2026-11-07), so it fits as Notion AI's does: a card floating over the bottom right corner by default, and a header button docks it at the right, where it takes the side panel's place (the panel comes back when it closes, and showing the panel closes it). The choice is remembered. It replaces the Ask your library dialog (`ore ask-library` stays for the eval). One conversation, kept while he moves around the app; + starts a new one.
  1. **Context.** The source on screen is its context, shown as a chip over the field (× leaves it out). Its passages go first, then the library's (`tutorPassages`).
  2. **Stance from context, no mode switch** (`tutor@1`): his own questions get short, straight answers, cited to the passage like Ask (each [n] opens it, the panel stays). When his sources don't cover it, it may answer from general knowledge and the reply says "Not from your sources"; it never passes that off as his source. Practice he is meant to answer himself gets hints, one rung per reply (nudge, narrower question, worked answer on "show me"; Bastani 2025). Free-form talk follows the tutor's tone: warm, direct, one question back at most.
  3. **@ sources and skills** (Tyler, 2026-11-07, as Claude and ChatGPT do): @ lists skills and sources, picking one writes @Name into the message, and every name still there when he sends points the tutor at it (`tutor@2`): those sources' passages first, a skill keeps the library search to its sources. The @ context carries forward (Tyler, 2026-11-07): the last message's @s stay in effect for later messages, so a follow-up ("Show me", practice feedback) still reads that material, until he @s something else (the new message's @s replace them), drops one with its chip's × (shown beside the open source's chip) or starts a new chat (Undo brings them back with the thread).
  4. **Show me and + Card** (`tutor@3`): a reply that holds back an answer (a hint rung, or a quiz question) says so (`hint`), and the last one offers Show me, which asks for the worked answer. A reply that explains one idea worth keeping proposes a card; + Card keeps it as a draft on the source it cites first (else the one he asked about), `from: tutor`, with that source's skills and the reply's AI stamp (D9, D21); ⌘Z retires it.
  5. **Quiz me** (Tyler, 2026-11-07): a button under the chat opens Review on his due cards, scoped to the skills he @-mentioned, else the open source's skills, else everything due. He rates them himself as in Review (FSRS unchanged, D21), and Esc comes back to the chat's page. Unscored practice in the chat stays on the "Test my understanding" starter. The mascot comes later (Tyler: an advanced one).
  6. **Practice** (Tyler, 2026-11-07, after the research doc "Open-ended exercises in the tutor"): not a mode. A Practice starter asks the tutor for one open exercise to write from memory, the kind picked to fit and varied: recall and use, explain why, a new case, connect, argue it (never "teach a beginner", which does worse written). With a source open, "Write it in your note" opens the source note under the exercise as a heading, with placeholder lines, the source hidden and the tutor out of the way, as a chapter recall does (D115). ⌘↵ sends what he wrote, Markdown and diagrams (read as their labels and arrows), back to the chat, and Esc leaves it unsent. His answer stays in his note. The feedback says what holds, what's missing (cited) and asks one follow-up, never a score (D21, D23). Short exercises he can answer in the chat. It replaces the "Test my understanding" starter.
- D129. **The soft terminal look** (Tyler, 2026-11-07, signature-style thread; north stars opencode for calm, Railway for pixel art woven in, Charm for play only; amends D101 and D105). Learning can be anxiety-inducing, so the app is calm, delightful and satisfying, and still feels advanced, hence the terminal. No "cute" (too Duolingo), no 3D, no blue or violet cast.
  1. **Palette.** opencode's neutral ramp a step softer: ground `#1b1b1b`, raised `#232323`, text `#e4e4e2`. Intents and skill colours are pastel (key point `#dcc070`, card `#82bde6`, question `#b59aec`, try `#79d6b2`). Focus and links are neutral, not blue.
  2. **Type.** Labels, metadata, keys and big numbers are OpenTUI Mono (opencode's Iosevka build, OFL, no reserved name; Latin subset, Geist Mono fills the rest), in sentence case: no uppercase labels. Geist stays for the interface, Charter for a source's text. Geist Pixel is gone; 8-bit stays for gems, icons, rings and bars.
  3. **Icons.** Every UI icon is a MingCute glyph (Apache-2.0, the set the skill icons already use) redrawn by hand on a 16 grid, for MingCute's thickness in 8-bit (Tyler: "8-bit iffied"). `icons.tsx` keeps the Phosphor names; sprites draw at 16px (32 at 24px and up) with crisp edges. A missing icon is drawn on that grid from its MingCute glyph. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: icons draw at 20px, D170, D171.)
  4. **Highlight popup.** The picked type sits on a grey key with only its icon in colour (option B: the underline "feels cheap"); the popup is a soft raised sheet with a 2px edge in that colour.
  5. **Wordmark** (Tyler's pick A of four): "ore_" in Poppins ExtraBold Italic (OFL) with the trailing underscore as a terminal cursor in progress green, after Charm's lettering. It lives where a name does a job, the app icon (and so About and the Dock), never inside the app's screens (purpose rule).

- D130. **A skill page's reading list is Reader's, with the questions a source may answer on its row** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick B of five structures laid over the real page, refined; module round after the element rounds of D129). On a skill page a source row is Readwise Reader's: a 96×54 picture, the title, the author and other skills, then the kind as a mono label (its length joins once import records one). A source that may answer an open question of the skill (D75's matches) carries a question mark in the question colour at the row's right, centred; hovering it lists the questions, so choosing what to read next and chasing a question are one act. The list's title stays its view menu (D74); a sort or type line waits until there is a sort to pick or a type filter worth having. All sources keeps D114's rows and columns. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the view menu became section tabs, D226.)

- D131. **A skill page's question is one row, the question first** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick A of five with E's circle; amends D47's row on the skill page). The row is the question in one line (his note on the highlight, or the passage when he wrote none, or what he typed), its source under it in faint; the passage it came from moves into the row's hint and is one click away (Open passage). A circle in the question colour at the left is Answered, as a try's box is Tried (D127): a click marks it, with Undo; answered rows show a check in the circle and strike the question. A square in the question colour at the right says a source may answer it (D75): the hint names the sources, a click opens the first. Answer and Begin research stay in the row's hover actions and menus. The reader's Questions tab keeps its layout. (Amended 2026-11-13: the may-answer square is a round dot, Tyler's pick 2026-11-13, F82, D184.)
- D132. **The warm-up is one quiet line** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick B of four structures laid over the real page; amends D46's warm-up card and D41's ⓘ). While a skill's note is empty, "What do you already know about …?" is one muted line under the Your note head with a small bordered Start at its right, no band and no ⓘ: the why ("Without looking. It becomes your note's first draft, and recalling first makes it stick.") is Start's hover tip. The head keeps its label, widen and +. Nothing else changes: Start opens the D41 recall, the line stays through his first lines and goes once a recall is saved or on the next visit.
- D133. **Typing after a highlight writes its note** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, from the Impeccable round 5 taste calls; amends D126's Tab-first note). With the highlight popup open, the first printable key moves focus into the note and lands there, as in Notion's comment box, so putting the thought into words is the next thing, not an extra step. 1–4 still set the type while the note is empty; Tab, Backspace, Enter and Esc keep their meaning. No single-letter reader shortcut (L, O, C, Q, N) runs while the popup is open.
- D134. **Lists carry only what he acts on** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, Impeccable round 5 taste calls; the purpose rule). The sidebar shows no count beside All sources or a skill (what would he do because AI says 1?). A source with a note shows no dot after its title (it read as "unread", and the side panel shows the note). Unopened sources are not dimmed; only a read Feed post dims. Questions in the reader's Collected tab are one-line rows like its other groups (amends D131's "the reader's Questions tab keeps its layout"): Jump to the passage, Show where it was asked and Not answered here live in the row's ⋯ and right-click menus, and the answer pieces show in its answer box.
- D135. **All sources uses the skill page's Reader rows, and Settings uses the side panel's segmented control** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, Impeccable round 5 taste calls). Every source list (All sources, a skill page, Feeds) shows the same Readwise Reader row: a 96×54 picture, the title, and the kind in mono; lessons and Feed posts keep their own rows. Settings' choice controls (reading width, reading page, skill tags, style) are the side panel's segmented control: a rounded track with the chosen option raised, so one control looks the same wherever it appears.
- D136. **The tutor's ✦ lives in the title bar** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick A of three mocks; amends D128's bubble at the bottom right, which covered the panel's foot and Home's skill column). A bare 16px Sparkle drawn as the panel toggles (26×24, `faint`, `bg-hover` on hover, "Tutor ⌘J" hint): left of + Add on Home, All sources and Feed, left of the reader's panel toggle, alone at the top right on a skill page. Hidden while the tutor is open, as before. Amended by D160: the button is an owl at the bottom right again.
- D137. **The tutor searches the web when his sources don't hold the answer, and cites the pages as it cites passages** (Tyler, 2026-11-08: "tutor should be able to search web + answer from its general knowledge as well. Just use citations both for web search sources and internal ore paragraphs"; settles D128's open question on not-in-library questions). His library comes first: a question his passages answer never searches. When they don't, the reply says so in words, then for a specific fact (a date, number, name, result, anything recent) it searches and cites the page: `[n]` opens it in the browser, and its row under the reply leads with the site ("britannica.com · Hermann Ebbinghaus"), so a long title can't hide that it is from the web. A general explanation it knows well comes from general knowledge, under "Not from your sources", without searching. A web citation is checked in code as a passage's is: its URL must be one the search returned and its quote words the search returned (on Claude, whose stream reports them; Codex doesn't, so there a link is only checked to be one). Never on practice, quiz questions or small talk.
- D138. **The tutor's reply streams in as it is written** (2026-11-08, from the speed loop; ChatGPT's and Claude's chats). Its first words show about 2 seconds after he sends, where the whole reply took 5. `ore tutor --stream <id>` reads claude's stream-json with partial messages, picks the `reply` out of the structured answer's half-written JSON and writes it to stderr as `{"progress": {stream, reply}}`, which the app's `ore-progress` events put on the turn with that id. Its `[n]` markers wait for the citations, which come with the whole reply; a retry after a failed check starts the text over. While a web search runs the wait reads "Searching the web…" instead of "Thinking…". Codex replies come whole. The tutor also stops loading Read, Grep and Glob: a task with no files to read gets no file tools (about 3k fewer input tokens a turn).
- D139. **Citations read as ChatGPT's desktop app shows them** (Tyler, 2026-11-08: "Citations are ugly and bulky make them more ux friendly and concise. Like how ChatGPT desktop app does it"; amends D128 and D137's numbered markers and the source rows always open under a reply). Each run of `[n]` markers is one small grey pill named for its source: a page by its site as people say it ("britannica", "wikipedia"), his own source by its title, cut to fit, "+1" when the run cites a second. Hovering it shows a card per source: where it is ("britannica.com", "Your source › History"), its title and the quoted words; a click on the pill or a card row opens it (his source at the quote, a page in the browser). The list of every source waits behind a "Sources" pill under the reply. No favicons: they would need a call to a third-party service for every page.
- D140. **The tutor gives the answer on the second wrong try, and Quiz me corrects a miss with the answer** (2026-11-08, from the new tutor pedagogy eval, `docs/guides/tutor-eval.md`; refines D128's ladder). The hint ladder counts his attempts across the conversation: on his second wrong try the reply is the answer and why, not a third hint. In Quiz me, a wrong or partly wrong answer gets the right answer and why in a sentence, cited, then the next question (corrective feedback is what makes a missed retrieval stick: Pashler et al. 2005). Hints before answers stay the rule for a first try (Bastani et al. 2025).
- D141. **Review shows the words that earned each key point** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick 2 of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-shadcn-components.md`, after assistant-ui's confidence marker). The grader (`grade@3`) also returns, for each covered key point, the shortest stretch of his answer that states it, copied word for word; core keeps a quote only when the point is covered and the words are found in his answer (case, curly quotes and spacing folded), so it never shows words he didn't write. At the check step, hovering a key point he got, or focusing its mark, underlines those words in Your answer, and VoiceOver hears them in the mark's label; a missed point, or one he marks missed, underlines nothing. Why: feedback that points at the exact words works better than a verdict (Shute 2008), and a wrong credit is plain to see, so his own rating (D21) is better informed. Practice feedback in the tutor (D128.6) can take the same marks later.
- D142. **Show me's worked answer comes one step at a time** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick 3 of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-shadcn-components.md`, after assistant-ui's math block; amends D128.4). When a reply is a worked answer or a worked example (`tutor@7`), the tutor writes it as 2 to 6 steps after a one-sentence lead, cited like the reply. The chat shows the first step; Next step shows the one after, and its hover asks him to work the next step out first; the last step hands the field the focus. The steps he has shown stay shown while he moves around the app, and they go with the next message as what the tutor said. Why: a worked example teaches most when the learner explains each step to himself before reading it and the working fades into his own (Renkl & Atkinson 2003; self-explanation, Chi 1994); a wall of answer is read once and nodded at.
- D143. **A figure in a source opens to fill the window on a click** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick 6 of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-shadcn-components.md`: "Add it"). A figure, slide or chart drawn at the column's width often has labels too small to read; a click grows it from where it sits to fill the window less 24px, over the page's own colour (Medium, Readwise Reader). A click or a scroll shrinks it back into place, Esc puts it back at once, and no key or click reaches the page behind while it is open. A picture that is a link follows the link, and one already about as big as the window doesn't zoom. One zoom for the whole reader (Kibo UI's image zoom and `react-medium-image-zoom` were tried: one dialog and hidden button per image, and keys and clicks still reached the page).
- D144. **New cards take a fixed share of every session** (Tyler, 2026-11-08: "assume the user is going to have a large backlog of due cards"; Ore is for a 10-minute-to-1-hour daily habit, made as productive as possible). With a standing backlog, "new cards only in the goal time the due cards leave" (D65.2) meant new cards never came in. Now one card in five is a new draft, mixed in, for as long as due cards last; the due cards fill the other places, most urgent first (D22, D24), and today's relearn passes come last. `new_per_day` stays the ceiling, and a draft still waits a day (D65.3). A session ends when its time is up; "caught up" is not the aim. This settles review-08 of the 2026-11-08 stress test (drafts no longer rank above due cards with R = 0). Design: `docs/design/daily-session.md`. Amends D65.2. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the session no longer ends itself; the mole says the goal is met, D246.)

## iPhone navigation (2026-11-08, Tyler picked 1A, 2A and 3A)
- D145. **The iPhone is a drawer, one search sheet, and rows with swipes** (Obsidian mobile, Linear). Amends D78.
  1. **Drawer, no tab bar.** At Today or Sources, nothing is behind you, so the left edge (or the icon at the top left) opens the drawer: Today, Sources, the skills by collection. Inside a source or a skill the left edge still goes back, so swiping right always moves outward: source, list, drawer.
  2. **One sheet for search and commands.** Pull down at the top of a list or tap Search: the Mac's ⌘K, as a full sheet with its field at the bottom near the thumb. Sources by title and text, skills, Start review; no Ask row, since AI runs on the Mac (D78.3). Text search rewrites the core's FTS5 statements for sql.js's FTS4, without bm25's ranking, so on the phone text hits come last touched first.
  3. **Recency only where it can't mislead.** With no query, Recent is the last 8 sources opened or added, Done included. While typing it only breaks ties, on the Mac too: a better match always wins, so results don't shift week to week and old sources stay one good match away. No separate "recently added": `touched` already means added or opened.
  4. **Rows.** 44 pt with small thumbnails (Linear). Swipe left archives, swipe right moves between To read and Done, both with Undo (Mail); long-press is the Mac's row menu, less what needs a Finder or keyboard. It opens after 500 ms, as iOS's own menus do (Tyler, 2026-11-08, over the menu library's 700 ms): a timer of Ore's own opens the menu where the finger is and stands the library's down.
  5. **Archive.** In the drawer: a source swiped left lands there, and swiping it left again restores it, with Undo.
  6. **Not yet.** Feed on the phone. Adding sources waits for sync (Tyler, 5C): what the phone adds would never reach the Mac until then (D78.6).
- D146. **A wait's mark says what the AI is doing** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick 1 of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-more-components.md`, "do all 10"; after Rare UI's Matrix orb and Inline AI status, assistant-ui's dot matrix; amends D113's working line). The 8px four-cell spinner becomes a 5×5 dot matrix (2px cells a pixel apart, 14px) with a hard-framed pattern per stage: `read` (a line passes down the rows) while it reads his sources or his recall, `search` (a radar arm sweeps from the centre) while it searches the web or finds sources, `write` (cells fill the middle rows like typed text) while it writes prequestions or an explanation, `check` (a pixel check draws itself, then holds) while it grades, and `work` (two cells chase round the edge) for the rest (adding, opening, downloading, toasts). The tutor's wait reads "Reading your sources…" instead of "Thinking…", switches to the search pattern the moment a web search starts, and to "Writing…" once it ends (D137, D138; Claude's stream reports both, Codex's neither), so he knows where the answer will come from before it lands. Never the check pattern where a check means he got it (the quiz badge). Still on the stage's telling frame under reduced motion. One mark for every wait, so a new wait picks a stage, never a new spinner.
- D147. **No idea comes back as two cards; a card asks one thing** (2026-11-08, from the new question-quality eval, `docs/guides/question-eval.md`; refines D118 and D19). At a finish, the plan writes each prequestion's review prompt first, and a highlight (key point or plain) whose idea that prompt asks goes in the prompt's `also` instead of getting a card of its own; when an earlier card already asks a prequestion's idea, the prompt asks him to apply it to a new case. Every question asks one thing (no second question joined by "and"), every rubric point is asked for by its question, and answers keep the source's hedges (`enrich@12`). A mistyped id in `also` is dropped rather than failing the run. Why: the eval found the same idea asked twice in most finished sources (5 to 7 a run of 34 cards), and two-part questions with rubric points they never asked; each repeat is months of review time spent on one idea, and a question with two targets has no clean answer to grade (Matuschak 2020: focused, precise prompts).
- D149. **A card's reviews show on a line before he rates it** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick 2 of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-more-components.md`, "do all 10"; after assistant-ui's timeline). At the check step, above the four ratings, a hairline carries a dot per past review in its rating's colour (Again red, Hard orange, Good green, Easy blue), the gaps between them to scale, and a hollow dot where the rating under the pointer or focus (else the suggested one, else Good) would put the next review; the line runs from the first review to Easy's date, so the hollow dot moves along it as he weighs a rating. Hovering a dot names it ("Good · Sep 9", "Next in 5 weeks if Good · Dec 15"). A card never reviewed shows nothing. The dates come from the review log (`ore card show --review` returns `history`: the reviews and the four next dates), nothing new is stored. Why: learners distrust spacing because massed practice feels better (Kornell & Bjork 2008); seeing this card's gaps widen while recall holds, or a card that keeps dropping back, shows him the method on his own card, and what each rating does before he gives it. It is about this card, never a score or a streak (D23). Nothing animates: he sees it on every card.
- D150. **A step track over Show me's steps** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick 3 of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-more-components.md`, "do all 10"; after Rare UI's step player, Dice UI's stepper; extends D142). Above a worked answer's steps, one mark per step on the 2px grid: the steps he has shown are 12px marks, the current one a 24px bar, the ones still hidden 4px dots; the group is named "Step 2 of 5" for VoiceOver. A shown mark scrolls back to its step inside the log (never the page under a floating tutor). The bar grows in one 150ms ease-out when he asks for the next step; still under reduced motion. Why: knowing there are two moves left, not five, tells him how big his own next try is, which is the point of showing the steps one at a time (D142). No play button: he reveals each step himself.
- D151. **Reading page and font are picked from previews** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick 5 of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-more-components.md`, "do all 10"; after Rare UI's theme selector; amends D26's Dark / Light segmented control and D135 for these two rows). Settings › Reading shows the page as two small cards of the page itself (dark, light, three text lines on each) and the text's face as two cards setting "Aa" in it: Charter, the book serif, or Geist, the interface sans (the serif reading option he asked for, 2026-11-08). The picked card is ringed; it is a radio group, so arrows move and pick, and the palette offers "Reading font: Geist" as it offers the page. Stored per viewer like width, size and page (localStorage); the reader's text and title take the face. Why: he picks what reads best by seeing it, not by guessing from a word.
- D152. **The phone shows where he is as a pill that opens the sections** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick 4 of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-more-components.md`, "do all 10"; after Rare UI's scroll progress; the phone's answer to D45's rail). On the iPhone the reader has no room for the outline rail, so a 28px pill sits at the foot of the page, above the home indicator (Books puts its place there): a ring that fills as he reads and the name of the section he is in, crossfading when it changes. A tap opens the sections as a bottom sheet (the iOS way, as What to review opens); a section jumps there and ⌘['s history keeps where he was; swipe down, a tap outside or Esc closes it. A source with fewer than two headings shows the ring alone. Why: in a long chapter on the phone he can't see where he is or go back to reread the section a question came from; the rail does this on the Mac. A narrow Mac window keeps the rail for now.
- D153. **Finishing a source sets off one burst of pixels** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick 10 of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-more-components.md`, "do all 10"; after Magic UI's confetti, drawn as squares; priority 3, delight). When the finished badge appears, 14 4px squares fly out from its edge in the colour of the source's first coloured skill (green when none), in six hard frames over 600ms once the badge has popped, once; none under reduced motion. It marks this source done, never a count or a streak (D23), and nothing else on the screen changes.
- D154. **A skill's next due questions fan out from its tile on Home** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick 8 of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-more-components.md`, "do all 10"; after Rare UI's folder). Hovering or focusing a skill tile with cards due fans out, under it, its next three due questions in queue order (D22, D24; `ore due --skill --limit 3`, read once per visit), each a row that starts review on that skill. A skill with nothing due fans nothing, and the phone (no hover) has none. The rows come out from under the tile one after another in 160ms; still under reduced motion. Why: a glance at what is due tempts a short session more than a number does; it shows the questions and only the questions (the purpose rule), never a count.
- D155. **The phone's ⋯ menu is one sheet that steps through** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, pick 9 of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-more-components.md`, "do all 10"; after Cult UI's family drawer and Apple's action sheets; amends the phone part of the ⋯ menu convention). On the iPhone a ⋯ button opens a bottom sheet of 44pt rows instead of a dropdown, and a submenu (a source's Skills) swaps into the same sheet behind a back row, the sheet's height easing to fit, never a menu over a menu. A pick closes the sheet and runs; a tick that keeps the menu open (`keep`) stays; swipe down, a tap outside or Esc closes it, Esc from a submenu steps back first. Shortcuts stay off it (the phone has no keyboard). Add is not on the phone yet (waits on sync), so the sheet starts with a source's menu and takes Add when it lands. Built as `ActionSheet` in `apps/desktop/src/Menu.tsx`.

## Applying it for real (2026-11-08, Tyler: start small with B)
- D148. **A try comes back: "Next time I'll…", one try at the end of a session, and a record of use** (Tyler, 2026-11-08, from `docs/design/learning-system.md`: the apply-for-real loop is the gap that would reshape rewards, so it comes before tuning them; "start small with B"; amends D127.2 and D127.4). A try was one rep: checked off, written up, gone. Now the loop closes, plan, do, reflect, adjust, again (ch7–8 of the learning-science guidebooks; Di Stefano 2015).
- D156. **A source on the iPhone keeps Back and ⋯ on top and what he does while reading at the bottom** (Tyler picked 4A, after Obsidian mobile and Books; amends D78). A floating bar holds Collected, Note and find in the source; every control near the thumb, one tap each. Both bars slide away as he scrolls down and come back at either end of the source; between, a single tap on the text brings them back or sends them away, as in Books (amended 2026-11-18, Tyler: they came back on every scroll up). A scroll back up to reread leaves them away. A tap on a link, a picture, a timestamp or a highlight keeps its own job, a double tap still highlights its paragraph (D224), and a tap that closes the popup or a selection only closes it. Finish sits at the end of the source and in ⋯, so it stops competing with reading.
  1. **Next time.** "How did it go?" has a second line, "Next time I'll…". Filled, it is the next try's plan: the try goes back to To try with that line as its note, and the round he just finished (his plan and how it went) moves into its record (`uses`). One highlight per idea, so the passage is painted once and the record is one line per idea; the round's reflection still drafts its card (D127.3, one card per idea). `ore tried --next`; `--undo` steps one change back (a re-plan comes back as the tried round it was).
  2. **One try after review.** The end screen shows the oldest open try as one line (his plan, or the passage, and its source) under the session's numbers: the one thing left to do away from the screen now that the session is over. A click opens its passage. Nowhere else, no count, no reminder, nothing owed (D23). Tyler's pick over Home.
  3. **Used.** The skill page's Tried fold is Used: each idea he has tried, with every round's plan and how it went under its passage; an idea planned again is in both lists (its next plan in To try, its rounds in Used). No number anywhere, nothing in gems (Tyler: a record of use, "start small"). Whether use should grow gems, or the mining metaphor should widen to extract, plan, try, reflect, repeat (an idea used crystallising its gem, or forging a tool), waits for real tries.
  4. **Not in v1.** Tutor feedback on a reflection, reminders, counting.
- D157. **Pixel icons only: no emoji, no smooth glyph anywhere** (Tyler, 2026-11-09, "keep ONLY the pixel icons + colours, as clean as possible"; amends D36 and D105/D129's icon rule). A skill's icon is `mc:<name>` from a curated set (`apps/desktop/src/skill-icon-set.ts`, ~210 subjects a skill is about) drawn on the UI icons' 16 grid in the skill's colour; the picker is that set with search, recents and random, no Emoji tab. The redraw (`scripts/pixel-skill-icons.ts`) erodes the glyph before sampling so a coin's digit or a target's rings stay open, draws thin shapes plain, and every icon was checked at 4× before joining; one that can't be made to read is left out, not shipped muddy. Emoji leave the app: the picker's tab, the notes editor's `:` menu and their data (frimousse, emojibase); an emoji typed in a note is his own text and stays. Core accepts only `mc:` (D36's `ph:` and emoji values draw nothing). A new subject is added to the set, redrawn and looked at zoomed, never dropped in from another icon library.

## Practice judged against a committed card (2026-11-09, Tyler: "lets do it. 1 and 2", after a review of LearnHarness's practice doctrine)
- D158. **A Practice exercise is written as the card it becomes, before he answers; a miss offers that card; two kinds never name their idea** (amends D128.6; prompt `tutor@9`). When the tutor sets an exercise it also commits, unseen, the review card the exercise would be: a standalone question (holding the cases in full), the model answer from the passages, and 2 to 4 rubric points. The app carries that card with the turn, and when his answer comes the tutor marks each point covered or not (`covered`) and judges against the card, not against an answer it makes up after reading his. Code offers the committed card with + Card when a point was missed, never one the model wrote after his answer; nothing in the feedback changes (what holds, what's missing, one follow-up, no score, D21). Why: grading against a key written before the answer is what keeps feedback consistent and un-lenient (D56 does the same for prequestions), and a missed exercise now comes back on FSRS in a new form (D66) instead of being gone when the chat closes. Two kinds join the list: **tell apart** (two short cases that look alike but call for ideas he could confuse, or the same idea once and not the other time; he says which fits each and what decides it; case comparison, Alfieri, Nokes-Malach & Schunn 2013) and the rule that a new case and a tell apart never name the idea they test, so the case itself has to bring it to mind (D24 serves confusables adjacent, but nothing asked him to tell them apart). Not through card variants: D66.2 keeps a variant to its own card's idea. The eval (`bun run eval:tutor`) checks the card after a miss and, by judge, that the two kinds don't name their idea.
- D160. **The tutor opens from a pixel owl at the bottom right** (Tyler, 2026-11-09, "move the AI bubble back to the bottom right, like Notion's", pick B of three owl sketches; amends D136, which moved the ✦ to the title bar). A 52px round button 16px in from the bottom right corner, where the floating tutor opens, as Notion AI's is. Inside is an owl drawn for Ore: a 16-grid sprite of round dots like the UI icons, in four tones for depth (darker wings, a light face disc, bright pupils). It blinks every 5 to 9 s and every 15 to 20 s turns its head a column to one side and back, in 8-bit steps that never leave the grid. On hover or focus it faces him and blinks. It is still under reduced motion and while the window is hidden. Why: the tutor is always one click away in the same corner it opens into, and the owl gives it a face without a word of chrome. D136's reason (the bubble covered the panel's foot and Home's skill column) is met by `--owl-room`: Home, skill columns, lists and the source note keep 84px at their foot so their last line scrolls clear.
