The owl hides while the tutor or a quiz panel (which holds that corner) is open. Not Notion's character, and no other mascot appears anywhere. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the owl became a mole, D168, D169, and an old miner, D238.)
- D159. **A reply that lands while the tutor is closed comes out of the owl** (Tyler, 2026-11-09, "test using Cult UI to enhance the UI/UX": the trial pick of `docs/reviews/2026-11-09-cult-ui.md`, after Cult UI's speech bubble and Intercom's message preview; builds on D160). A tutor reply takes about 10 seconds (D138), and he can close the tutor while it writes to keep reading. When that reply (or its error) lands with the tutor closed, a raised bubble left of the owl shows its first three lines as plain text, its tail a stair of 3px steps pointing at the owl, which faces him. A click on the bubble (or the owl, or ⌘J) opens the tutor at the reply and the bubble goes; its × on hover waves it off, the reply still in the thread. A reply that lands while the tutor is open says nothing later; New chat or Try again takes the bubble away. It grows out of the owl once in 180ms; none under reduced motion. VoiceOver hears "The tutor answered: …". Why: he can look away during the wait without checking back, and the answer's first words meet him where he is, so he returns to the question while it is still in mind. No count, no badge, no sound.

## Components fit to how Ore teaches (2026-11-09, Tyler: "1-4 now", then "5 6 and 7 as well", picks from `docs/reviews/2026-11-09-learning-components.md`)
- D161. **A suggested card shows what it changes** (pick 4, after drift-ui's diff viewer, MIT). In Review's Ask about this card, a proposed rewrite (D61) shows as a word-level change to the card he has: words it drops struck through and muted, words it adds underlined, the rest as it was; Use this card is unchanged, and the change stays shown after it (it is kept against the card as it stood when the proposal came). Why: a rewrite should ask the same thing more clearly, and one that asks something else no longer fits the card's review history; seeing only the changed words shows which it is at a glance, where two whole cards take a careful read. A small LCS word diff written for Ore (`apps/desktop/src/worddiff.ts`), no dependency.
- D162. **Practice feedback marks each point of its card, with the words of his that earned it** (pick 3, after assistant-ui's confidence marker, as D141 did for Review; prompt `tutor@10`). When his message answers an open exercise, the tutor returns, beside `covered` (D158), `evidence`: per point the shortest stretch of his message that states it, word for word. Core keeps a quote only for a covered point and only when it is in what he wrote (`quoteIn`, the grader's fold of case, curly quotes and spacing), and returns `marks` (point, covered, quote). Under the feedback the committed card's points show with Review's marks, read only (there is no rating to change): a tick for one he covered, a dot for one he didn't; hovering or focusing a covered point underlines its words in his message, and VoiceOver hears them in the point's label. Why: which parts of the idea he has and which he doesn't, tied to what he wrote, is the most useful feedback there is (informational feedback, Deci, Koestner & Ryan 1999), and Practice and Review now check him the same way. Nothing else in the feedback changes; + Card after a miss is still the committed card.
- D163. **A tell apart shows its two cases side by side, each with its own answer line** (pick 2, after assistant-ui's comparison card; amends D158's reply for a tell apart; prompt `tutor@10`). A tell apart's exercise carries `cases`, the two cases in full (exactly two, or the reply goes back to fix), and the reply gives only the task. The app sets them in two columns, A and B, each a label over a rule (never framed), stacking when the tutor is too narrow for two; under each, while it is the open exercise, a line for which idea fits and what decides it. Enter in A goes to B, Enter in B sends both as one message ("A: …", "B: …"), ⇧Enter is a new line; the chat field and Write it in your note still take an answer written any other way. Once he has answered, the cases stay without their lines. Why: comparing two cases teaches what tells them apart when they are seen together, not read one after the other (Gentner, Loewenstein & Thompson 2003; Alfieri, Nokes-Malach & Schunn 2013), and a line per case makes him commit to each before the feedback. The card keeps the cases in full in its question (D158), so nothing about review changes.
- D164. **Words selected in a tutor reply can go into a card or the source note** (pick 7, after assistant-ui's quote reply and Dice UI's selection toolbar; round 1's 4). Selecting words inside one reply shows a small toolbar above them (below when the log's top would clip it), as the reader's highlight popup sits: **Card** sends "Make a card from this: “…”", and the tutor replies with that card behind + Card (prompt `tutor@10` says so); **Into note** quotes the words (`> …`) under "From the tutor" in the note of the source the reply cites first, else the one it was asked about (absent when there is neither), with an Undo toast that puts the note back as it was and keeps anything written since (`--set` with `--base`); an open note's editor saves its draft first, then shows the file. Why: the line worth keeping is usually one sentence of a reply, and + Card took the whole reply's idea or nothing.
- D165. **Calibration shows all three confidence taps** (pick 5; amends D69). D69's line on the end screen becomes three pixel bars under "When you say": Guess, Think so and Sure, each ten square cells filled to how often he was right after that tap, with the percent beside it; the hover hint gives the counts ("Right 6 of 12 Explain answers in the last 12 weeks"). Same answers, same window, same weekly place as D69, and the block still waits for 20 Sure answers; Guess and Think so each show once they have 10 (`MIN_LEVEL_N`). Why: the Sure line could say he is overconfident but not whether his taps mean anything: if Guess and Sure are right as often as each other, the tap carries no information, and that is the thing to fix (resolution, not only bias). `ore session end --json` has `calibration.levels`. (Amended 2026-11-13: the cells are round dots, Tyler's pick 2026-11-13, F82, D184.)
- D166. **A figure's parts become cards** (pick 1, image occlusion; after Mischief's redaction and bounding boxes, MIT; the idea from lmscn's hotspot and Anki's Image Occlusion). In figure zoom (D143), a drag over part of the figure draws a box and a small field asks what's under it; Enter makes a Fact card (D59.2: no typing in review) whose question is "What’s under the box?", whose answer is what he typed, and whose frontmatter keeps `figure: { src, box }`: the image as the source's doc.md names it and the box as fractions of its width and height. One box, one card (one idea per card, D147); the figure's cards show as boxes when it is open again, a click outside a box drops it, Esc drops it then closes, and Undo retires the card. In review the figure shows under the question with that box covered and the rest visible; the confidence key reveals it, outlined, with the answer. Core refuses a figure the source doesn't show, a box outside it or too small, and an empty or multi-line answer (`ore card figure <source> --src --box x,y,w,h --answer`). A figure that no longer loads leaves the card's text. Why: a labelled diagram, map, chart or anatomy plate couldn't be learned with Ore at all, though textbooks and slides are in the core promise (D122) and those are where the figures are; retrieval works on pictures as on words (Carpenter & Pashler 2007). Left out until asked for: typing the label in review, and "hide all, guess one".
- D167. **A recording plays in place** (pick 6; after Mischief's audio player, transcript viewer and scrub bar, MIT; round 1's 10). An audio file, audio link or podcast episode now keeps its audio as the original (`source.<ext>`, as D122 promised) with its timed transcript beside it (`transcript.vtt`), instead of keeping only the transcript. In the reader it plays as a video does (D48 to D50), on the Mac's own audio element: the same pinned scrubber and controls with no frame, the transcript lit as it is spoken and followed, every `[mm:ss]` stamp seeking it, Space and the video keys driving it, the place kept per source and the speed shared with videos. His highlights show as ticks on the scrubber where they are spoken, on a video's scrubber too, so he can go back to the moments he marked. Reimport hears the kept audio again, so a better transcriber reaches it. A recording added before this kept only its transcript and reads as before; a video file still keeps only its transcript (it can be gigabytes). While it is open it answers the system too (Tyler, 2026-11-21, round 8 pick 4; `now-playing.ts`, the Media Session API): the keyboard's play key, AirPods and Control Center play and pause it, skip back 15 and forward 30 (the track keys skip too, as in Podcasts) and show its title, author and picture. It still stops when he leaves the source. Left out: a waveform (the transcript already shows where the speech is).
- D168. **The tutor's button is a mole, not an owl** (Tyler, 2026-11-09, "the owl doesn't match the mining analogy, let's make it a cute mole, same style"; amends D160's character only). The mascot is a mole poking out of its burrow, with its claws on the rim and a miner's helmet with a small lamp. It is drawn as the owl was: a 16-grid sprite of round dots in four tones (fur, a darker helmet and burrow, a light snout and paws, and bright eyes, nose and lamp; the eyes are round 2×2 blocks, since one-dot eyes looked creepy, Tyler 2026-11-09). Everything else in D160 stands: the bottom right corner, the blink and the stepped glance, facing him on hover, stillness under reduced motion, and the room kept at page feet; the reply that lands while the tutor is closed (D159) now comes out of the mole. Why: Ore is mining (ore, gems, the rail and cart), and a mole is the animal that digs, so the one character in the app now belongs to that world.
- D169. **The mole is redrawn from Tyler's picked concept, and it acts out the tutor's state** (Tyler, 2026-11-09; amends D168's drawing). After ten rounds of critique on the old sprite stalled, Tyler had ChatGPT draw six dot moles and picked one as the base and two as example states ("1 being baseline, 2 and 5 being example animation states"). The mole is now its head and two white paws over a row of dirt, no helmet, drawn as round dots of one size on a 22×18 grid with an Animal Crossing face: a lighter muzzle, black eyes with one glint, a pink nose (an exception to the colour rule, the mascot's one colour). Each pose comes from one function (`moleGrid`), so a mood is a timeline of poses and every move shifts whole dots. On the tutor button: it comes out of its hole once a launch, rests with D160's blink and glance, thinks (looks up, taps a paw) while a reply is written with the tutor closed, and hops when it lands (D159). At the end of a Review session it cheers once from the top of the ring (Tyler's pick of four placements; nothing at the start of a session, so the first card has his focus). Why: the old 16-grid sprite read as a skull, a robot or a ghost to every reviewer; Tyler's pick reads as a mole at 40px, and building the states as poses of one drawing means a later change to the face carries into every move.

## Icon sizes on a Retina screen (2026-11-09, Tyler: "a pass to refine the style of our icons and fix any errors, including how they're rendered")
- D170. **Pixel icons draw at 24px, each dot on whole device pixels** (amends the 20px of the dot icons (signature-style pick E, #114) and D157). A 16-grid sprite at 20px gave each dot 2.5 device pixels on his 2× screen, so alternate columns fell on different sub-pixel phases: every other column drew dimmer and up close the dots blurred into a plaid. At 24px a dot gets 3 device pixels and at 40px 5, so every dot draws the same. The dot's radius drops from 0.44 to 0.38 of a cell, which keeps each icon's ink about what it was at 20px and leaves the gap between dots visible. One `dots()` (`apps/desktop/src/dots.ts`) draws the UI icons, the section and highlight icons and the skill icons; a test holds every sprite size to whole device pixels at 2×. 16px in a chip and a menu row stays (2 device pixels a dot). The same pass fixed single stray or missing dots and off-centre sprites, with tests that hold symmetric subjects mirror-symmetric and every UI sprite centred on its grid. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: menu rows draw their icons at 20px, D231.)
- D171. **Icons go back to 20px, centred by their ink and on their label's capitals** (Tyler, 2026-11-09, on D170's 24px in the sidebar: "is the relative sizing of the text to the icons off, as well as alignment?"; amends D170's size). At 24px an icon's ink stood nearly twice a 13px label's capitals, and the sprite overflowed its 20px slot downward, so it sat about 3px below the label's middle. Back at 20px the ink stands about one and a half times the capitals, as Linear's do; D170's 0.38 dot stays, which keeps the gaps even at 20px. `dots()` now centres every drawing by its ink in whole cells, so an icon whose drawing leaves more room on one side (the terminal prompt, the sparkle) no longer sits off the line, and the sidebar's icon column sits 1px up so each icon's middle meets its label's capitals. The section head's + is redrawn 10 dots wide, to sit beside the ··· and the caret instead of over them.

## Tutor chat history (2026-11-09, Tyler: "we will want to add chat history to the AI part, right, because I want to make a new chat or go back to old chats? Notion does this well")
- D172. **Every tutor chat is saved, and the chat's name opens the list of old ones** (amends D128's one conversation, which + set aside behind a ten-second Undo and a quit lost; after Notion AI's chat switcher). Each chat is one file in the library, `chats/<id>.json` (its id is its first turn's), written through `ore chat` after each message and each reply, so a restart loses nothing and the library's history holds it. A reply that lands after he has moved to another chat goes into its own chat's file. The tutor's first reply also returns a short title (`tutor@12`, no extra call), so the list reads by topic and not as ten chats called "Practice"; until it lands the title is his first message. The header's left side shows the chat's name with a caret (an empty one reads "New chat"); a click drops down the list: a Find field, then the chats under Today, Yesterday, Past 7 days and Older, newest first, the open one ticked. One click (or arrows and Enter) opens a chat where he left it, with its @ context (D128.3); a turn that was cut off by a quit says so and offers Try again. The bin on a row or its right-click menu deletes it, with Undo (no shortcut: ⌘⌫ is No idea in Review and clears a line in Find). + starts a new chat with nothing to undo, and a relaunch starts a new chat (Notion, ChatGPT). Left out for the purpose rule: renaming, pinning, folders, sharing, counts, a time on each row (the day headings say when) and finding words inside messages. Why: the tutor is where he works an idea through, and an explanation he got last week is worth finding again rather than asking for twice.

## Skill importance and how skills relate (2026-11-09, Tyler: "Curious if we should add skill importance", picked "As recommended")
- D173. **A skill's importance is shown and set, a new skill starts Light, and a skill can be archived** (amends D22's labels, D25's "creates it (P2)", D36, D58, D80 and D118's cap). Every skill already had a priority (P1–P3, D22) that set its cards' retention and their weight in the queue, but the app never showed it and every new skill was P2. His case: a few articles and podcasts on go-to-market for a role he may not want, read lightly, raised if it sticks, out of sight if it doesn't.
  1. **Three levels, Linear's bars.** P1 **Focus**, P2 **Normal**, P3 **Light**, drawn as three pixel bars (lit up to the level, dotted above it, `ImportanceFocus/Normal/Light` in `icons.tsx`). Set from the bars after the name on the skill page, **Importance ▸** on a skill's right-click and ⋯, and ⌘K ("Importance of X: Focus"); each change is on the history (⌘Z). `ore skills set <skill> --priority P1|P2|P3`. The math is unchanged: retention 0.92/0.88/0.80 and queue weight 3/2/1 (D22, D65).
  2. **New skills start Light** (`ore skills add`, the sidebar's +, the picker's Create, an accepted suggestion). He raises the ones that matter; one he forgets to raise still keeps its cards at 80%.
  3. **Light keeps fewer cards.** A ts-fsrs simulation (FSRS-6 defaults, a year, 2,000 cards) gave 11.3 reviews a card at 0.92, 8.6 at 0.88, 7.6 at 0.80, 7.2 at 0.75 and 6.3 at 0.70: lowering retention saves about a third and then little more, so the number of cards is the lever. A source filed only under Light skills drafts at most 2 cards from plain highlights instead of 5 (`LIGHT_PLAN_CAP`, enrich@13); key points and its prequestions still get theirs.
  4. **Archive.** `archived: true` in `skills.yaml` (`ore skills archive <skill> [--undo]`; Archive skill on its menus and ⌘K, with Undo). An archived skill folds into an **Archived** group at the bottom of the sidebar (closed until opened; a collection whose skills are all archived hides), and leaves Home's tiles and Continue reading, What to review and the phone's drawer. Cards filed only under archived skills leave the due queue (`ore due --skill` still reviews them); a card that also has a live skill stays, at that skill's priority. Enrich never files under an archived skill; when it suggests one by name, the dashed chip's accept brings it back. Nothing is deleted, and neither FSRS state nor a card's retention changes (retention reads every skill, archived or not), so the cards come back on the same schedule. Its page shows **Bring back** where the bars sit; filing a source under it (the picker, an add, a snippet, a course, a suggestion accepted; not a Feed preview) brings it back at its level, in its place, and that filing's Undo archives it again.
  5. **The sidebar keeps his drag order**, not sorted by importance, so a skill stays where his hand expects it; only Archive changes what is listed. What to review lists Focus skills first, then Normal, then Light, most due first in each, all still ticked.
  6. **Not doing:** auto-archiving a skill left alone (hiding what he made, unasked), and a fourth "read only, no cards" level (Light plus his own key points covers it).
- D174. **Skills relate through what they share, with no structure he keeps** (Tyler asked for no hand-kept graph and simple). A source and its cards can be filed under several skills, and the AI's topics (`topics/*.md`, `skills: […]`, D25) span skills; a card takes the highest priority of its live skills (D22, D173), so a go-to-market card also filed under Creator Business gets that weight with no link to maintain. The rule that keeps the list short: **a skill is something he would set a goal for (D102); anything narrower is a topic** the AI makes and merges (HubSpot and Salesforce are topics, not sub-skills of CRMs). Collections (D36) stay optional. Not doing: a graph view (it shows links but serves no learning step, purpose rule) and a hand-kept hierarchy (parts, option C of `design/skill-goals-and-parts.md`, still deferred by D102: its stress test counted about 12 new rules over 6 screens). If he later wants a skill's parts, its AI topics are the zero-upkeep version.

## Diagrams open in a centred sheet (2026-11-09, Tyler: "can we have diagrams open in a centered popover? … basically the same aspect ratio as the screen or as the main window, but maybe 15% smaller on each side. It can optionally go full screen because I feel like that helps me as a user orient myself in the app better")
- D175. **A diagram opens in a sheet centred over the window** (amends D40's overlay and its 2026-11-06 note, which opened it beside the source in the reader and full window elsewhere; after Notion's centre peek and Ore's own Settings sheet). The sheet is the window's shape with 15% of the window showing on every side under Settings' 50% black scrim, the page colour inside, 12px corners and `--float`; its head holds the name, ⤢ (⇧⌘↵) for the full window and back, and Done. A press on the scrim, Esc or Done saves and closes. Every open starts in the sheet; the full window is for that drawing only, not remembered. The phone keeps the full window. The reader's beside-the-source mode goes, so a diagram has one place it opens (Tyler's pick on the card, over keeping it). Why: a full-window editor hid where he was in the app; the sheet keeps the app in view around the drawing, with the full window one key away when a drawing needs the room.

## The shape of a session (2026-11-10, Tyler: "2, 3 and 4 together … proceed", picks from the next-work shortlist)
- D176. **A session opens on a likely recall, his own cards come in first, and the log can answer the open science questions** (amends D60's "freshest source first" and D22's order of the first card only; no FSRS math changes).
  1. **The first card is a likely recall.** Most urgent first put the hardest cards of a backlog back to back at the start, and hard tasks in a run raise quitting (Kang, Daniels & Schweitzer 2022, `reviews/2026-11-09-pubmed-check.md`). The likeliest recall among the first ten due cards moves to the front (`winFirst` in `queue.ts`) when a session starts (`ore due --start`; a fetch mid-session and the quick quiz, which asks the card that is next, keep the order); the rest keep their order, and a card served in a block (confusables, one source's definitions, D24) stays in it.
  2. **New cards from his own work come first**: a card drafted from his notes, then one from his highlight, key point or answered question, then the AI's prequestion cards; the freshest finished source first within each. Generating beats reading (Bertsch 2007) and self-made cards beat premade (`research/brief-learning-science.md` §4); a prequestion was already retrieved once at the finish. Under a backlog only a few new cards get in each day (D144), so which ones matters (`reviews/2026-11-02-science-pass.md`, rec 2).
  3. **A review keeps who suggested its AI rating** (`ai`: vendor, model, prompt version, as all AI output must), and `ore stats --json` gains two numbers the app never shows: `ai_agreement` (how often his rating is the AI's, the watch on D21) and `explain` (pass rates of typed and in-head answers on cards old enough for either, the watch on D68, whose evidence the PubMed check called weak). Why now: his library has one review; two weeks of real use should answer these, not need a rebuild first (science pass, rec 1 and 5).
- D177. **A session doesn't end on a miss, and the end screen names the longest gap he recalled across** (`design/daily-session.md` idea 3, `design/learning-system.md` stage 8).
  1. **One more card when the goal closes on a miss.** The likeliest recall of the next ten (60% or better, never the card just missed or a new one) is served first, and whatever he answers, the session then ends. With none in reach it ends as before. Why: the last moment colours the whole session (peak-end, Kahneman 1993), and a hard task that comes last raises quitting (Kang 2022). Order only (`lastWin` in `apps/desktop/src/ore.ts`); the missed card still owes its relearn pass (D21). (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the session no longer ends itself, D246.)
  2. **"You recalled one you last saw 23 days ago"** on the end screen, when the longest gap among cards he passed is a week or more (`longest_days` on the session's end receipt). Purpose: spacing's gain is delayed and invisible in the moment, so the session shows it once, as a fact about his memory, never a score (D62).

- D178. **⌘K follows what is open, never what is selected** (Tyler, 2026-11-10; amends the palette's "actions on the open or selected object"). With a source or a skill page open, ⌘K lists its actions. A row selected in a list (a source, a feed post, a course, several sources) adds nothing: its actions are on its ⋯, its right-click and its keys (A archives, R renames). Selecting with the arrow keys is not something he does on the way to ⌘K, and a palette that changes with the highlight is not natural to him. Note blocks stay out of ⌘K too, as in Notion. An action that must be one click (marking a try tried) gets a control on the row, never only a menu. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: list actions take ⌘ keys: ⇧⌘A archives, ⌥⌘R renames, D232.)
- D179. **The prequestions stay in reach while he reads: a "Reading for N" chip in the reader's top bar** (Tyler picked B of two mocks, 2026-11-10; adds to D18). Prequestions help when he looks for their answers while reading (St. Hilaire & Carpenter 2020, `reviews/2026-11-09-pubmed-check.md`), and the block sat at the top of the source, out of sight after the first screen. The chip shows once he has started the source (status reading, or a highlight made) and until he finishes it; hovering or focusing it lists the questions with his guesses (Radix HoverCard, as the skill fan D154); a click, Enter or ⌘K "Show prequestions" goes to the block and opens it. No count of answered ones and no progress: it is a reminder of what to look for, never a score (D23). On the iPhone (Tyler, 2026-11-15, "Add it"): no hover there, so the chip is a ? in the source's bottom bar, before Find, and a tap lists the questions with his guesses in a bottom sheet, as the reading pill lists the sections (D152); a question goes to the block, opened.

## Cards from each day's reading (2026-11-10, Tyler: "1. Approve", from the second next-work shortlist)
- D180. **Plain highlights are planned the day after a sitting, not only at the finish** (amends D118.2; D106's Keep reading still drafts nothing at that moment). Why: his library had 22 sources in reading and one finished, so a week of highlights (13 on one chapter) could never reach review, and a first retrieval the next day is better spacing than one that waits for Done. While a source is `reading`, its plain highlights the plan has not weighed are pending once no highlight on it was made or changed for six hours (`READING_REST_MS`), so a sitting is planned whole; the day's catch-up (D8) then runs the same plan as the finish: grouped into ideas, at most 5 drafts from plain highlights (2 for a Light source), strays skipped, nothing a skill's cards already ask, the weighed ones saved in `planned`. The cap is per plan, so a source read over several days can get up to 5 a day; new drafts still enter review at the fixed share of a session (D144). Prequestions keep waiting for the finish, where their prompts apply an idea a card already asks to a new case (D147). An inbox or archived source is never planned. Prompt `enrich@14` (one line: he has not read the rest). On screen only two hover lines change: a highlight's "Cards are planned from your highlights the next day", and Keep reading's "its questions become cards when you finish".

## "Card" becomes "exercise" (2026-11-10, Tyler: "Would changing from 'card' to 'question' or 'knowledge exercise' or something like that be better? … I don't want 'card' and its notions to limit us again", then "commit to exercise")
- D181. **Exercise** (amends D43's "cards for every review item"). What review brings back is an **exercise**: one thing Ore asks him to do with an idea from memory. He answers, sees how he did against the source, and it comes back later, often in a new form (D66). The app and its docs say exercise wherever they said card: Edit, Retire and Delete exercise, New exercises a day, Remix exercises, + Exercise in the tutor, Answered + exercise, "Exercise made" for a figure's box. The two kinds keep their names, **Fact** and **Explain**, and a ★ highlight stays a **Key point** (D118): the look-up's "Make card", which makes one, now says Key point, and the Collected tab's hover names highlights, key points and questions. File fields and folders, the CLI (`ore card …`), key ids (`edit-card`) and code keep `card`, as under D43, and earlier decisions are not reworded: read their "card" as exercise. `apps/desktop/test/names.test.ts` fails when a string the app shows says card. Why: "card" carries flashcard notions (two sides, one short answer, flip) into design talk and into what the AI is asked to write, and review already asks for more than a card holds: explain, apply to a new case, predict, find the mistake, label a figure, write up a try. Which exercise types the evidence supports, how to write each and what to avoid: `docs/research/brief-exercise-types.md`. Not in this change: the AI prompts still say card. The tutor's own "exercise" is its Practice task, and "Make a card from this:" is the line the app sends it for one, so that line and the tutor's and Ask's own words wait for one prompt change, with the per-type writing rules, checked by the question and tutor evals.

## How an exercise is written (2026-11-10, Tyler: "Yes to your recommended exercise types/process above", after `docs/research/brief-exercise-types.md`)
- D182. **One process for every exercise, rules for each type, and fill in the blank only for exact things** (amends D19's "atomic Q/A or cloze"; refines D66, D118, D147 and D158). Evidence and grades for every line: `docs/research/brief-exercise-types.md`.
  1. **The process.** The AI that writes an exercise works in this order: decide what he should be able to do with the idea (recall it exactly, explain it, use it), and let the type follow; write the answer and its key points from the source before the question; one target; words the source did not use, standing alone. Review already does the other three steps: every attempt is corrected against key points written beforehand (D56, D59), an answer with parts is typed (D59; D68's in-head exception is the one to watch), and an exercise he knows comes back on a new case (D66).
  2. **The types Ore writes.** **Fact** (a term, name, number or single fact; never a list, a yes/no, or a question that holds its answer). **Fill in the blank**, a Fact written as a statement (below). **Explain** (a specific why or how, never "Explain X"). **Apply** (stored as Explain: a setting from his work the source did not use, a decision or product asked for, a key point tied to the setting, answerable from the source's idea alone; a central idea gets a Fact for its definition and an Apply, since use need not wait for the facts). The changed versions of D66 (new case, predict, find the mistake, choose and justify, name it from a case) each carry how to write them and what to avoid. The tutor's Practice kinds (D128, D158), figure labels (D166), prequestions, the finish recall and goal tries stay as they are. Not added: multiple choice (a candidate for the iPhone, where typing is slow) and order the steps (a candidate once procedures and code matter more); each would be mocked first.
  3. **Fill in the blank.** Only when exact wording is the point: a term inside its definition, a number, a name, a command or piece of syntax, one part of a formula. A fresh, complete statement, never the source's sentence, with that one thing written `____`; the answer is what it hides; exactly one answer fits. Never a blank for a reason, a mechanism or a step. Core sends a draft back (`blankProblem`) when it has more than one blank, a blank plus a question, a blank in an Explain exercise, or an answer over five words.
  4. **A blank shows as `____`** (a bug this found). The prompt used to ask for Anki's `{{c1::word}}`, and nothing hid it: review showed the question as written, answer included. Core now keeps and reads any such blank as `____` (`hideBlanks`), VoiceOver reads it as "blank", and a blank exercise still gets no changed versions.
  5. **The prompts say exercise** (finishes D181): `enrich@15`, `tutor@13`, `chat@2`, `grade@4`, `leave-out@2`, `variant@2`, `variant-check@2`. The kind ids stay `card` and `prompt`. The tutor's selection bar sends "Make an exercise from this: …", which the tutor reads as a request to keep one for review, not as Practice.
  6. **Checked by the question eval** (`docs/guides/question-eval.md`): `code:lifted` fails a question that shares eight words in a row with its source (a blank is read with its word back in), and the judge's `blank_fits` fails a blank that hides a reason, an unimportant word, or one several words could replace. First runs (two each, old prompt and new): no change on the five earlier sources (67% and 64% of exercises passing, inside the noise; they draw almost no Fact exercises), and on a new reference page of exact things (Git commands) 36% (5/14) against 75% (9/12), with three lifted questions against one. The tutor eval passed 32 of 32 and the grading eval 20 of 20.
  Why: what he can do afterwards follows what the exercise asked. Practising a fact strengthens that fact and did not lift questions that need the idea used (Agarwal 2019); a different case each time is what builds use (Butler 2017). Fill in the blank helped the blanked item and little else (Hinze & Wiley 2011; Tran et al. 2015), and a lifted sentence can be completed from its shape. Format matters less than what is retrieved and whether he is corrected, so the types Ore has stay and the rules for writing them tighten.

- D183. **One way of drawing: round dots. Gems are halftone, rings are a dot per step, a screen's hero number is a dot face** (Tyler's pick B of four, 2026-11-10; amends D101's square-pixel gems and D129's "Geist Pixel retired", refines D90 and D60). Why: one Home row drew its icon in round dots (D157, D170), its gem in square pixels and its ring in square-ended segments, three ways of drawing within an inch, and he judged that a rule allowing squares beside dots would not hold. Nothing, the reference for the look, keeps dots for what defines character and sets them large; Ore had them only at its smallest size. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: a cut gem's dots fuse into square pixels, the one square allowed, D236.)
  1. **Gems** are halftone: one round dot per cell in the skill's colour, the dot's size the shade, a crack a line of missing dots, an empty slot an outline of small dots. Two drawings: 10×8 below 22px, 14×11 from there up. The states and what they mean are D90's, unchanged.
  2. **Rings** (the goal ring, a skill's level ring) are one dot per step from 12 o'clock: 14, 20 or 30 dots by size. Lit dots are full size, today's would-be dots dimmer, the track one step above the ground. Any progress lights one dot and only a finished ring lights them all, so a ring never looks empty after work or full before the goal. The goal ring still fills when shown, each dot a beat after the one before (none under Reduce Motion).
  3. **The hero number** (today's minutes on Home, a skill's cut gems) is Geist Pixel Circle (OFL) at 40px. A dot face stops reading below about 24px, so row counts and every other number stay in the mono.
  4. **The week's days** on Home are round.
  Tried and not picked: every gem in plain dots (a blur at 16px), flat smooth facets for gems with dot rings, facets with thin smooth rings. Not in this change, still square and next: the skill badge's corners, the Start review button, skill chips, dithered progress bars and Review's rail. Mocks: `/mnt/project-files/house-style/dots-round-1/`. `test/dot-ring.test.tsx` fails if a gem draws a square.

- D184. **The last squares go: bars and Review's track are dots, and corners are soft** (Tyler's pick B of three, 2026-11-10; finishes D183, amends D101's square corners and stepped skill badge and the signature style's pixel rail). Why: after D183 the gems and rings were round dots, but they sat in square-cornered rows beside dithered square bars and a stepped badge, so one screen still showed two styles.
  1. **Bars** (how far he has read on Home and under a list thumbnail, a long Review session) are a row of whole dots, lit by the ring's rule: any progress lights one, only a finished bar lights them all. A dot is never cut.
  2. **Review's track** is Home's week track: a hairline with a round dot per exercise, green behind him, white where he is, the ore cart above it drawn in dots. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: Review's track and its cart are gone; the cart runs only on the end screen, D246.)
  3. **Corners are soft**: rows 8, buttons and fields 6, chips 4, menus, panels and sheets 12, Review's passages and answers 10, the skill badge 10 (its stepped clip is gone, so its keyboard focus is the ordinary ring), the level chip a pill. The burst's particles are round.
  Tried and not picked: dots with square corners kept (round dots in square boxes still read as two styles); full pills for Start review and skill chips with a circular badge (pulls toward a generic app). Mocks: `/mnt/project-files/house-style/squares-round-1/`.

- D185. **One motion system, two moods: "Glide"** (Tyler's pick B of three, 2026-11-10, on a page he could click through; makes law 5 of the house style real). Why: the stylesheets had some 30 durations and 6 curves, each picked on the day, so nothing said how fast a menu should be or where a bounce was allowed. (Amended 2026-11-13: the sidebar cursor keeps D113's one-frame bounce, Tyler's call on F83.)
  1. **Tokens** at the top of `styles.css`: `--t-1` 100ms (press, hover), `--t-2` 150ms (small moves), `--t-3` 200ms (what opens), `--t-4` 300ms (what slides in), `--t-moment` 600ms, `--stagger` 16ms, and three curves, `--ease-out`, `--ease-in-out`, `--ease-spring`.
  2. **Everyday** moves use the four short times and ease-out, never the spring. **Moments** (a ring filling, a gem cut, a tick, a session's end) may use the spring and the moment time. Keyboard-driven actions don't animate.
  3. Every transition and animation of 400ms or less in `styles.css` and `retro.css` moved to its nearest token, and every curve to one of the three (the settings switch lost its spring). Hand-drawn moments over 400ms, stepped pixel frames and loops keep their own time. `apps/desktop/test/motion.test.ts` holds the line.
  Tried and not picked: Crisp (80 to 220ms, no spring anywhere: fast, but celebrations barely register), Bounce (a spring on menus and tabs too: slower every day, and the bounce wears in a daily habit). Not in this change: the few timings set from script (the phone's sheet and drawer drags, the sidebar's drop, Collected's fly-in). Page: `/mnt/project-files/house-style/motion-round-1/index.html`.

- D186. **The mole's chat: speech bubbles, one mole by the field, a blinking block** (Tyler, 2026-11-10: "a bit like a retro video game or terminal, with a blinking cursor, and speech bubble vibes"; his pick C of three, then place 2 of three for the mole). Why: the chat read as a generic assistant panel, a sparkle beside plain text, with nothing saying it is the mole talking.
  1. **Bubbles.** The mole says each reply in a `bg-active` bubble; his messages are outlined. The sparkle is gone.
  2. **One mole**, not one per reply: it peeks over the field's top edge at the left, wonders (looks up, taps a paw) while a reply is written, hops when it lands and jolts on an error. Only the newest bubble has a tail, a stair of 3px steps pointing down at it, so no bubble moves when the next one starts.
  3. **A blinking block** marks where words are about to appear: after the mole's waiting line and its reply as it streams, and at the start of the empty field (his caret there; typing brings the system caret back, since the Mac's web view cannot draw a block one). The waiting line in the chat drops its dot grid for the block.
  Tried and not picked: an all-mono terminal with a prompt (long explanations read worse in mono, no mole), a game dialogue box with the mole's portrait on every reply, the mole beside the newest reply (older bubbles would slide left each time), the mole in the header (too small to see it wonder). Mocks: `/mnt/project-files/house-style/mole-chat-round-1/`, `mole-chat-round-2/`.

- D187. **The tutor's chat has Stop and Copy** (Tyler, 2026-11-10: "there is no way to stop the chat once I send a message", shown against Notion AI). Why: a wrong or mistyped message cost him the full wait, and a reply could only leave the chat by selecting it.
  1. **Stop.** While a reply is written the send button is Stop (a dot-drawn square; ⌘., rebindable). The `ore tutor` run is cancelled. Words already written stay as the reply, marked Stopped; with none the bubble says "Stopped." with Try again. A stop is not a landing, so the mole doesn't hop.
  2. **Copy** beside every reply's bubble (always shown on the newest, on hover for older ones): the reply as Markdown, with a worked answer's steps.
  Checked against ChatGPT, Claude and Notion AI and left out: thumbs up and down (nobody reads them), share, a model picker in the chat (Settings has it), voice. Not decided: editing a sent message and regenerating a good reply (both replace a reply, so they need a way back), attaching a file or image to a message.

- D188. **A drag selects blocks, and a diagram or image is a block of its own** (Tyler, 2026-11-10, from a clip: he dragged down the gutter beside a diagram and nothing was selected, and "Gh" typed by the diagram had become one block with it; reverses the first Notion pass, which left the drag box out). Why: selecting blocks took Esc or a click on the ⋮⋮ handle, both things to know, where Notion takes the obvious drag; and a block that holds words and a diagram has one handle for two things, so neither can be moved, selected or deleted alone.
  1. **A drag from beside the note** (its gutter, up to 40px either side, and any empty space of the note) draws Notion's box and selects every block it passes, by height: the box need not touch the text.
  2. **A drag from inside text** selects words until it is a few pixels into another block, then whole blocks from the one it began in. Text across two blocks is still selected with ⇧ and the arrows.
  3. **An image or diagram stands alone on its line.** Words typed with the caret before one go on a line above it, after one on a line below, and `/diagram` on a line with words puts the diagram under them. A note saved with both on one line (Markdown allows it) is split on its next edit, not on opening, so opening a note never rewrites it. Only lines of text are split: an image in a heading is left.
  Not in this change: the empty line a diagram's block shows above it (where that caret sits), and scrolling a long note while the box is dragged from the gutter (the wheel works). The Mac's web view is not Chromium, where this was driven: `e2e` "note drag to select", `notion-parity.test.ts`.

- D189. **The tutor's chat takes files, and its starters are rows** (Tyler's picks 3 and 4 of four, 2026-11-10, after D187's audit).
  1. **Attach.** A paperclip left of send picks files, and a paste into the field attaches a copied picture or files copied in Finder. An image (png, jpg, gif, webp), a PDF or a text file, 20 MB and four a message at most; anything else is refused with the next step (add it as a source). What is attached shows as a chip beside the mole with ×, then over his message once sent (a picture as a thumbnail). A message needs words as well as a file.
  2. **Where it lives.** `ore chat --attach` copies the file into `chats/files/`, named by its content, so the same file is kept once and a saved chat shows it again. It is for the chat only: not a source, never indexed. Deleting a chat leaves its files (Undo needs them); nothing clears unused ones yet.
  3. **How the tutor reads it.** `ore tutor --files` hands the agent the paths (and those of the last six turns, so a follow-up still has the picture) with its file tools on; `tutor@14` tells it to read them, answer from them, never cite them, and not to mark that as general knowledge. Not measured: `eval:tutor` has no case with a file, and Codex reading a picture this way is untested.
  4. **Starters** are one row each, an icon then the words (Notion AI's list), in place of the pills.
  Not built: dropping a file on the chat (a drop anywhere still adds a source), editing a sent message, regenerating a good reply.
- D190. **A finished session lands with the cart tipping** (Tyler's pick B of three played on the real end screen, 2026-11-10; the first signature moment of the house style, after he called the sketch "really amateur": the idea was right, the motion had to be built properly).
  1. **What happens.** On Review's end screen the ore cart that rode the rail all session rolls in from the left on a rail just over the ring, brakes, and its own momentum tips the bucket. One dot of ore falls out for each ring dot this session lit and becomes that dot where it lands; one rough stone falls for each memory that went solid (the eight shown) and is cut into its gem where it lands. The bucket drops back, the cart rolls off the other side, and the mole comes up and cheers (D169). About two and a half seconds, once. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the rail is gone; the cart runs only on the end screen, D246.)
  2. **Why the cart.** It is the one mining noun already on screen during a session, so the moment finishes a trip he watched (law: mining lives in moments and nouns). The juice follows success and stays as the record: the ring and the gems are today's screen, unchanged once it is over.
  3. **How it moves.** The cart is drawn like the mole, round dots on a grid with shade by dot size; its wheels turn as far as it rolls. Ore flies on a real arc under gravity, never a straight line or an easing curve. Speeds and curves are the motion tokens: the rolls take `t-moment`, in on `ease-out` and off on `ease-in-out`, run to twice the distance so the cart crosses the window's edge at its fastest; only a fall keeps its own time, which is gravity's.
  4. **When it doesn't play.** Nothing to tip (the ring lit no new dot and no gem was cut), the quick quiz, the iPhone, Reduce Motion, a hidden window: the end screen is as it was. Done, ⌘Z and Esc work throughout; leaving stops it.
  Not picked: A Light up (today's screen, tuned), C Mole strike (the mole strikes twice and knocks gems loose). Sketches: https://claude.ai/artifact/7S5D5UJVar3U4TtCuABoJF. Rive was asked about and not used: its editor is hand work and the free tier adds a splash screen.

- D191. **The tutor opened on a skill's page is scoped to that skill, a chip he can remove** (Tyler, 2026-11-10, "yes" to the Codex UX pilot's class B finding; amends D128.1 and D128.3). Why: on a skill page the tutor had no context, no chip and nothing about the skill in the request, where Notion AI takes the open page. The page on screen is the context: the open source as before, else the skill whose page it is, when it has sources (with none there is nothing to search, so no chip and nothing scoped). Its chip sits where the source's does, drawn the same with the skill's icon as the @ list shows it (D128), and × ("Leave this skill out") leaves it out for that page while the tutor is open, through Review or a trip Home and back; another skill page or source has its own chip. The source's × works the same way now, keyed by page (it was one remembered source). Each message sent while it shows goes pointed at that skill through the @ path (`ore tutor --skill=`, D128.3: the library search keeps to the skill's sources), with the message only, as the open source goes, never carried, and a question asked from ⌘K on the page goes the same way; the turn records it (Try again sends what it sent), so a follow-up from another page goes with that page's context. An @ of the same skill is sent once and shows as the one chip, whose × takes out both. Quiz me takes the skill when he has @-mentioned none (D128.5). The placeholder reads as it does over a source; the starters stay the general ones, which read right scoped to a skill. No core or prompt change. Journey "tutor skill page".

- D192. **Later: an exercise can be put off to the end of the session, with nothing recorded** (Tyler, 2026-11-10, picked "Yes, as Later" on the Codex UX sweep's class B finding; copied from Anki's Bury, cut down to one session). Why: in Review the only ways past an exercise were a rating or Retire, so not wanting it now meant lying about how well he knew it (D21). Purpose: skip this one for now without a false rating.
  1. **What it does.** ⌘L, the clock in the exercise's toolbar (beside Ask, Edit and Retire, after the answer) or Review ▸ Later in the menu bar, before or after the answer is shown. The exercise goes to the end of this session's queue. Nothing is written: no rating, no review line, no FSRS change (D21, D22), no minutes toward the goal, nothing on the session bar, the end screen or Home's ring (D60, D176, D177). Put off and never reached again, it is simply still due next session. No counter or badge of put-off exercises (D23, purpose rule). No right-click menu: Review has none (interaction check's "Review: nothing"). The Mac only for now: the phone's Review shows no Later and its toolbar is unchanged.
  2. **The key.** ⌘L, L for Later. Anki's Bury is a plain `-`, but a plain key would type into a half-written answer; with ⌘ it works from inside the answer as ⌘⌫ No idea does, and it is free in Review and in the app (no Mac Edit-menu key). Shown in the toolbar's hint, the key bar under the exercise (both steps) and the Shortcuts sheet; rebindable like the rest of Review's keys. A held ⌘L puts off one exercise, never the ones after it. It works with Ask open too, from its field.
  3. **Where the order lives.** Only in Review's session state (`putOff` in `apps/desktop/src/ore.ts`); core, `ore due` and the queue rules are unchanged, and nothing new is persisted. Leaving with Esc ends the session as before, so the next one starts in the queue's own order; Open in source and back keeps the session, put-off order and all. The quick quiz (D76) works the same in its own session.
  4. **It can't go round forever.** When every exercise left, this one included, has been put off this session, Later ends the session instead (the only one left, or a whole pass of Laters): with ratings the end screen counts only those, with none Review just closes, as Esc does. One put off before can be put off again while one he hasn't put off is still waiting. Rating an exercise takes it off the put-off list, so a miss's relearn pass (D21) is a turn of its own.
  5. **What comes back with it.** At the end of the session it is asked afresh, with what he typed and how sure he was kept, as after Esc (`drafts`). ⌘Z after Later brings it back as the current one exactly as he left it (the answer shown, the AI's marks, its passage, Ask open or not, its clock; an answer still being checked comes back to check again), wherever steps since have left it in the session (a Retire of the next one); on the end screen ⌘Z reopens the session on it, as for a rating, its clock going on from the Later so the time on the end screen never counts; ⇧⌘Z puts it off again. A Later whose exercise has left the session (answered in the quick quiz) leaves the history and the ⌘Z goes on to the step before it; once Review closes all Laters leave it: they recorded nothing to take back.
  6. **D177.** A Later is no answer: it never counts as a miss or as the last rating. Put off the one more exercise served after a miss closed the goal, and that turn passes to the next one he answers, so the session still doesn't end on a miss. A rating's ⌘Z takes back the one more it earned with it.
  7. **The exercise loaded ahead** (the next one loads while he answers) is used by Later as by a rating, so it moves straight on; journeys "review: Later …" and "… (D192)" check each rule above.
- D193. **The sidebar's edge drags to resize** (from the Codex UX sweep of lists and the sidebar, 2026-11-10, class B finding; approved for build through the project coordinator; extends D44's resizing to the sidebar). Why: the right-hand panels resized and the sidebar did not, where Finder, Linear and Notion all let the sidebar's edge set how much room names get. Its right edge is the same divider as the source note's and the tutor's (`useSideWidth`, now measured from either side): from 180 px, where the title bar's buttons and a short skill name with its icon still fit, to 360 px or 40% of the window, whichever is smaller, so the content keeps its room at the 720 px minimum window; a narrower window clamps what shows and a wider one brings his width back. An open note panel or docked tutor gives back what the sidebar takes past 216 px from its own 60% max, so the reader column never gets less than it had before (224 px at 1100). Double-click resets it to 216 px, Esc mid-drag puts it back, the width is remembered (`ore.sidebarwidth`) and kept through hiding and showing the sidebar, and the hover peek opens at it. Everything sized off the sidebar reads `--sidebar`, set from that width; Settings keeps its own column at 216 px. No keyboard resize, as none of the panels has one. Journey "sidebar:".

- D194. **A drag from blank space beside a list's rows selects the rows it crosses** (Tyler, 2026-11-11, "yes" to the Codex UX sweep of lists: "Drag a rectangle to select rows in a list, as Finder does"; extends D188's box from the note's blocks to lists). Why: selecting several sources took ⇧ or ⌘ held down, where Finder takes the obvious drag over empty space.
  1. **Where.** The lists with the selection model of the Conventions table's "Any list" row (⇧-click, ⇧↑↓, ⌘-click, ⌘A, Esc): All sources in each view and a skill page's sources, which are one list in `App.tsx`. Home, Feed, Questions and the sidebar have no multi-selection and get no box.
  2. **From blank space only.** The list's own space (its padding, below the last row) or the bare column beside it, up to 40px either side as for the note. Never from a row (a row drags to file and opens on a click), a control, a header, the bar above, the sidebar or its divider, or a scrollbar.
  3. **While dragging** the box (D188's, one way of drawing) selects every row it crosses by height, live, and lets go of the ones it leaves. ⇧ held at the start adds to the selection there was, ⌘ toggles the rows it crosses, as Finder. Esc puts the selection back as it was. Released, it is a selection like ⇧-click's: ⋯ and right-click act on all of it, ⌘C copies, Esc clears, ⇧↑↓ go on from the row the box reached.
  4. **A click on blank space with no drag clears the selection**, as Finder does (it kept it before).
  5. **One box at a time.** A press beside the note or the list counts only level with it (amends D188.1, which took the whole column), so on a narrow skill page, where the sources sit under the note, a press beside the sources starts only theirs. A blank press lets go of words selected anywhere, as a click does. A box still being dragged ends with no more picks when the view changes or the window loses focus.
  Not in this change: scrolling the list when the box nears its edge, as D188 left it out (the wheel scrolls, and the box follows). `e2e` "list drag to select" (Chromium, a skill page, WebKit), `test/marquee.test.ts`.

- D195. **A playing source takes YouTube's K J L beside Space and the arrows** (Tyler, 2026-11-10, "yes", from the Codex UX sweep of the reader; he picked L as Forward on a source with sound and Listen everywhere else; amends D50 and D85). Why: YouTube's keys are in his hands, and the player ignored them. On a video or a recording, K plays and pauses as Space does, J goes back 15 s as ← does, L goes on 30 s as → does (D50's amounts, not YouTube's 10 s). Each is a second binding of the same command, rebindable on its own and listed under Video and listening; the play, back and forward buttons' hints show both keys. They are guarded as Space and the arrows are: not while typing in a field, a note, the tutor, find or a popup, and a seek turns following back on (D49). L stays Listen (D85) on an article, and while read-aloud plays L still stops it; read-aloud takes Space and [ ], not K or J. The keymap gives a source with sound of its own and one without their own scopes, so Listen's L and Forward's L are not a clash; every other pair clashes as before (Listen still meets Space and [ ], and K J L meet every reader key). A menu click runs its own command only, never one in the other scope that shares its key, and Source ▸ Listen is greyed out on a source with sound. A key he had already given another command (an old J on Recall this chapter) stays his: the new default on it loads not assigned. The menu bar has no player menu, as before (plain keys never show there). Journey "K J L".
- D196. **One build per component** (Tyler, 2026-11-10: "do all four", after the component page showed the same thing drawn several ways; options: https://claude.ai/artifact/QuU5zEoVUkU59GUdrPVTna).
  1. **Small buttons** (his pick A of three). Add, Finish, Start and Settings' buttons are one button: 26px, a 1px edge, no fill, the sans at 12px/500. The mono is kept for a screen's main action and the answers in Review, so mono on a button means "this is the thing to do". Not picked: B every label in the mono, C every label in the sans.
  2. **A skill on a source** (his pick C of three). Its icon in its colour and its name in `muted`, with no fill and no edge, on Home's reading rows, a source row and the reader's byline alike: the icon already carries the colour, and a filled chip in a row was a box in a box. Not picked: A a tinted chip with a mono label, B a tinted chip with a sans label.
  3. **The segmented control** (D135 made true). The side panel's tabs, the highlight's types and Settings' choices share one rule: a 32px `bg` track with a 1px edge, options 24px at 12px/500, the chosen one a raised pill. The panel's was 13px on a 26px pill and two tracks had another fill.
  4. **Counts beside gems** are in the mono, both of them (DESIGN.md: a dot face is unreadable small, so counts in rows stay in the mono). The second count was bold rounded sans.
- D197. **Gems have grades, hard recalls carry richer ore, and a session says how much memory it added** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: "proceed with implementing your recommendations", picks 1, 2, 3 and measure A of `docs/research/rewarding.md`; adds to D90, D62 and D190, amends D148.3's "nothing in gems" for remixes only). Why: the cut count stands still for weeks, a miss only ever read as damage, and nothing showed progress past recall. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: no seam or glow: a missed gem comes loose and is Won back, D236.)
  1. **Gold seam.** A cut gem he missed and won back is mended: its crack is filled with gold dots, and it keeps them. Purpose: a miss that was corrected is shown as the more valuable gem, since corrected errors are the best remembered (Butterfield & Metcalfe 2001). On the end screen when it is won back, and in the skill's strength popover (`mended`).
  2. **Polished.** A cut gem he has passed as a remix (D66, rated Good or Easy, whenever that was) is polished: its top facets all highlight. Purpose: progress beyond recall is visible (transfer, Pan & Rickard 2018). Each gem has one grade: polished, else mended, else cut. One rule (`gemGrade`) grades a gem for the skill page and the session receipt alike. On the end screen an exercise gets one gem: with its grade when it becomes solid, or polished when a gem cut earlier gets its first passed remix. **Set in a tool** (an idea used in a real try) is not built: D148.3 keeps use out of gems until he has done tries.
  3. **Richer ore.** In the cart tip, one ore per rescued recall (D62's rescued: recalled after it had nearly faded) is bigger and brighter, in the heap and in the air, and pours first; the rescued line carries the same dot when a cart ran. No rich ore when the rescued line is left out (most of the session was rescues, a backlog day). Purpose: the harder recall is the better find (rewarding effort raised the choice of harder tasks, Clay 2022). It lands as an ordinary ring dot: the ring is still minutes.
  4. **Depth.** How long his memories now last, in days added up (FSRS stability). The end screen says what the session added ("+41 days of memory", in months or years when it is that much, counting the exercises he passed; a miss takes nothing off it, so one lapse on an old memory never hides a good session); the strength popover says the skill's total ("3 months deep"). Purpose: one true number that moves every session. Never in the header, where the cut count stays the only number (D72), and never a goal.
  Not built from the same list: teach the mole, geodes, mined-out sources (rewards finishing, D62), the assay, then and now, today's find, the deep cut, and the other measures.
- D198. **A source's row names two skills, then counts the rest** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: "After two skills, should we have this go to +1", on a Continue reading row whose six skills had shrunk to "L." and "Story…"; Linear's labels on an issue row; adds to D114 and D196). Why: a name cut to a letter says nothing, and every skill added made the others harder to read. Home's reading rows, source rows and the Feed's rows show a source's first two skills whole (icon and name, D196), then "+N" in the mono; hovering it names the rest, and VoiceOver reads them. A click anywhere on them still opens the skill picker, which lists them all. A fixed two, not as many as fit: the rows line up and a row never changes as the window does. With Skill tags set to Icon only every skill shows, an icon being narrower than the count. The reader's byline keeps every skill, where there is room to wrap. `SkillChips`, test `skill-chips.test.tsx`.
- D199. **A notebook cell's output is folded until he has guessed it** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: pick 2 of the round 4 component pitches, https://claude.ai/artifact/BXRs8QehRQHxmDfJjVEd5m; the idea from nteract's `output-area`, which has no licence file, so no code of it: the row is a plain button). Why: output printed under its code is read, never predicted, and a guess before the answer is the step a prequestion is (D18). In a source whose original is an `.ipynb`, each `text` block (what core's `notebook.ts` writes a cell's output as) shows as one row, "Output", the shape of the block it opens into; hovering says "Guess it first, then show", and a click or Enter shows the output for as long as the source stays open. Opening the source again folds them again, since a second read is a second guess; nothing is typed or scored. The output's text stays in the page, hidden, so highlights and the text index do not move when it opens, and Find skips it while it is hidden. Plots stay visible: a picture can't be guessed in the head. Other sources' text blocks are untouched. `Output` in `Reader.tsx`, journey "notebook:".
- D200. **A table's column can be covered, and a covered cell makes an exercise** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: pick 1 of the round 4 component pitches, https://claude.ai/artifact/BXRs8QehRQHxmDfJjVEd5m; after Mischief's `redaction`, MIT, and D166's boxes on a figure; Anki's cloze over a table). Why: a table is a list of pairs to remember, and it could only be reread. Purpose: recall a table's cells where he reads them.
  1. **Cover.** Hovering a column's heading shows a button at its end ("Cover this column to test yourself"); it covers every cell under it that has text, each a block in the cell, and stays in the heading while the column is covered. Covers last while the source is open, as a folded output does (D199): nothing is saved or scored.
  2. **Show.** A click on a covered cell, or Enter or Space with the keyboard on it (Tab reaches each one), shows that cell; the heading's button shows whatever is left.
  3. **An exercise from a cell.** Right-click on a covered cell: Show, Show this column, and Make an exercise of this cell, a Fact exercise (D59.2) that asks the cell by its row and column ("Top-p · Typical range?") with the cell as its answer, made as a draft like a figure's (D166), with Undo. The first column names the row, so it offers none; a cell makes one exercise. `ore card cell <source> --row --column --answer` checks that one table row of the source holds both the row's name and the answer.
  4. **Right-click elsewhere in a table stays the Mac's own menu** (D83): only a covered cell, which shows no text to look up, has this one.
  5. The cover is a class on the cell: its text stays in the page, so highlights and the text index do not move. No key and no ⌘K command: both would need a column to be pointed at first.
  `DocTable` and `ColumnHead` in `Reader.tsx`, `packages/core/src/cell.ts`, test `cell.test.ts`, journey "tables:".
- D201. **The tutor's last message can be edited and its last reply tried again, and both keep what was there** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: pick 4 of the round 4 component pitches, https://claude.ai/artifact/BXRs8QehRQHxmDfJjVEd5m; ChatGPT's edit, regenerate and ‹ 2 / 2 ›, after assistant-ui's `edit-message`, `regenerate-menu` and `message-branches`, MIT, as the pattern; finishes D187's "standard chat functions"). Why: when an explanation missed, the only way on was to retype the question, and a second explanation of the same idea is often the one that lands.
  1. **Edit.** Hovering his last message shows a pencil ("Edit"); ↑ in the empty field opens it too. The message becomes a field: Enter sends it and asks again, Esc or a click away leaves it as it was.
  2. **Try another reply.** The last reply has a retry button above Copy; it asks the same message again.
  3. **Versions.** Neither throws the old reply away: the turn keeps each message and reply as a version, with ‹ 2 / 2 › under the reply to flip between them, on any turn that has more than one. That pager is the undo. A reply that failed is replaced, never kept as a version. Versions are saved with the chat (`alts`, `alt` on a turn).
  4. **Only the last turn.** Editing an earlier message would have to drop every turn after it; the tutor reads the whole chat, so a later turn would answer a question that is no longer there.
  `chat-versions.ts`, `useTutorChat` in `Tutor.tsx`, test `chat-versions.test.ts`, journey "tutor versions:".
- D202. **A PDF's own page shows beside its text, in an Original tab** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: pick 3 of the round 4 component pitches, https://claude.ai/artifact/BXRs8QehRQHxmDfJjVEd5m; Preview beside the text, after Mischief's `pdf-viewer` and `page-navigator`, MIT, as the pattern, drawn with pdf.js, Apache-2.0, which core already reads PDFs with; adds to D75's two tabs and D122). Why: a formula or a table the import got wrong could only be checked by opening the file in another app, so a broken import hid, and he had to take the text on trust.
  1. **The tab.** A source whose original is a `.pdf` has a third side-panel tab, Original, after Source note and Collected; no other source has it, and the phone has none (its original stays on the Mac). ⌘K: "Original page beside the text". The tab is remembered like the other two; on a source with no PDF the panel opens on the source note.
  2. **It follows the reading.** The panel draws one page, at the panel's width: the page whose text is at the top third of the reader. The reader knows by the `<!-- page N -->` marks core writes into a PDF's text (the body is sliced at them, with a hidden mark in the page where each starts). A PDF read by a reader that writes no marks stays on the page he turns to.
  3. **‹ 3 / 4 ›** above the page turns to another by hand; reading on to a new page takes the panel back. No pager on a one-page file.
  4. **Loaded when opened.** pdf.js is a chunk of its own, fetched with the tab; the file comes over the asset protocol (the CSP's `connect-src` now allows it). A file that can't be drawn says so and points to ⋯ ▸ Open original.
  Not built: lighting the passage being read on the page (the sketch showed it; it needs the text matched to the page's own words), zoom (the panel's width is the zoom, and Open original is one key away), clicking the page to move the text, and the same for EPUB, slides and scans' images. `OriginalPage.tsx`, `pageChunks` in `text.ts`, test `text-pages.test.ts`, journey "original:".
- D203. **A skill page's To read and Questions each have a search, and a list's search ranks and forgives a typo** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: "should probably have dedicated search if the user wants to quickly find something… fuzzy matching… the title as the preliminary ranking, then the author and other metadata", "a magnifying glass to the left of the plus sign"; Linear's and Raindrop's header search, which opens from its icon). Why: a skill with many sources or questions could only be scrolled, or left for ⌘K. Purpose: reach the source or question he has in mind in a few keys.
  1. **The control.** A magnifying glass left of each section's +, which becomes a field in the header; also in the section's right-click menu. Typing narrows that section's rows. Esc clears it, a second Esc closes it, and it closes when left empty. ↓ moves into the rows; ↵ opens the first source (or moves to the first question). Nothing shows until he asks for it. ⌘F on a skill page stays Find on the page (D81).
  2. **One ranking** (`match.ts`, `rankScore`), for these two and for All sources' ⌘F filter: every word typed must be found. Rows come in tiers, each in the list's own order, so typing narrows the list without shuffling it (Raindrop): the title is the query; every word is in the title (a question's own text); a word is found only in its course, author, site name (without its .com), skills or type (a question: its source, then what he has answered); a word is found only as a typo. A word of one or two letters counts outside the title only as a whole word ("ai"). A typo is one wrong, missing, extra or swapped letter in a word of four letters or more, two in eight or more, against a word that starts with the same letter, so short words and scattered letters never match (⌘K's rule, `titleScore`, which shares its folding of accents). (2026-11-11, after bench's "filter sources" went from ~960 to ~1,230 ms: ".com" made "Com" match nearly every web page and the ranking reordered hundreds of rows on each key; back to ~980.)
  3. **Sources** then list the ones whose text, highlights or note hold the words, with the line (`ore search`), as All sources already did. **Questions** searched show answered ones with the open ones, with no cap.
  Not built: a shortcut for it (⌘F is taken on this page; `/` types into the note), and typo matching in ⌘K. Tests `match.test.ts`, journey "skill page search".
- D204. **What to review has a No skill row** (2026-11-11, from a rehearsal of Tyler's Review on a copy of his library; amends D80's "a short list of the skills with cards due"; Linear's "No label" filter; Tyler approved it, 2026-11-11: "yes"). Exercises from a source filed under no skill (D89 and D109 allow that) were in the session when every row was ticked, but in no row: the rows added up to 7 while the session had 11, and unticking any skill dropped them without a word. They now have a row of their own, listed last, ticked like the others; unticked, they wait as any unticked skill's do. `ore due --skill -` is that pick; `ore today` counts them as `no_skill_due`, the way it counts each skill's row: from the whole session's queue, after the day's limit on new exercises, so a row can show fewer new ones than that row alone would serve, as a skill's row already can. Rejected: carrying them along under any pick (the rows still would not add up to the session). (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: rows show the gems a session could win, not a due count, D249.)
- D208. **Hiding the source turns its lines into dots** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: "Can we make the transition from visible source content to hide the source while you write be cooler? And on brand"; his pick C of three options recorded from the app, https://claude.ai/artifact/Ujjs7Lruk4v4TCh3XXWTs4: A the blur eased, B a halftone wash over the blur, C lines to dots). When the source hides (the eye or ⌥H, a chapter block, the tutor's exercise, the finish step), each line of its text on screen becomes a row of round dots its own length, sized and spaced by its font so a heading reads as bigger dots, and pictures, diagrams and code become a sparse dot field; the words fade under them in `--t-4`. The dots arrive top to bottom over `--t-moment` and go back up in `--t-4` when it shows again; the change can be reversed midway. Under Reduce Motion the dots are simply there. Replaces the blur. Why: the blur snapped on with no motion and was a smudge; dots are the house's way of drawing (D183). Purpose: the words are out of sight but the page keeps its shape, so he still sees where he was, and the change says plainly that the source is covered on purpose. `HideDots.tsx`, test `hide-dots.test.ts`.
- D207. **A reading line holds about 66 characters, at any text size** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: "make sure the default width is optimized for reading speed"; amends D27 and the Narrow/Medium/Wide widths). Why: comprehension and comfort peak around 50 to 75 characters a line, about 66 the usual target (Bringhurst; Dyson & Haselgrove 2001 found longer lines read a little faster but no better understood, and short ones slowest). The column was 60ch of the 13px interface font, so it held about 72 characters of Charter at 17px, about 55 at 22px and 87 at 14px, and Geist read narrower than Charter. Now the column is set in the reading text's own size: Medium is 31 of its em, Narrow 26, Wide 34.5, which hold about 66, 55 and 75 characters of Charter at every size; Geist's column is 1.03 times as wide for the same count. Charter's numbers come from macOS Charter in the release build (the first cut, 28em, measured on Linux's Bitstream Charter, held only about 60 on the Mac, 2026-11-11); Geist ships with the app, so the journey counts its characters, and checks Charter's column is 31em. Review and anything else on `--measure` follows. Journey "reader width:".
- D209. **The audio and video transport is plain: −15 ▶ +30** (Tyler, 2026-11-11, "these look terrible, we need much simpler versions"; pick A of three mocked; amends D48's copy of Eleven Reader's circled skip arrows and round play button). The skip buttons are their seconds in the mono face with a sign, like the time and speed beside them, and play is the 16px dot triangle with no disc. Rejected: a small dot arrow beside each number (B, still busy) and smooth outline arrows (C, breaks D157's dots-only rule).
- D212. **The reader's outline opens after a 300 ms rest** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: "add a slight delay before the table of contents pops up"; amends D45). A pointer passing over the right edge, on its way to the scrollbar or a Key point, no longer flashes the table of contents; it opens once the pointer rests 300 ms, Home's skill card's wait, and closes at once on leave. Tab onto it still opens it at once. Journey "the outline opens after a short rest".
- D210. **The reader's byline holds who wrote it and where it's from** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: move source.pdf "to be right of AI and also have an author field right there that would be blank but actually still show something that can be filled in"). One row under the title: the author, the date, the skills, then the original (`source.pdf ↗`, a page's site) last, where it used to sit alone above the title. A source with no author found on import shows a faint "Author" in its place (Notion's empty property); one click edits it in place, as clicking a named author does, and the title's menu and ⌘K keep Add author…. Not on the iPhone, which can't edit it. Amends D77's order. (2026-11-12, Tyler: the link sat low on the phone and the article started too close: the original link sits on the byline's line, or starts the next line flush when the byline fills one, and 28px, up from 16, clears the byline from the text, Mac and iPhone.) (2026-11-21, Tyler: the lone + that showed on hover where a source in no skill keeps its skills "looks stupid" and was "inconsistent with author when author is blank": the byline holds a faint "Skill" there, always shown, in the blank Author's look, and one click opens the skill picker.
