- D211. **Clicking away from a field saves it** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: he typed an author, clicked away, and it was gone until he went back and pressed Enter: "that generally just shouldn't happen throughout the app"). Every text field edited in place saves on Enter and on a click away, as Finder's rename, Notion's properties and Linear's titles do; Esc is the one way to throw an edit away. Covers a source's title and author, a row's rename, a skill's name and goal, a collection's name, a new skill, a new question, a diagram's name and the review plan. An unchanged or empty name keeps the old one, a taken name stays open and says so, a save that fails leaves the text in the field, and switching to another app saves nothing (the field is still there on return). Not the tutor's edit of a sent message (D201): that would send it, so a click away still leaves it as it was. Amends D63, D102 and the Conventions table's "Title edited in place".
- D213. **A drawn shape goes back to Select, and angles land on 45° steps** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: "after you draw a shape, the tool should switch back to Select"; reverses the 2026-11-10 Conventions row from #233 that kept the picked tool; Excalidraw's and tldraw's default). A box, diamond, ellipse, arrow, line or text, once drawn, leaves the Select tool with it selected, so the next click moves or labels it instead of drawing another. The pen keeps its tool, as in Excalidraw, since a drawing is many strokes. The lock (Q, its toolbar button) still keeps any tool and is remembered; it starts off for everyone, his old setting included. Why: with the tool kept, a click meant to pick what he just drew drew a second shape. Also (Tyler, 2026-11-11, from two lines that were nearly but not quite perpendicular): a line, arrow, dragged point or rotation within 4° of a multiple of 45° lands on it, with a dashed guide through it while the drag lasts; ⌘ turns it off like the other snapping, ⇧ still steps by 15°. And the diagram's red, green, blue and orange are drawn in the app's palette (styles.css `--c-*`), the same as a note's coloured text beside it; the file keeps Excalidraw's values. Journey "diagram: a drawn box is selected under Select".
- D214. **The mole is a puppet: smooth springs, sampled onto its dot grid** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: "Now I like smooth motion", after a lab of today's stepped mole beside a dot puppet and a Rive mole, https://claude.ai/artifact/4M6jeuAztzKTTRWmZvgtPz; his pick "Build the puppet"; amends D169's "every move shifts whole dots" and D185's "spring only on moments" for the mole). Each value of its shape (how high it stands, its dome, its lean, its paws, where it looks, how open its eyes are) follows its own spring, and each frame the smooth shape is sampled onto the same 22×18 grid: a dot's size says how much of it the shape covers, so the head and paws glide in halftone (D183). The face stays whole dots that move a column at a time (eyes, glints, nose, mouth), since a feature sampled smoothly shimmered and lost its glint in the lab. Three rows of room above the grid let the dome round out (it was clipped flat). Surprised now opens an O under the nose. Same moods, places and props as before; at rest it still only blinks and glances (no breathing, no following the pointer: neither has a purpose), and frames run only while it moves. Still under Reduce Motion and in a hidden window. Rejected: Rive (its shapes have to be translated back into dots each frame, which glitched, and it adds a 650 KB runtime and a closed editor for one sprite). `moleDots` and `MOVES` in `Mole.tsx`, test `mole.test.ts`. Polish pass (2026-11-12, Tyler: "perfect and apply the mole animations across all relevant places"): every place the mole lives already used the puppet (tutor button, chat field, Review's end), and no place was added (AI waits keep D146's line, empty states have no purpose for it). Its moves were too small to see: a dot is 1.6 to 1.8px in the chat and the button and the hop moved one row. Now a hop is a squat, a spring of two rows with both paws thrown up, and the dirt as it lands; a paw's tap is a row and a half; thinking blinks; the head keeps its mass (narrower when stretched, wider when squashed). At Review's end it comes up out of the ground before it cheers whether or not a cart came first.
- D215. **An episode's own page comes in as its episode, its page text one click away** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: "a button to optionally add the page content … simple by default and one click to add the page content when the user finds the page content useful and worth studying"; amends D92 for audio, D122 and D167). A show's or publisher's page that is an episode (Scientific American's Science Quickly, with a Megaphone player) came in as article text only: the audio, the player and the timestamps were lost. Now it comes in as an Apple Podcasts link does: the audio kept as the original to play in place, its heard transcript the text, under the page's own title, author and date; the fetched page is kept beside it as `page.html`. On such a source a quiet "Add the page's text" button under the player (Snipd: the show notes one tap from the player), its ⋯ item and its ⌘K row add the page's text as the article reader makes it (headings, links, images, speaker names) below the transcript under "From the page"; no highlight moves, the button goes, and Undo takes the text out (its highlights with it, back on Redo). `ore page-text <item> [--undo|--redo <cut-id>]`. Unlike D92's page with its video, the page's text isn't added by default: a publisher's page is often the same transcript, edited. When a page is an episode (`pageAudio`): its page-level JSON-LD is a PodcastEpisode or RadioEpisode whose media is audio, or `og:type` says episode with an `og:audio`; otherwise it plays exactly one audio (its `og:audio`, an `<audio>` file, a Megaphone episode player) at an address with a part after `/podcast(s)/` or `/episode(s)/`. A news story with a clip or a read-aloud of itself, a show's index and a video podcast stay articles. Audio that can't be fetched or heard leaves the article as it was, and a machine that can't hear speech (not a Mac on macOS 26) doesn't download it. Reimport keeps a source's shape: an article stays the article, and an episode he added the page text to gets it again after the new transcript. Tests: intake "a publisher's episode page", "page text is added below its transcript"; journey "an episode with its page kept".
- D216. **A selected node shows + on its free sides** (Tyler, 2026-11-11, "yes" to FigJam's and Miro's quick create, after Codex's second diagram sweep found the mouse had no way to grow a map from a concept). One selected box, ellipse or diamond shows a small square + past each side that no link already uses; a click makes a linked node that way with its label open, as ⌘+arrow does. Purpose: the next concept and its link without leaving the one he is on. The same pass, from that sweep: a typed or tidied map never runs a link through a node it doesn't join (each joined map laid out on its own, a root one column before its nearest child, a node a skipping link would cross moved below it); an elbow arrow's middle segment drags and stays (its corners follow, so they are not handles); text and a box's label align left, centre or right, and a box's label can sit at its top, for a section's title (Excalidraw's text and vertical align, in Style › More). Codex's third sweep tightened it: a loop's link back no longer sets columns (no link joins two nodes in one column) and a node is placed level with its parents, so a map with loops and fan-in stays clear too; a + that would sit on another shape isn't shown, since it would take that shape's click; ⇧ on a dragged end steps by 15° as it does when drawing. Journey "diagram: a selected box's +".
- D217. **Prequestions lean toward the skill's goal** (Tyler, 2026-11-11: "Are the pre-questions written with the associated documents' skills goals in mind?", then "really just have this nudge towards the direction of enabling the user to learn the knowledge more deeply that will fulfill his goals"). Enrich (`enrich.md` v16) already passed each skill's `goal` but used it only for review exercises. Now, when a skill the source is filed under has a goal, the prequestions favour the central ideas that bear on it and ask them as questions about doing it. A nudge, not a filter: every prequestion still asks a central idea of the source, and a source that says little toward the goal ignores it. Evidence: a pretest mostly helps the tested information, with little spread to the rest (Carpenter & Toftness 2017), so what a prequestion asks is what he reads for; each prequestion also becomes a review exercise. `eval:questions` cases git-undo and feedback-talk carry a goal the source serves and sourdough one it doesn't: prequestions 100% on both runs, cards 70% and 64%.
- D218. **What to review has Select all / Deselect all, and ⌥-click solos a row** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "where is the one click to toggle selecting all?"; amends D80). D58's Home chips let ⌥-click solo a skill and ⌥-click again turn every one back on; D80 moved the chips into the picker and that went without a word, leaving one click per row to change the whole pick. A quiet text button beside the heading (Gmail, Linear) reads Deselect all while every row is ticked, else Select all, shown when there are two rows or more; ⌥-click on a row ticks only it, and ⌥-click on the only ticked row ticks every row again (Final Cut, Logic).
- D221. **The app icon is a pickaxe in dots** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "a cooler app icon ... something with a pickaxe in it"; his pick of fifteen sketched directions, https://claude.ai/artifact/7VauDS3DX8dPkeNswMwm2t, then of six that added a page, a book or a pencil, https://claude.ai/artifact/2cfJTGU2rkfKjY9gZgqErX: "honestly, i like it plain", "we can commit to that as v1"; amends D129's wordmark icon). The Mac and iPhone icon is one pickaxe drawn as halftone dots (D183) on the dark tile: white at the head, fading to gray down the handle, no colour ("the random blue tip isnt it. if anything id want a gradient or just plain white + gray"). Its ink is centred on the tile and spans 60% of it on its longer side. `apps/desktop/scripts/app-icon.ts` draws it and writes every icon file, each size from the vectors; on the Mac it also rewrites the iPhone's Xcode icon set, which is not in git. The "ore_" wordmark stays the lettering wherever the name is written. Why: the wordmark said nothing of mining and looked like a terminal tool; the pick is the one tool of the app's whole metaphor, drawn the way the app draws everything.
- D222. **The library lives in Ore's iCloud Drive folder, and the iPhone reads and writes it** (2026-11-11, once the paid Apple team existed; carries out D78.6). The Mac's library moves to `~/Library/Mobile Documents/iCloud~com~tylerpoelking~ore/Documents` (shown as "Ore" in iCloud Drive), and `~/Ore` becomes a link to it, so the app, the CLI and agents keep their path. `.index/`, `.ore/` and `.git` stay in the folder but iCloud skips them (the `com.apple.fileprovider.ignore#P` attribute), so they stay per device as D78.6 said. The phone loads the folder's text files at launch, sends each write back as it happens (an atomic write's temp file never goes), and reads the Mac's changes in when it comes back to the screen and every 30 s while it shows; a file it hasn't sent yet keeps its version. A file iCloud hasn't downloaded is asked for, and the first launch waits for it. Logs never clash, since each device writes its own (D78.4); a note edited on both at once keeps iCloud's latest. The snapshot built into the app stays only for the browser (the e2e journeys). Why iCloud and Ore's own folder: no server or account of Ore's (D4), and it is how Obsidian syncs a vault of files between a Mac and an iPhone.
- D219. **While reading, find in the text and filter the lists** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "should we have a search within the collections section? and the source note? … so its clear to the user what's being searched over vs not", and "where needs the next or previous functionality vs just simple filter"; his pick A of three; amends D81's "the side panel (source note or Questions)"). One rule: text read in order (the source, a note) gets find with next and previous, because where a match sits matters; a list of separate rows (Collected, To read, Questions, All sources) gets a filter, because every match shows at once and arrows add nothing. Nowhere has both.
  1. **⌘F says what it searches.** Its field reads "Find in source", or "Find in source and note" while the Source note tab is open (and "Find in note" from a ⌘K match in the note; "Find in page" on a skill page). Opening or closing the note while it is open counts again. Collected and the Original tab are no longer part of it.
  2. **Collected has a filter.** A magnifying glass at the right of the side panel's tabs, on the Collected tab only, opens a full-width field in a row under them (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "putting it here instead of cramped horizontally"; the field in the tabs' row squeezed them); the glass shows pressed while it is open, and a click on it clears and closes it. Typing keeps the rows whose passage, note or question match, in reading order, every group open; a group with none goes, and if none match it says "Nothing collected here matches". ↓ or ↵ moves into the rows, Esc clears it, then closes it. It clears for each source. Not on the phone yet.
  3. **Collected's Questions are this source's** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "yes"; amends D75.2 and D99). The group listed every open question of the source's skills, and inside Collected, under Key points and Highlights from this source, with the skill's name hidden, a passage highlighted as a question in another source (and shown as its passage, with no question typed) read as this one's. Now it lists the questions asked in this source and those the AI matched as "May be answered here"; the skill's others stay on its page. A question asked with its + is marked as one this source may answer, so it stays where he asked it. Empty, it says "No open questions from this source."
  4. **Not searched.** The Original tab (a picture of the PDF page; open it to check a page, and the source's text is what ⌘F finds) and the tutor chat. The Source note needs no search of its own: ⌘F covers it.
  `Find.tsx`, `HeadSearch.tsx` (`SearchButton`, `SearchField`), `CollectedPanel`, journey "search while reading".
- D223. **Adding a source on the iPhone queues its link for the Mac** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "yes" to the plan in the iPhone sync thread; carries out D145.6 now that sync exists, D222, and gives the ⋯ sheet's missing Add a home, D155). A + in the phone's top bar opens a sheet with one field: he pastes a link and taps Add. The link is written to `inbox/<id>.link.json`, reaches the Mac through iCloud, and the Mac imports it with the whole import and enrichment (`ore inbox --import`) when Ore opens there or comes back to the front after 10 s away. A toast says what came in (or that it was already there), or why a link couldn't; a link that failed is tried again next time, as the Mac may just have been offline, and the same reason isn't announced twice. Until then the phone lists it under "Waiting for your Mac" on Sources, with Remove (and Undo), and a link the Mac couldn't import shows its reason there. A link pasted without https:// gets it. Why the Mac: the import has to be whole (D96, D122), and the AI runs there (D78.3). Not yet: files picked on the phone, and filing under a skill from the phone (it lands unfiled, and its suggested skills come from enrichment).
- D224. **A double click highlights the sentence, a triple click the paragraph; on the iPhone a double tap highlights the paragraph** (Tyler, 2026-11-12, picked this over keeping the Mac's word and a sentence tap on the phone). Why: the sentence is the smallest passage worth reviewing, and one-word highlights were mostly accidents (his AI Revolution source had strays like "Increasing" and "4"); in the reader every selection is already a highlight (D15), so the Mac's plain word select was gone anyway. Mac: a double click's word grows to its sentence by the system's sentence breaks (Intl.Segmenter, within its block), shows selected at once, and is kept after the double-click time unless a third click makes it the paragraph (one write, one ⌘Z); ⌥ double click keeps just the word; a double click dragged on selects by words as the Mac does; in code or math it keeps the word. Right-click Look Up is unchanged. iPhone: a double tap on plain text highlights its innermost paragraph (a list item, a table cell, a caption), trimmed, and opens its popup with Remove a tap away; a heading, a link, a figure, a timestamp or an existing highlight keeps its single tap, so none of them waits for a second; a tap that stops a scroll or moves is no tap; the reader's text takes `touch-action: manipulation`, so iOS's double-tap zoom is off and a pinch still zooms. No triple tap. A double click on a word inside a highlight opens that highlight. The system's sentence breaks already go on past "e.g." or "Fig. 3"; where they still split before a capital, after an initial (J. R., U.S.) or a common title, reference or month (Dr., Prof., No., Fig., Jan., …), the sentence goes on; "etc." is left out, as it often ends one. A rare real end after one of those joins two sentences; the edge handles (D117) fix it. A double tap over a paragraph that already holds a highlight grows that one, as a selection past its edge does (D117). Amends D15 and D123.
- D225. **A long press on the iPhone opens the Mac's right-click menu, and any source can be reviewed on its own** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "I'm long pressing sources and skills on iOS and nothing is happening", then "Yes" to the two lists, and "review this source is kinda cool ... if someone really liked a YouTube video or book and want to practice that in isolation"; amends D145.4, which held only the Sources list's rows).
  1. **The hold.** A still half second (10 pt of drift allowed) on a source or a skill, wherever it is listed (Today, Sources, a skill's list, the drawer), opens its menu from the row's left edge, as iOS lines its menu up with what was pressed. Radix's own touch timer gave up at a real finger's first drift, and Today's rows had no menu on the phone. The click WebKit may send as the finger lifts is the press, never a pick of the item under it.
  2. **A source's menu**: Open, Rename… (in place), Review this source, Pin to top, Move to Done or To read, Open original, Skills ›, Archive. Pinned sources lead the phone's To read, as on the Mac. Not on the phone: Reveal in Finder, Copy path, Copy as Markdown, Show (Finder and keyboard things).
  3. **A skill's menu**: Open, Review this skill, Edit skill… (name, icon, colour, collection), Move to ›, Importance ›, Archive skill. Delete skill stays on the Mac, where a slip can't take a skill's exercises with it.
  4. **Review this source**, on the Mac's rows and the reader's ⋯ too: a session of only that source's exercises, its new ones included past the day's new-exercise limits (`ore due --source`), and under an archived skill. It is his pick of one thing to practise, as picking a skill is; the daily goal counts it as any review. Nothing due: "Nothing from this source is due right now."
  Journeys "phone: a long press on a Today source", "phone: a long press on a skill".
- D226. **A page's section names are folder tabs, bigger and brighter** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: the Note, To read and Questions headers were "boring"; of four mocked directions he took none whole, asked for "the original font", the icon kept, "bigger and brighter", then described his own design and said "build it" on its clickable sketch, picking the outlined tab over a filled one). On a skill page each section's head is a tab drawn in the rule's own line, the rule broken under it, holding the section's icon and its name in 16px mono `text` (was 12px `muted`). A section's views are tabs: the open one shows its name, the others are their icons (Done a green tick, Archived a box, Answered a tick), named on hover with their shortcut, and a click grows the tab out to its name; from the keyboard it changes at once, and ←→ move between tabs. This replaces the To read ▾ menu (its row-details checkboxes stay under a small ▾ in the open tab) and the Questions list's "Answered (n)" line; "Show all n" stays, since it only lengthens the open list. All sources' list has the same tabs. Home's section names are the same 16px mono `text` over their rule. The sidebar's group labels stay 12px `muted`. Amends D131's Answered toggle and 2026-11-11's "the list title's menu shows no shortcuts" (the hints carry ⌘1 and ⌘2 again, as every control's does). The grow animates the tab's width, which the animation standards keep for transform and opacity: it is three small elements for 200 ms, and what he asked for is that the tab grows. Not picked: a Geist title with no rule or icon, the dot face over a rule of dots, a solid tab in the skill's colour, a heavy white rule with small caps, a filled tab. The caret that folds a section sits at the far right of its head, past its + and search (Tyler, 2026-11-12, on the Mac: at the left of the tabs it "looks like just another view"). Mocks: `/mnt/project-files/house-style/bolder-round-1/`.
- D227. **The iPhone imports what it captures; the Mac only brings in what a phone can't** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "full feature parity on the ios… importing new sources through the share sheet in a way that immediately syncs across devices", then "Proceed" on the spike and "do the phases and whatever you see fit"; amends D223 and D145.6). The spike showed the Mac's importers run inside the phone app and give the same `doc.md` (web pages, a PDF, an EPUB, in 1 to 6 s), so a link no longer waits for the Mac. A YouTube video is the one source added with a part missing: a phone can't run yt-dlp, so it comes in at once with its title and channel (YouTube's public oEmbed), plays, and carries `owed: [transcript]`; any device with yt-dlp fills it as a reimport on its next inbox pass (`fillOwed`, which finds it by its note `inbox/<id>.owed.json`), and one that fails keeps `owed_error` until he reimports it. `owed` names what is missing, never which device fills it, so a server could later. **Audio and podcasts are heard on the iPhone itself** (2026-11-12): the shell runs Apple's on-device speech (`apps/phone/ios/Hear.swift`, iOS 26; `can_transcribe`, `transcribe`), which the core reaches through `setTranscriber` (audio.ts), so an episode comes in on the phone with its timed transcript and its audio kept. It works while Ore is on screen (iOS pauses it in the background and it carries on when Ore is back). A phone that can't (an older iOS) leaves the episode for the Mac, as before. Downloads and binary saves cross to the shell as raw bytes, and saved audio leaves the web view's memory: an hour's episode peaked at 1.9 GB before, about 360 MB after. **An episode is a source at once** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "the source should still open even if it's not fully populated… with all the metadata, the title, and the thumbnail filled out. It needs to show that it's loading there", F60): on the phone `inbox --import --owe` adds it with its title, show, date and artwork and `owed: [transcript]`, and the app fills it after, outside any command (`fillOwed` with a `turn`: the download and the hearing hold nothing, only the write takes its turn among his own commands), so the lists and the reader stay his. The reader, where the transcript will be, and the source's row say what is happening and how far (`Filling.tsx`: "Downloading 12 of 45 MB", "Transcribing", a bar once there is a fraction, "Keep Ore open to finish."), and the transcript lands in the open reader. **It plays meanwhile** (Tyler, 2026-11-13: "keep working" on audio playable right away): the owed note keeps the episode's audio address (`audio`), `ore show` gives it as the source's audio with no words yet, and the reader's recording bar plays from it; the audio lives above the view that is drawn anew when the transcript lands, and keeps the address it began with, so the listening isn't cut off or sent back to 0:00. The device that added it goes first; another that can waits five minutes (the note's `self`), as for the inbox's links.
  1. **Saved first, then read.** A link added with + or shared from another app is written to `inbox/<id>.link.json` at once, so nothing is lost if the app closes. The phone then imports its inbox itself, when the link is added, at launch and whenever the app comes back. A toast names what came in.
  2. **The device that captured it goes first.** Another device leaves a link alone for five minutes, then imports it itself (the phone's app may never have opened), and takes it at once when the first device couldn't read it. So the two don't both import a link while iCloud is still carrying the news; a link already in the library is kept once either way.
  3. **What the phone can't read waits for the Mac, and says so.** Sources lists these under "Coming in": a link being read says Reading…, and one the phone couldn't read keeps its reason and "Your Mac will try it.", with Remove. (Was "Waiting for your Mac", which every link used to do.)
  4. **Other sites are fetched by the shell** (`http_fetch`), since a web view can't read them; a source's saved images, audio and PDF load from the library folder through the asset protocol, scoped to that folder.
  5. **The share sheet** (a Share Extension, Swift, no web view) saves the link the same way. It imports nothing itself: Apple caps an extension's memory, and it peaked at 22 MB against the app's 230.
  Exercises and suggested skills still come from the Mac's daily catch-up (D78.3). Files and photos come in the same way, picked with + or shared (`inbox/<id>.file.json` beside `inbox/<id>/<name>`), and the share card files a link with one tap on a skill's chip. Each chip carries the skill's icon in its colour (the app writes the icon's dots beside the name, `phone/share-skills.ts`), and the card sits at the foot of its sheet, in reach of a thumb (Tyler 2026-11-12: "everything aligned on the bottom"; iOS sizes the sheet), and says what was shared: its picture across the card in its own wide shape (a square cut its sides off), then its title and site (a file's name), drawn on the card's dark, over the chips, and stays until Open, Done, a tap off it or a swipe down. **Open** is its main button, filled, with a quieter Done beside it (Tyler 2026-11-12: "Open should be the default action"); it goes on to the source in Ore (Tyler 2026-11-12, after ChatGPT's share, which opens its app): the card leaves the item's id in the App Group and brings the app forward with `ore://open`; the app imports it and opens what it became (`share_open`, `importQueued`'s `inbox`). Apple has no call for this, so it uses the route other apps' share extensions use and an iOS update may stop it; Done keeps him in Safari: closing by itself after 3 s read as a glitch on his phone (Tyler 2026-11-12). Not yet: a podcast's transcript made on the phone. Three rules keep a server possible later for someone with no Mac: importers stay in `packages/core`, files stay the library, and what a source is still owed names the work, not the device. Journey "phone: a link added is read on the phone itself".
- D228. **The iPhone gets the skill page, courses, New skill, Settings and the review reminder** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: iPhone parity phase 4, "we're going to crank them all out today"; question 5 of the parity plan, the reminder as a notification now). Each copies the Mac's own screen or an Apple app:
  1. **A skill's page** is the Mac's (D46, D226) in one column: the badge, name, importance, goal with Try it and the strength gem, then a Your note row that opens the note's own screen (as before), then To read and Done as the section's tabs (the first 8, then Show all, with its search), To try, and Questions. Nothing folds on the phone. + on its sources adds a link filed under the skill.
  2. **A course is one row** (D79), placed and labelled as on the Mac (in To read while a lesson is left, "part · next lesson" under its title); a tap opens its lessons in order on a screen of their own, as Podcasts opens a show. Its long press: Continue (or Open), Show lessons, Open original, Archive course (with Undo). Archive lists its sources flat, as the Mac's does.
  3. **New skill** is the drawer's last skills row: the name is typed in place (the Mac's sidebar, Files' New Folder), Return or a tap away makes it and opens its page, a name he has is refused in place, empty it goes away. Icon, colour and collection stay in Edit skill… on its long press.
  4. **Settings** is the drawer's last row, a pushed screen of grouped rows as iOS Settings: Minutes a day, New exercises a day, Weekdays only, When you review, Reminder (a switch, then the time on iOS's own wheel), and iCloud (whether this phone is up to date, what it is still sending or waiting for, or why a save failed, with Check now). The Mac's AI, shortcut, menu bar and reading settings have no phone twin here.
  5. **The reminder** (D67) is one time for both devices, kept in skills.yaml. iOS runs nothing of Ore's while it is closed, so the phone hands iOS the next 14 days of reminders (local notifications, tauri-plugin-notification) and plans them again whenever the app opens, leaves the screen or ends a review: today's goes once the goal is met or nothing is due, weekends go with Weekdays only. Turning it on asks iOS for permission from that tap. The Mac's LaunchAgent follows a time changed on the phone when Ore on the Mac opens or comes to the front. Known limit: a goal met on the Mac doesn't stop the phone's reminder that day until the phone opens Ore.
- D229. **A key point is a gem, and the popup's icons are always solid** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "I still think these icons could look way better", then "Every star just looks jank", "making the key point a blue gemstone instead", and of three sets mocked in the popup, "I like A the most for question, and try it"; amends D118's star). At rest the popup drew an automatic one-dot outline of each filled sprite: faint, and the ? cut from a coin turned to mush at 20px. Now each icon is the same solid drawing on or off, in its colour, and the pill alone says which is on: a cut gem for Key point (the ore worth keeping, which review cuts into one of his gems, D90), a bare question mark three dots thick, a flask with liquid in it. The same drawings show wherever a type does (the margin, Collected, skill pages, the phone). Tried and dropped: a two-dot line set, a coarser 10-grid of bigger dots (crisp, but off the 16 grid every other icon uses), halftone shading (blurs at 20px). `PixelIcon.tsx`.
- D230. **The iPhone reads every kind of source as the Mac does** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: Phase 3 of the iPhone parity plan, https://claude.ai/artifact/T5B7jesXfVy6AfBbTf5GAk, "we're going to crank them all out today"; carries out D78 for the reader). Each part copies the Apple app that does it on a phone:
  1. **Pictures** (Books, Photos): a tap on any picture in the text opens it over the whole screen, grown from where it sat; a pinch or a double tap zooms it, a drag pans it once zoomed, and a tap or a swipe down puts it back. Drawing boxes for exercises on a figure (D166) stays on the Mac: on the phone a drag is the finger's. `phone/FigureView.tsx` (react-zoom-pan-pinch, MIT).
  2. **Video** (YouTube): the player stays pinned under the top bar while the transcript scrolls under it.
  3. **A recording** (Podcasts' mini player): its controls are a bar just above the source's bottom bar, in reach of the thumb wherever he reads; when the bottom bar leaves as he reads on, the player drops into its place. Back to current sits above it.
  4. **Timestamps** lead their paragraph (the phone has no gutter), with a 44 pt touch to seek.
  5. **Title and author** are edited in place from ⋯ (Rename…, Edit author…); the title field keeps the title's size. Copy path leaves the phone's ⋯ (no Finder there).
  6. **A PDF's own page** (D202's Original tab) is "Original page" in ⋯: a page beside the source, as the note is, Back returning to the text; ‹ › turn it, and a pinch zooms it, drawn again sharper once the pinch settles.
  Journeys "phone: a picture in a source opens…", "phone: a recording plays from a bar…", "phone: a video stays pinned…", "phone: Source actions renames…", "phone: a PDF's own page…".
- D231. **Every menu item has an icon, and a menu's groups are split by lines** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "add icons to the menus, like the triple-dot menus and right-click menus, similar to ChatGPT. Sectioning is good too, with the thin lines"). Each item of a ⋯ or right-click menu on the Mac starts with its 20px pixel icon, `muted`, from one table keyed by the action's id (`menu-icons.ts`), so Rename draws the same pencil in every menu and the lists that build menus stay text. Groups: what to do with it (Open, Review), how it changes (Rename, Pin, Skills, Done), where it opens or copies (Open original, Reveal, Copy), then Archive or Delete last on its own. Five icons were drawn for it on the 16 grid after MingCute: Pin, Folder (Reveal in Finder, Move to a collection), Scissors (remove from the text), User (author), Path (Copy path). Submenu items draw none, as in ChatGPT; the diagram canvas's long command menu draws none, as Excalidraw's and Figma's don't; the phone's action sheet is unchanged. (Amended 2026-11-13, Tyler: "make sure icons are in all iOS and macOS right click/3-dot menus": the iPhone's ⋯ sheet draws the same icons, at the left like its hold menu; a submenu draws a mark where its items are things that have one: a highlight's Type shows each type's mark in its colour, Move to shows a folder per collection, beside the skill icons and importance levels those submenus already drew. Value lists (Show, a view's details) and the diagram canvas stay plain.) (Amended again 2026-11-13, Tyler "Turn into, yes": Turn into's block types each draw an icon, as Notion's do, in the ⋮⋮ menu, the selection bar and the phone's sheet: eight drawn on the 16 grid after MingCute (Heading 1–3, bulleted and numbered list, to-do box, callout box, code), Text, Toggle and Quote reuse T, the caret and the quote marks, and a toggle heading draws its heading's.)
- D232. **Typing a title selects its row; a list's actions take ⌘** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "f8 yes" to type-to-select from the findings list, then "can we have actions be behind shortcuts instead of just letters? Similar to how ChatGPT does it, with rename and pin"; amends the plain A, R and O of D84 and the reader). In any source list, letters typed within a second of each other make a prefix and the first row whose title starts with it is selected (case and accents ignored); the same letter again steps to the next row starting with it, round to the top; no match leaves the selection; Space joins a prefix already begun (Finder). So no plain letter acts on a row: Rename is ⌥⌘R, Pin or unpin ⌥⌘P (new), Archive ⇧⌘A and Open original ⇧⌘O, in lists and in the reader alike, shown in the row menus and the menu bar (ChatGPT's chat menu). A key he had rebound stays his. Why: a plain A archived the row he meant to jump to, and a ⌘ key can't fire while he types. Rejected: keeping A, R and O and letting the other letters jump (a title starting with A archived its row). `typeahead.ts`, journey "list type-select".
- D233. **A highlight's note keeps a list going** (Tyler, 2026-11-12, "as ChatGPT and Claude do in their chat boxes"). In the popup's note, ⇧↵ on a line that starts "- ", "* " or "1. " starts the next item ("2. " after "1. "), and ⇧↵ on an empty item ends the list, as in those chat boxes; ↵ still closes the popup. A typed "- " or "* " shows as a bullet (•), as in Claude's box, and saves as "- ", so the note stays plain Markdown (`list-keys.ts`); digits in the note share one width, so "1." and "2." items line up. D133 amended: a digit in an empty note still sets the type, but a "." or ")" right after it takes that back and writes "1." so a note can open with a numbered list. A note past four lines widens the popup once from 360 to 440 px and keeps it so while open (one rewrap, never back and forth while typing). Copy, Remove text and Remove are pinned to the popup's top right, beside the types, rather than centred or at the foot: a growing note never moves them from where the pointer met them. A highlight note's hover preview keeps its line breaks. Journeys "highlight note: ⇧↵ carries a list on…", "highlight note: "2." in an empty note…".
- D234. **Home and the Feed pick and drag like the source lists** (F9, F10; Tyler 2026-11-12: "consistent behavior on views that ultimately look similar"). On Home's Continue reading and in the Feed, ⌘-click adds or drops a row, ⇧-click picks the run from the last one clicked, a plain click opens, and Esc clears the pick (Finder). Right-click or ⋯ on a picked row acts on the whole pick: on Home the lists' many-source menu (file, archive, copy…), in the Feed Mark N read or unread and Copy N as Markdown, each with one Undo. A Continue reading row, or the pick it is in, drags onto a sidebar skill to file it there, as list rows do. Mark read in the Feed moves from U to ⇧⌘U (Mail), so a plain letter never acts on a row (D232). Amends D107.

- D235. **Notes and Review on the iPhone: moving blocks, table edits, a picture, diagrams to view, and Later** (Tyler, 2026-11-12, iPhone parity Phase 5, "we're going to crank them all out today"; diagrams view-only was his answer to the plan's question 4; amends D192.1's "the Mac only for now"). Why: on the phone a note's blocks moved one menu trip at a time, a table could only be typed into, a picture couldn't go in, a diagram opened the Mac's drawing editor a finger can't use, and Review had no way past an exercise but a rating or Retire.
  1. **Moving a block.** The keyboard bar's ⋮⋮ ▸ Move picks up the caret's block (the Mac's ⋮⋮ selection, which typing can't change) and the bar becomes ↑ ↓ ✓: each tap is one editor step, and ✓ puts the caret back in the block. A tap per step rather than a drag: iOS takes a long press on note text for its own selection, and arrows can't drop a block in the wrong place (Reminders' and Bear's reorder). Move up and Move down left the ⋮⋮ sheet for it.
  2. **Tables.** With the caret in a cell the bar swaps the block kinds (to-do, bullet, heading, indent) for Table actions: the Mac's row and column grips' edits in one sheet, each as the Mac's (`editTable`, the header row stays on top). Tab still goes to the next cell.
  3. **A picture.** An image button on the bar, and / Image, open iOS's own sheet (Photo Library, Take Photo, Choose File) through a file field, as Notes and Mail do; the picture is copied into the note's `images/` as a paste is (`notes --attach -`). The CLI decodes the pasted bytes without Node's `Buffer`, which the phone's web view lacks. It shows through the phone's asset protocol (D227).
  4. **Diagrams to view.** A tap opens the drawing on its own screen, fit to it: pinch zooms, a finger pans once zoomed, a double tap zooms in on that spot or back out (Photos); Done closes it. Nothing there edits the file, and / Diagram is off the phone's menu. Drawing stays on the Mac.
  5. **Review's ⋯.** The phone's Review has a ⋯ beside × (Mail, Notes) with Later (D192, before or after the answer), Edit exercise and Bad version once the answer shows (before, Edit would give it away), and Retire or Delete. It replaces the toolbar under the answer on the phone, whose Ask needs the Mac's AI (D7). The Mac is unchanged.
  Journeys "phone: Review's ⋯ …" and "phone: the note's keyboard bar …" (D235). Not in this: iCloud's conflict copies of a note (the plan's Phase 5 lists them; nothing on the phone shows one yet).

- D236. **A gem's dots fuse into solid pixels when it is cut, and no gem glows** (Tyler, 2026-11-12, choosing a look for the product video's hero moments after boards of six gem styles, five kinds of glass and a soft gem in the app: "remove the glow, at least the way we have it now. It forces the gems to be anticlimactically small... too blurry", "the dots almost get bigger... could we do the dots and 8-bit video game style and mesh those two?"; of square, gapped and rounded pixels he picked square, "the most vibrant-looking end result"; boards https://claude.ai/artifact/6dMgwS1RSJQYGZ31VQsrbJ, https://claude.ai/artifact/NdcckDyMoE1cANx7wm3m39; amends D183's "one way of drawing" and D184 for the cut gem only, D90's glow, and D197's polished look). One grid the whole way, and how solid a gem looks is how solid the memory is: a growing card is loose halftone dots as before; one review from solid, its dots have swollen until they almost touch (this replaces the glow, and one due today swells and settles slowly where it used to breathe); cut, they have fused into square pixels with a tone per shade. Shape tells the state, so the states read apart at 14px (his: the last three were "hard to truly distinguish" small, and "I do like the idea of it transforming from a blob or a rock into a gem"): a memory is a rock until its first cut and a gem's outline ever after. Nearly cut is still the rock, swollen, with one face already cut. Missed is the gem's outline in its own colour with its dots come loose again: no crack and no grey, because a miss on a memory he was sure of is when it is learned best, and the memory is weakened, not broken (this amends D90's cracked gem and D197's seam). Won back, the gem's rim is gold for good, and stays gold if it comes loose again (his pick over a seam, a whole gold gem and a gold top face: "I like the rim better than the blob of gold on the top"; board https://claude.ai/artifact/5aiVfPzQHUJSwfonWygjAC); on a yellow or orange skill the rim is white gold. The words follow: "Missed" and "Won back" where the app said "Cracked" and "Mended". The smallest gems grow two pixels (Home 14, the skill page 18). Polished is the cut gem with a sparkle, a plus of pixels off its top right corner, and a faint halo at the end screen's size; the block of white facets is gone (his: "this weird big white mark on the top left"). The hover says "nearly cut" where it said "glowing". He is keeping the dots-only rule open ("I'm definitely still figuring that out"), so squares are earned by the cut gem alone for now. Rive was asked about a third time and not used. Next in this style: the session end with ore that travels as dots and lands as pixels (his pick C of three, https://claude.ai/artifact/6HYu6J4ewi7UEBYW2bvpBD), then the highlight moment. `Gem.tsx` (`FUSE`), test `dot-ring.test.tsx`.
- D237. **The moments after an answer: the exercise's own gem, a reward sized by the memory added, and a second press to rate over a miss** (Tyler, 2026-11-12, after the gem states of D236: "the mole comes in... then he pops back out", a hard recall "should be associated with the strongest visual reward", "if the user says it's good... but they actually got it wrong, wouldn't it be bad to make the gem increase", and of the playable sketch "I like the direction we're going with the moments after an answer"; board https://claude.ai/artifact/5aiVfPzQHUJSwfonWygjAC; amends D21 and D149). (1) At the check step the exercise's gem sits at the start of its timeline, as it is before the rating: a rock, nearly cut, cut, or come loose, with its rim and sparkle. (2) A rating that changes the gem for good gets a moment: the gem turns from what it was into what it is, with the days of memory the rating added (FSRS stability, the end screen's unit) and one line the mole brings. It shows for a gem that is cut, won back, polished, or missed after it was cut, and for a recall that adds three weeks or more to a gem already cut, which is the hard recall after a long gap and so the largest reward. Every other rating gets none, because a rating is given a hundred times a day and the next exercise comes at once (D185). The moment sits over the next exercise for four seconds (six on a miss) and never holds it back. (3) The line is true to the case and never drawn from a bank of praise: how long since he last saw it, that a gem cut once comes back faster (it never promises the exercise returns this session, since a miss that closes the day's goal doesn't), and, when he had said Sure, that a miss he was sure of is the one a correction fixes best (hypercorrection, Butterfield & Metcalfe 2001). (4) His rating is still the one recorded (D21), but Good or Easy two steps over what the marks say (his own marks once he has changed them, the check's until then; a blank answer counts as a miss) takes a second press of the same rating, with the reason said under the buttons. One slip can no longer cut a gem that should have come loose (a second press inside half a second, a double click, doesn't count); a deliberate override costs one key. With nothing to go by yet (the check still running, an answer in his head he hasn't marked) there is no guard: that rating is his alone, as before. Fact exercises, which he rates himself, have no guard. Not built: a running count of days in the head, and a different mole line per subject. `study.ts` (`cardHistory`), `moment.ts`, `Review.tsx`; tests `scheduling.test.ts`, `moment.test.ts`, journey "review: Good over a miss takes a second press".
- D238. **The mole is an old miner: a helmet with a lit lamp and a white moustache** (Tyler, 2026-11-12: "make the mole have a mustache and look almost like an old, wise mole instead of a cute one", after the gruff old-miner voice he is designing for the product video; he had ChatGPT draw six concepts and picked 4, then chose the 22-dot redraw over a 30-dot one for everyday use: "there's something charming about the 22 because it still feels kind of like a classic video game"; page https://claude.ai/artifact/RsNbsWSLAGX4v4oHZPhWHh; amends D169's drawing, keeps D214's puppet). - D239. **At a session's end a stone is cut where it lands: dots while it moves, pixels at rest** (Tyler, 2026-11-12, for the product video's session-end hero moment, picking C of three sketches of the same four seconds, all dots, all 8-bit stepped, or "dots in motion, 8-bit at rest": "C is good"; the bar he set is "as impressive as Duolingo, but in our own style"; adds to D190, in the look of D236). - D241. **The end screen says one thing at a time** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, of the Review end screen: "there's too much text. The ring is 236px and the gems the session cut sit inside it (56px for up to three, 48px for up to six, 32px beyond, with the +N), so the cart's pour (D190, D239) ends in the hero; the mole (D169) stands on it. The count of memories kept is the screen's one hero number, under the emblem, drawn 56px tall in the ring's own fat dots (`DotNumber`; the dot face's one-dot stroke was too thin to carry it) and counting up from nothing once the cart has filled the ring; with no gem cut it stands inside the ring. (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: the session bar is gone, D246.)
- D243. **At a session's end the mole says what it won, and three lines are left under the ring** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, of the new end screen: "It'd be nice to have the mole saying something encouraging. Also, I like edgy... until [we define a character], you can have it be whatever you deem is best, based on the science", and "there's still a lot of text on the bottom... keep it to 2, maybe 3 max, and then have our friend saying something instead"). Once he has come up and cheered, a raised bubble right of his head (the tutor's bubble, D159, mirrored, with its stair tail) says the session's lines one at a time (D241): the verdict first, in place of the headline ("Goal met. Tools down.", "Seam's empty. Today counts.", "Honest work."), then the memory added, the longest gap recalled, the rescued ones, each level reached, the minutes left to the goal and the new exercises held back. Under the ring: the count of memories kept, the next step, Done. His voice (`end-says.ts`, the one place the lines live): an old miner's (D238), short and dry, and only ever what the numbers say. Why that voice: feedback helps when it is specific and about the work or its result, and praise aimed at the person, or praise the result didn't earn, does not (Hattie & Timperley 2007, cited from memory and not re-read for this; Kao 2024, from the house style consult: feedback that follows success helps, plain amplification hurts), so every line names a result, none says "you're great", and nothing is said that isn't true of this session. A session of misses still gets the reason it was worth it, never a jab (D62). Under Reduce Motion the lines still take turns in the bubble, without the roll. The quick quiz has no mole and lists the same lines. Amends D241 (where the lines stand), D242 (the headline label and the lines under the emblem are gone). (Amended 2026-11-13, pointer only: typed out in a dialogue box, D245; at most three lines, D247.)
- D244. **Home lists what he just added with what he is reading** (Tyler, 2026-11-13: "we need to have a way to elegantly favor recently added in home", then picked "One list, newest on top" over its own Recently added section; amends D58, follows D110). Continue reading also holds every source added in the last seven days and not opened yet, each placed by when it was added among the rows by when they were last opened, so the newest touch is on top as in To read.
