- D245. **The mole speaks from an old game's dialogue box: typed out, with his block** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, of the end screen's bubble: "make sure the text animating in is matching the retro video game vibe with the cursor, the font that we pick, and the way the box is styled. I think it should match the Mole's style"). One component, `MoleSays`, for anywhere the mole says a line outside his chat. The box is flat `bg-active` (his chat bubbles' fill, D186) with corners cut in two 3px steps, the same steps as its stair tail (D159), no shadow and no round corner. The words are the mono, 12px, typed a letter at a time (28 ms a letter, a breath after a full stop or comma) with the chat's block cursor riding the last letter; the block blinks once the line is whole, the line holds 2.6 s, and the next is typed from nothing. The whole line is in the page from its first letter, the letters to come only unseen, so words never jump to a new row as they arrive and a reader hears the line whole. Hover or focus holds the line. Under Reduce Motion a line stands at once and the block is still. Typing is a moment's authored motion (D185 allows a moment its own time); it runs only where the mole speaks, never on something done a hundred times a day. Two other boxes were mocked and not built (a dotted outline, the round raised bubble with mono words): `/mnt/project-files/product-video/end-screen/says-board.png`. Amends D241 and D243 (the roll in the bubble is gone; the quick quiz's list keeps it). The tutor's closed-chat bubble (D159) keeps its round raised box until Tyler says the two should match.
- D246. **Nothing counts a Review session up top; the mole says when the day's goal is met and the session flows on; the gem never says what a rating would win; days of memory are capped per exercise** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, on `research/reward-loop-audit.md`: a timer or ring atop Review "would be distracting", "it'd be better to have a notification (like the mole) pop in and say something when the user does meet their daily goal", and of days of memory "very meaningful"; amends D59, D65, D144, D177, D197, D237).
  1. **Header.** Review's bar of steps is gone and nothing replaces it: it measured done / (done + all due), so over a backlog it barely moved and a miss moved it back (audit F1; Villar 2013: a constant progress bar doesn't help and a slow-early one raises drop-off). Undo and the close button stay. The quick quiz keeps its 22px goal ring.
  2. **Goal met.** When a rating closes the day's goal, the mole comes up in the moment's box: "Goal met." over "The rest is extra.", with Done (hover: End the session, Esc). Told once a session, it stays until the next rating, and the next exercise is already on screen: the session no longer ends itself (D65, D144). Esc or Done ends it on the end screen as before. D177 holds: a goal that closes on a miss is told after one likely recall. The quick quiz still ends at the goal. No points and no multiplier after the goal (D23, D62): what came after is said as part of days of memory (point 4).
  3. **Honest rating.** Before he rates, a gem one Good from cut shows as an ordinary rough one, and a Fact exercise, which he grades himself with no check, shows no gem (audit F4; Schweitzer 2004: just short of a goal people overstate; Rawson & Dunlosky 2007). The gem's change still lands after the rating. `ore stats --json` gains `near_cut`: pass rate against FSRS's predicted recall for growing exercises one Good from cut and for the rest, to watch for inflation; nothing in the app shows it.
  4. **Days of memory** (D197's depth). Each kept exercise adds at most 30 days (`DEPTH_CAP_DAYS`) to a session's sum, so one old exercise or an Easy can't make the number (audit F3). The receipt also carries `depth_extra_days`, the part earned after the day's goal closed, for the end screen's line.
  5. **Not built here.** Three sizes of ending, gems in the picker, and then and now follow in their own PRs. The goal note is drawn as the mole and his speech box (D245).
- D247. **The mole says three lines at most at a session's end: the rare things lead with their own mark, days of memory keeps its place, the everyday lines are shuffled** (Tyler, 2026-11-13: "the rare ones show first, always, with an associated icon or color, or both. For when there are common ones, we could just add a little bit of randomness"; amends D243, keeps its "2, maybe 3 max"). Rare, rarest first: a skill's new level (its icon in its colour), a gap of a week or more he recalled across (the clock), memories pulled back just in time (the gold-rimmed gem). Days of memory is always among the three, and says what came after the goal: "+41 days of memory, 12 of them extra" (D246). With nothing rare the verdict opens; with something rare it is one more everyday line. The everyday lines (the verdict then, minutes left to the goal, new exercises waiting) take what room is left in an order drawn once per end screen: which true line shows varies, never whether anything was won (D62: no random reward). A session of misses still opens with why it was worth it. Rejected: a line naming the session's most notable exercise ("sounds like a weird AI slot message"); "N memories kept" stays the hero. Amended 2026-11-14 (Tyler: "Should remove the random ass icon in the text box"): the rare line still leads, in words alone; its mark is gone from the speech box.
- D248. **Then and now: once in four weeks the end screen shows an exercise that beat him weeks ago beside how he answered it today** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, on `research/reward-loop-audit.md` P8: "We could try it, and then I will just critique it as we go... I think it's a good general idea to have a long-term thing like this"; charts and an analytics section are left open, to think through later). Nothing in Ore marked the weeks between a day's gems and a level months away (audit F10), the stretch where a habit is likeliest to lapse. When a session ends, if no then and now was shown in the last 28 days, core looks among the exercises he typed an answer to and passed for one he missed or guessed, also typed, at least 21 days ago, and takes the oldest: the receipt carries `then_now` (the question, his answer then and its date, his answer now). The end screen sets it under the ring: the question over a rule, "4 weeks ago" and that answer in `muted`, "Today" and this one in `text`. The mole points at it first ("4 weeks back that one beat you. Read it now."). His own words are the evidence: nothing is counted, scored or kept as a tally (D23, D62), and a session with no such pair shows nothing and waits for one that has. The quick quiz shows it too, since its session spends the month's turn. To try and to change on his critique.
- D249. **What to review shows what a session could win in each skill, not how much is due** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, agreeing to `research/reward-loop-audit.md` P7; amends D80, D204). A row said "12 exercises ready": the backlog, which never empties (`design/daily-session.md`), read at the moment he decides whether to start. Now a row shows only the gems that are within reach there: those one Good from cut today (the nearly cut gem and a number) and cut ones that came loose (the loose gem and a number), in the skill's colour, the words on hover, literal and looking forward as he asked ("2 gems one good answer from cut", "1 gem to win back"; his pick of four mocks: gem and number over gem and words), and nothing when it has neither. The due count stays for a screen reader. Row order (Focus first, then most due) is unchanged.
- D250. **The iPhone app follows the text size set in iOS (Dynamic Type)** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, F70: "Yes, follow it"). Every font size and line height in the phone build's CSS is multiplied by `--type`, iOS's body size over its default 17pt, measured from `-apple-system-body` and followed live when he changes it. Titles of 28px or more grow about half as much, as iOS's Large Title does (34 → 40 at the largest regular size, 44 at the first accessibility size). Ore follows up to the first accessibility size (28pt body); past it single words broke inside titles and buttons clipped their labels, so the four larger sizes read as that one. Icons, spacing and the Mac app keep their sizes.
- D251. **From 48px up the mole is concept 4 itself, traced dot by dot** (Tyler, 2026-11-13: "adapt the exact number 4 mole", then "let's go for it"; amends D214, D238). The 22-dot miner was a redraw of ChatGPT's concept 4 on a strict grid with flat tones, and it lost what he liked in the drawing: the dented helmet with its drooping brim, the fat slanted lobes, the shading. Those come from dots that sit off the grid, about 30 across, in many greys. So `scripts/mole-trace.py` finds every dot of the drawing (`design/mole-concept-4.png`: place, size, grey) and `mole-fine.ts` draws them where the drawing put them. He moves as a cut-out puppet on the springs and moods the small mole already had (`MOVES` is shared): the head rides and leans, the helmet tips back off a brow and jumps when he is startled, his cheeks lift when he is pleased (each lobe rises and swings out a little from under his nose, over eyes shut to arcs), the paws lift on arms, and a dot under a part above it shrinks away so nothing shows through or pops. Under 48px his dots would be near 1px, so the tutor button, his chat and the Review moment keep the 22-dot mole (`FINE_FROM`); the goal note (48px) and the end of a review (96px) show the traced one. This bends the one-way-of-drawing rule's grid for him alone (his yes). Greys are ten steps from `bg` to `text`, so he follows the theme. Eyes are placed by hand (black on a dark page can't be traced), and the pleased arcs, brows and mouth are drawn, not traced: the drawing has only his rest.
- D253. **The traced mole has fourteen more emotions, with comic marks; none plays in the app until each has a moment that calls for it** (Tyler, 2026-11-13: "make sure the new mole gets new animations for a broad range of emotions"; adds to D214, D251). Nod, shake, wink, laugh, proud, curious, sad, sleepy, set, shy, wave and huff are moves in `MOVES`, with one held pose, `lookdown` (asked for by the end screen, to watch each result land under the ring), on the springs the five first moods use, with seven new things to spring: a wink, a frown's brows, the helmet's height and tilt, the lamp's flare, the moustache's droop and puff. After a first look he asked for more ("add a tear in sad and a sweat bead in anxious or star eyes in ecstatic... kinda anime inspired. Make it exciting for people"): anxious and ecstatic join them, and marks are drawn over him in his own dots: a tear, a bead of sweat, star eyes and sparkles, pink cheeks, steam, Zs, a sparkle on a wink, a "?" when curious, a "!" when startled (the one change to a mood that was already shipped). He also found that "the white dot in his eye goes away in a lot of them and it makes him look a bit spooky": an open eye now keeps its glint in every frame of every move, which `mole.test.ts` checks, and ("the white dot should maybe only be gone if it has eyelids and his eyes are closed") the traced mole's pleased eyes stay open and bright instead of shutting to black arcs, which amends D251's pleased pose; the small mole keeps its arcs. They use only colours he already has (`text`, the greys, the lamp's amber, the nose's pink), so the colour rule stands. Every frame of each is checked by `mole.test.ts` (whole, over the dirt, no part popping in or out, settling back to rest). Purpose rule: a move earns a place on screen by the moment it marks, so this decision ships the moves only, and each use is its own pick. Out of the ground he has a whole body (Tyler on the cart sketch, 2026-11-13: "he needs a torso"; then "just need a sideways profile of him sleeping for the cart roll-in"): moods `nap`, `fall` and `doze`, used only by the cart at the end of a review. The laugh is deep and slow, three heaves ("dwarf vibes"). One brim dot a shade whiter than its neighbours was levelled ("a random dot brighter than the others").
- D252. **The end screen says its numbers in one row of three, and the mole says one line** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, on a recording of three sizes of ending: "I like the idea of the mole only saying one line and instead the achievements [...] below the circle with corresponding icon + font [...] like a video game"; he asked what Duolingo does, picked C of three recordings (one at a time, a list that builds, a row of three), and said the count of memories kept should be one of the three: "so it's just one row of numbers". Amends D242, D243, D247). Duolingo's lesson-complete screen is a character, one headline that changes with what happened, and exactly three results side by side that pop in and stay; nothing rotates. So under the ring there is one row: memories kept, the days of memory they added, and one more place for the rarest true thing of the session (a skill's new level, then memories pulled back in time, then a long gap recalled across, else the gems cut). The numbers are in the ring's dots, land one after another and run up from nothing; the mole says his line once the last is in. A rare result wears colour (its skill's, or the won-back gold) and an everyday one never does, so a big session looks bigger without a second layout. The mole now says only the first of D247's lines, the rarest thing or his verdict, and it stays; the verdict is a corny mining pun, one of a few per case ("Gneiss work.", "Goal met. Quarry on tomorrow.", "Schist happens. Every miss comes back sooner."; Tyler: "try making the wording a corny pun"), still never about the person and still saying why a session of misses was worth it (D62); the big count under the ring, the count standing in the ring, and his second and third lines are gone. Dropped from the full end screen: "N min to today's goal" (the ring shows it). The new exercises a daily limit holds back are still said, in a line under the row (D65). A session of misses has no row: the mole says why it was worth it (D62). The style rule of one dot-drawn hero number a screen bends here to three small ones, with his yes to the look. The quick quiz is unchanged.
- D254. **An ambience radio at the bottom left, the mole's twin: five looped channels, one at a time, off until he picks one** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, thread "Make an Ore ad with ElevenLabs": picked the amber LCD, then its "Scenes" take ("C is so cool"), then "its own circle in bottom left, mirroring our mole friend in bottom right", then "Yes"). Purpose: a steady sound bed for a 10 minute to 1 hour session, so he can stay with the reading or the review instead of reaching for another app. Channels: Cave, Rain, Lamp (a stove and an oil lamp), Deep mine (a far-off working mine) and Focus (a 3 minute lo-fi bed). The button is 52px like the mole's; playing, its channel's icon glows amber (the mole's lamp, `--lamp`, a colour for focus) and a ring of dots breathes with the level; hover shows the channel and its time in dot letters. A click opens the big screen: a dot LCD with a slow scene per channel, its name, time and level, over five pads. ⌥⌘A opens it, 1 to 5 pick while open, ⌘K has Play and Stop. Switching off plays a short power-down (the picture squeezes to a dot). Scenes run only while the screen is open; closed, only the button's ring moves (if that pulls his eye while reading, the ring goes still). The sounds are ElevenLabs output: their terms allow it inside an app but not as loose files, so they never enter this public repo; the build reads them from `~/Library/Application Support/Ore/sounds` (`ORE_SOUNDS` overrides) and bundles them, and a missing one says "no file" instead of failing. Loudness is set once per file by measurement (integrated loudness, one gain each), not by ear. Desktop only. Not yet: a volume control, layering channels, the iPhone.
- D255. **Two endings in three with a cart, the mole rides it in asleep and falls out onto the ring** (Tyler, 2026-11-13: "what if at the end the mole is sleeping in the cart on his back, hands behind his head, and he falls out in a comedy vibe into his position", then "make it more funny and edgy", "he needs a torso lol", "he doesn't have to pause upside down", and on the recording "lol hell yeah", "maybe make it slightly faster". Amends D190, D243). Until now he rose out of the ground after the cart left, which had nothing to do with the cart. Now he lies on the load on his back, drawn side on, whole body (the mole's `doze` mood), snoring; the bucket tips and his bed pours out from under him; the cart rolls off and he does not, asleep on nothing for a beat (`t-3`), awake and running on it for another (`fall`), then head over heels (gravity's time, as the ore falls) onto the ring, where he lands with a squash as the usual mole in his dirt, startled, then cheering. His line covers for it ("Wasn't asleep. Supervising.", "Goal met. Slept right through it."), a few of each kind in `PUNS`; a rare thing still leads, and a day with nothing left due still says it counts. It adds under a second to the ending. The other ending in three he comes up out of the ground as before, so the fall stays a surprise; more entrances (late, riding it standing) are sketched in words only. No cart (nothing new in the ring and no gem, Reduce Motion, the iPhone, the quick quiz): no ride. The whole body is only ever seen on the cart and in the air. (Amended 2026-11-13, his drawing only: asleep on the cart he is stout, a belly like a hill higher than his nose and two big flat feet with no legs; Tyler: "mega stout so it's funny", "no legs just feet", then "a bit less fat and feet bigger and flat".) (Amended 2026-11-13: while the ore pours he slides slowly down the tipped bucket and off its lip, Tyler: "he should be sliding slowly off the cart before fully falling"; and he falls into his hole, his body under the dirt line never drawn, so the ring no longer crosses his belly, F94.) (Amended 2026-11-13 after four rounds of independent review, Tyler: "definitely rough around the edges. Need it refined": the cart backs out the way it came instead of rolling through him; he hangs asleep in the air, braces, starts awake up and over his hole, dives into it head first, the hole stays empty a beat, and he pops back up with his helmet askew before he speaks. He no longer lands on the ring with a squash.) Technique pass (Tyler, 2026-11-13, "the stuff I see online is often a bit more advanced", picked "Technique pass + checklist skill"): the fall gets weight without a new library. He is pressed short in the brace and pulled long going up and in the dive (`pull` on `.cart-mole-body`, area kept); diving he is the `dive` mood, arms swept in and helmet pushed to his brows by the air, where starting up it floats off them a beat late; as his head meets the dirt it flies (`DIRT`, on gravity, back down to the line) and the ring's nearest dots are knocked down and spring back; the cart hops as his weight leaves it. The bucket never turns through him (F155): tipped, he lies along its rim at the bucket's own 58° and slides down it past the lip, and his dots and the bucket's are backed with the page (`#cart-solid`), so neither shows through the other. Second pass: he starts awake up an arc (up first, then across over his hole) in place of a straight line, and at the top he rocks, runs and sinks (`THRASH`) with one last grab upward before the dive. Third pass (Tyler, 2026-11-14, after comparing it blind with a Rive build: "Neither should have Mole go up quite as much before just sliding into the main spot"): the start up is 32px, not 62 and an arc; awake he stares, looks down, runs on air across to over his hole (`THRASH`), stops, and drops feet first in place of the head-first dive. Fourth pass (Tyler, 2026-11-14: "the code is still actually better", Rive tabled; he liked its cart and "how his hands go up when he's falling"): the brake skid, the load sliding to the low lip before it falls, and arms straight up in the drop are carried into the code version. Fifth pass (Tyler, 2026-11-14: "rive's cart. also the the line above the circle should not appear so early"): the cart is redrawn bigger in our dots (27 wide, a deep bucket, a heap of up to thirty pieces, spoked wheels), and the dirt line of his hole shows only once he lands in it. Sixth pass (Tyler, 2026-11-14: "add a little bit of dirt around where the mole is popping out, so it looks like he's popping out of a hole, like diglit [...] pop in or transition in elegantly as he lands [...] him as a whole should be a little bit higher as to not overlap with the top of the rings"): a mound of clods around his hole on every end screen, heaping up as he lands, and he sits 9px higher, clear of the ring. Seventh pass (Tyler, 2026-11-14: "He's just floating in the air for a while, sleeping. It looks weird"): asleep on nothing lasts 0.2 s, the lip's flick and one sag, then he braces and starts awake as the cart leaves (it was 0.7 s).
- D256. **On the iPhone a source's title takes two lines before it cuts, and Today's reading dots sit under the picture** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, picked C of four mocks: https://claude.ai/artifact/MhPCEdEsw9nqorb8k8iht1). Today's Continue reading rows gave the title what the 64px picture and the 64px dots left, about 25 letters; now the title has the row's width beside the picture on up to two lines (about 65 letters), and the dots, or New, sit under the picture, as the Mac's rows put progress under the picture. The Sources list and a skill's lists wrap their titles to two lines too. A row grows only when its title needs the second line, so the largest iOS text size (D250) still shows two lines. Readers like Pocket, the NYT and ElevenReader wrap titles the same way; Readwise Reader is not on Mobbin.
- D257. **The glint: a light crosses the dots once, at an earned moment** (Tyler, 2026-11-13: "add a bit more subtle premium flare to the app as a whole by having the dots shimmer across at certain times", for the ElevenLabs video; he pointed at the shadcn catalogs). Taken from Magic UI's shiny text (one band of light passing once), redrawn in the house style (D183): no gradient is laid over anything, each dot goes white and swells a quarter in its turn. Purpose: it marks the instant something was earned, so it plays only there and only once. On the session end screen: round the goal ring with its burst when this session closed the day's goal, across the field of dots behind the emblem at the same moment, and across each result number as it finishes counting (D252). Not on Home's ring, which he sees many times a day, and never as a loop. A dot's turn is `--i` on a `.glint` drawing; its time is `--t-4` (D185). None under Reduce Motion. The peak is plain white: a `color-mix` or a `var()` as a keyframe's `fill` drew black in Chromium, and `currentColor` there crashed it.
- D258. **The mole's pose fits the moment he is already in, sized to how rare it is** (Tyler, 2026-11-13: "Yes" to giving the new poses their moments, then "let's not force every moment to be in there? But the moments we do have that fit, yes utilize him". Amends D237, D243, D246). No new place for him: only the three he already had. After a rating that earns a moment he is `proud` for a gem cut, `laugh` for one won back (the hardest thing in a session, so the biggest pose), `wink` for one polished, `surprised` for a long gap, `set` for a miss on a cut gem (steady, never sad, D62); he is 48px there now, up from 40, since the traced mole that can show these starts at 48. The goal note waves once. On the end screen his pose is for the line he says: the hop is a new level's alone, `curious` for then and now, `surprised` for a long gap, `proud` for memories pulled back, `set` for a session of misses, `happy` for a gem cut or the goal met, else a `nod`. Until now every ending with something kept got the same hop, so the hop said nothing. Left out on purpose: `sad`, `shake`, `huff`, `anxious` (a miss never gets guilt), `sleepy` and `shy` (no moment calls for them), `ecstatic` (nothing in a session is bigger than a new level), a wave on Done (it would add a wait), and him watching the results land (the row lands while he arrives; making it wait for him lengthens the ending, his call).
- D261. **One mole: the traced drawing at every size** (Tyler, 2026-11-13: the small mole "looks mushy"; shown three hand-drawn small moles beside the traced one shrunk to 40px, "the big one, shrunk seems to look fine to me?", then his pick "Yes, big mole everywhere"; amends D251's "from 48px up", retires the 22-dot drawing of D169, D214 and D238). The 22-dot mole was mushy at 40px because its dots fell 1.82px apart, never on whole pixels, with halftone sizes on top. The traced mole at 40px is finer than a pixel, so it reads as a small shaded picture, which he prefers to a third drawing. So `Mole` draws `fineDots` at any size and the small sprite, its tones and its tests are gone: one drawing to keep, and every emotion (D253) works in the button and the chat too. Given up: the hop and the pleased moustache are subtler at 40px, and on a non-Retina screen he is softer.
- D259. **The end screen's results each stand beside a shaded dot object: a gem, a calendar, a comet** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, thread "Elevate the end screen result icons", for the ElevenLabs video: the 20px sprites over the numbers were "low quality" and badly sized; four redraws of them were "thinking too narrowly", so a board of reward screens from Mobbin came first: https://claude.ai/artifact/WCy8gVkbrAJzsR4DMyp2mP). Top reward screens rarely set a small icon over a number; of five directions drawn from them he picked Objects ("looks the best"), with "the numbers and icons in the same vertical plane rather than stacked". Purpose: each mark says at a glance what its number counts, and makes the result feel won. Memories kept is a cut gem. Days or months of memory is a calendar page with its later days lit (his idea; the hourglass first drawn "doesn't feel like gaining, it feels like countdown"). A long gap recalled across is a comet, from far off and still bright (his pick of four, then "more obviously like a comet": a small head, a tail in three streaks). Each is a 16 by 16 sprite at 48px in `EndMark.tsx`, a digit per cell for how lit it is, so a dot grows and brightens with the light (the halftone of D183); they were sampled from shaded vector drawings and are edited by hand from here. The mark stands left of its 28px `DotNumber`, 10px apart, the words under both; a tile is 140px wide. The rarer results keep their own drawings at the new size: the cut gem and the gold-rimmed gem at 32px (D236), a skill's icon at 40px. Amends D252 (a check, the clock and 16px gems over the number).
- D260. **The end screen's result marks loop, each the way the thing would move** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, picked "each its own" of three mocked loops, over one wave across all three and a twinkle): the gem's faces catch a passing light, the calendar's days light one by one, the comet's tail streams off its head, for as long as the end screen is open and from the moment each tile lands. It is the app's one endless loop, kept to this screen because it is seen once a session and is the reward; the glint (D257) stays play-once on the numbers. Dots swell and brighten in place (transform and opacity only), and nothing moves under Reduce Motion. `EndMark.tsx`, `end-*` keyframes in `styles.css`, DESIGN.md "Motion".
- D262. **On the iPhone a source's picture is as big as its row, and Today's reading dots sit on the picture's foot** (Tyler, 2026-11-13: "the progress dots need to be overlaid on the thumbnails, because right now we're sacrificing too much real estate for them, and the thumbnails should probably be maximized in general"; his pick A of four mocks, https://claude.ai/artifact/BRD7i39iNrK4nH7EoM4Dnx; amends D256's dots under the picture). Today's Continue reading picture goes from 64 to 112 pt wide (16:9), the height of a two-line title's row, and how far he is shows as the same ten round dots (D183), 6 pt each, laid over the picture's foot on a shade that fades up from dark, lit dots white: readable on a white page and a dark picture alike, taking no room of their own. A source not opened yet says New in a dark tag in the same corner. The Sources list and a skill's lists show no progress; their pictures go from 48 to 80 pt wide in the same 60 pt row. The Mac is unchanged. Not picked: a line of 27 fine dots on the picture's bottom edge, the same line fixed under the picture, a sideways shelf of big pictures.
- D263. **The radio's playing button is a named motion, the lamp, and Ore has one page of brand guidelines** (Tyler, 2026-11-14: "I love the animation of the radio when it's on in the bottom-left corner. Can we add more of that, or just solidify that in the style? I would like to make official brand guidelines"). The lamp says a thing is running right now: its dots turn amber (`--lamp`) and swell and brighten in place with a real signal, nothing travels, and off it is grey and still. Amber never means progress or a reward. Only the radio uses it; another place needs something really running, a signal to follow, a spot out of the reading column and Tyler's yes (the purpose rule). The rule is in `DESIGN.md` "Motion". The brand guidelines are `docs/design/brand.md` (feel, voice, eight rules, colour, type, motion, the mole, the mark), a digest of `DESIGN.md`, which stays the spec and is laid out in the open DESIGN.md format. The browsable version is the Ore design system page Claude uses for decks and mocks; `apps/desktop/scripts/design-system/build.ts` rebuilds it from the stylesheets and the real components, because the hand-drawn one of 2026-11-07 was wrong within a week.
- D264. **The lamp lights two more running things: Listen and the tutor's mole** (Tyler, 2026-11-13, "yes" to three places offered after D263). Reading aloud: the pause button's icon turns amber inside a ring of dots that swells with each word the Mac's voice begins (the utterance's boundary event; WebKit gives no sound level for speech). The tutor: while a reply is written the mole's helmet lamp is lit, its dots bigger with five short rays, brighter each time words arrive, on the button and in the chat. Both are grey and still when nothing runs, and still under Reduce Motion. One build: `Lamp.tsx` holds the ring the radio draws too, and `pulse`/`glow` for a thing with no level to read. The third place he said yes to, a playing video or podcast's row while he is on another screen, is not built: a recording stops when he leaves its source (D167), so nothing is running to light; and it stays that way (Tyler, 2026-11-14: no background audio, the point of the app is to focus on the source). The end screen's result marks stay white with their own loops (D260): amber means running now, never a reward.
- D265. **The "ore_" wordmark is retired** (Tyler, 2026-11-14, on the design system page's cover: "This looks weird. What's with the green line?", then his pick of three: retire it; amends D129's wordmark and D221's last line). Where the name is written it is a plain "Ore" in Geist beside the pickaxe icon. Why: the mark was drawn for the app icon, which became the pickaxe (D221); no screen shows it, its font is used nowhere else, and its underscore hangs below the letters. Not picked: lifting the underscore to the baseline, keeping it as it was.
- D273. **PDF tables and display formulas are read by Granite-Docling, so every model Ore downloads keeps Ore free to sell as open source** (Tyler, 2026-11-14). Rule: a new dependency, and any model weights Ore downloads, must carry a licence that keeps selling Ore open; permissive ones (MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD) are preferred. D125's reader used Docling's standard models: its layout model (Apache-2.0) with TableFormer and CodeFormulaV2, both CDLA-Permissive-2.0, a data licence D120's list doesn't name (F149). The optional Mac download now gets Docling 2.142.0 with its VLM extra (mlx, mlx-vlm: MIT; transformers: Apache-2.0) and one model, granite-docling-258M (Apache-2.0; its MLX build on Apple silicon), run through Docling's VLM pipeline; the CDLA models are no longer downloaded. Rescored first: the arXiv papers of D125's bake-off weren't on the Mac, so the same scorer (`packages/core/eval/pdf-structure`) ran on two pages written for the test in Typst (a two-column page of 8 display formulas and 2 tables, one with spanned headers; a page of 6 harder formulas, cases, a matrix, sums, and 2 tables) plus `tables.pdf`, `inline-math.pdf` and `dense.pdf`: 17 formulas, 6 tables, keys from their sources. In Ore's own doc.md (pdf.js text, the reader's regions swapped in) the standard models scored formulas 96%, tables 92%; Granite-Docling 96% and 94%. On a one-page PDF it took 7 to 14 s with the model's load against 24 to 234 s (the formula model runs on the CPU), and its model is about 600 MB against about 1.2 GB; the whole download stays about 2 GB. Granite places a whole table but not its cells, so a cell takes pdf.js's own items that spell the same letters and digits (powers and bold kept), else the model's text; equation numbers it writes after a space become `\tag`. Named losses: a header set over two rows stays two rows (the model marks no header cells), a cell of only a dash takes the model's hyphen, and the bake-off papers themselves are still to be rescored when they are next on the Mac (`docs/research/brief-pdf-inline-math.md`). Amended by D-new-paddle-pages: the reader is PaddleOCR-VL, on every PDF, and it marks headings too.

- D266. **The mole's speech box is a hairline outline, as in the Rive cart ending** (Tyler, 2026-11-14, having played the cart ending built in code beside the one built in Rive: "I like the speech box of Rive's better"). The Rive version's box was a 1px outline with 6px corners on the page's own ground, no fill, no tail, the line in 13px mono, as wide as its words. `MoleSays` takes that look everywhere it is used (the end screen, the goal note in Review): `border` hairline on `bg`, 6px corners, 13px mono, no stepped corners, no stair tail; on the end screen the box is as wide as its line, up to 248px, so a short line is one row. Kept from D245, because he asked for them by name there and the Rive box simply had no motion: the letters typed one at a time and the block cursor after the last, with 10px more room on the right so the block never touches the edge. Amends D245 (the box's fill, corners and tail). The tutor's closed-chat bubble (D159) is still its own round raised box.
- D267. **On the end screen the mole never says a number the row under the ring already shows** (Tyler, 2026-11-14, on Impeccable round 7's "the mole's line repeats the third result": "yes"; amends D252 and D247). When the session's rarest thing is the row's third number (a new level, memories pulled back, a long gap), he gives his verdict instead; a rare thing the row has no room for, and then and now, are still his to say. His pose stays the one for the line he says (D258). The quick quiz, which has no row, lists its lines as before. F138.
- D268. **On the iPhone, Review's buttons sit at the foot of the screen** (Tyler, 2026-11-14, on round 7's "the rating buttons sit mid-screen with the bottom 40% empty": "yes"). How sure he is, on an exercise he answers in his head, and the four ratings with the memory line once the answer is out, rest above the home indicator, where Apple puts an app's main actions; a long answer scrolls and they follow it. A typed answer keeps its buttons under the field, which the keyboard lifts to the thumb. What the last answer won (D237) stands above them instead of over them. The Mac is unchanged. F137.
- D269. **⌘K lists an ambience channel only once he types** (Tyler, 2026-11-14, on round 7's "five of the first nine rows are Play Cave, Rain, Lamp…": "sure"; amends D254, which keeps Play and Stop in ⌘K). "Ambience…" and, while one plays, "Stop ambience" stay in the list ⌘K opens on. F136.
- D270. **Review's key bar names Retire, and the memory line ends with when the next review falls** (Tyler, 2026-11-14, on round 7: "sure"; adds to D149). Retire (Delete on an exercise never reviewed) joins Ask, Edit and Later in the bar once the answer is out. The line over the ratings ends with faint words, "next in 3 weeks", that follow the rating under the pointer as its hollow dot does. F134.
- D271. **What a rating won is a chip in Review's head bar: the gem, then the days in dots** (Tyler, 2026-11-14, on round 7: "The wording needs to be concise... bigger emphasis on the + days", then of a restyled box "it looks like you just randomly place these things. Rethink the position of everything", picking the chip from three placements mocked against Speak, Duolingo and Oura; changes D237's box and its sentences, and D268's box above the buttons). The chip sits in the head bar, where nothing else is: centred on the Mac with the mole peeking in (D258's poses stay), at the left on the iPhone and the quick quiz. It reads gem, "+49" in the end screen's dot numbers, "days", and on the Mac a word or two after: "Cut · last seen 18 days ago", "Won back", "Polished", "Came loose". The iPhone and the quick quiz show the words only when there are no days (a miss). The sentences are gone, the one after a sure miss with them. F140.
- D272. **The iPhone skill header is one line: the rocks in the order they grow, into a small level ring and the cut count** (Tyler, 2026-11-14, on round 7: "a lot of wasted space here", then of two tighter mocks "the whole point is for the evolution of the rocks... the leftmost is the starter one, and then they graduate until the 19 shows on the right", picking Y1 of three; changes D90's ring and D228's header on the iPhone only). Under the goal the line reads rough, missed, nearly cut (each with its count, none drawn at zero), a dotted path between, then the cut gem in a 28 pt level ring, the cut count and "Lv 2". The ring stays because how near the next level is, and what today's review would add to it, is the part that moves him to review (the goal-gradient effect); the 56 pt ring and the count beside it are the Mac's. A tap still opens the breakdown. The importance bars sit at the right of the name. F141.
- D274. **The end screen's ring holds the day's minutes, and minutes past the goal go round again** (Tyler, 2026-11-14: "if the user goes beyond that, it'd be cool for the colors to keep going around and amplify even further... Maybe the time in minutes counts up alongside it in one of our hero fonts in the center"; picked over seven rounds of mocks and recordings, then "PASS"). Before, the ring stopped at full, so a 16 minute day and a 10 minute day ended the same, and the minutes stood nowhere on the full end screen. Now the ring's centre is the day's minutes in the ring's dots, 56px, over "min" and, on a day past the goal, a green chip "+N" (minutes past the goal; its hint says so), the pair centred. The ring fills at one pace straight through the goal; crossing it throws a ring of dots outward once (bigger and white at twice the goal: "the moment pop you did was pretty good"), and the bonus stretch is drawn on the same dots, each a touch bigger per lap (1.2x, then 1.34x) with a white centre (bigger on a third lap), so where the bonus ends is plain at rest. What he turned down, each for a reason that stands: a lighter lap, a darker lap ("not bonus"), twinkling dots and size alone ("you can't tell where the thing ends"), a number tucked under the gems. White sparkles pop over the bonus stretch only, a few past the goal and more past twice. Under the minutes one line rolls once through the session's skills, fewest to most, and rests on the most: the skill's icon, its name and the memories the session kept in it (`kept_skills` on the end receipt), never points (D62: these are real minutes and real memories). The gems the cart pours are still cut in the ring (D239), then make way for the minutes (his pick of three: cut then make way, small under the skill, dropped); under Reduce Motion the settled screen shows the minutes at once and the gems only as their count in the row. The mole's dirt now stands 4px clear of the ring ("make sure the mole bottom doesn't overlap w the ring"). The glint and burst at a met goal (D257) are for a goal the count did not cross on screen; when it crosses in the count, the thrown dots are the moment. The sparkles loop at rest as the end marks do (D260). Sound (a tick per bonus minute, a hit at the goal) follows in its own change. The quick quiz keeps its small ring. Amends D242 (the gems' place in the ring, the mole's dirt), D252 (a fourth dot number on the screen, with his yes), D257.
- D275. **On the iPhone, shake and a three-finger swipe walk the app's history** (Tyler, 2026-11-14: "for iOS use shake and 3 finger swipe", on F161). A touch screen had no way to the window's history (D82): highlights, Key points and notes join it quietly, with no toast to tap. Now the iOS gestures reach it, as in Notes and Mail: shake brings up UIKit's own Undo alert naming the step (with Redo when there is one), a three-finger swipe left undoes and right redoes. The shell answers UIKit's undo manager with one that mirrors the history (`apps/phone/ios/Undo.swift`, told the next step each way by `phone/native-undo.ts`), so the gestures take the same steps ⌘Z does; in a field with typing of its own, the field's undo goes first, as `ownsUndo` does for ⌘Z. No button or toast was added (the purpose rule).
- D276. **Where he looks to understand a number, the word stands beside it; and the paper primary is flat everywhere** (Tyler, 2026-11-14, on Impeccable round 8's four mocks: "try the fixes but make sure whatever we do works against edge cases"; amends D90, D179 and D100). Help had scored 2 of 4 for eight rounds because meanings were a hover away. Three places now say it in words, each chosen because he is already looking there and there is room (D249's picker, where words overflowed, stays as it is): (1) the skill page's gem breakdown puts a word after each count, "1 missed · 2 rough · 1 new" (`GEM_WORD`: polished, won back, cut, nearly cut, missed, rough, new; never a noun, so no plural to get wrong; the hover keeps the full line); (2) the reader's chip reads "Reading for 3 questions" ("1 question" for one); (3) Finish's scope field reads "Skip a part of this source? Like “the coding part”" in place of "Leave anything out?", which under the recall box read as a question about his recall (the iPhone's 320pt field says "Skip a part? Like “the coding part”"; a screen reader hears the full name on both). And the paper primary has no lip anywhere: Check, Save, Done and Skip to questions join Home's and the picker's flat rule, so the 3px lip means one thing, a button he answers with (how sure, the ratings). Checked at the worst case: all seven gem states with five-digit counts wrap to four clean rows in the 320px popover; the chip with 12 questions beside three chips at 128 fits from 1280 down to 900 wide, and under that the bar already hides its chips; the buttons at rest, hover, pressed, disabled and keyboard focus (which turned up F189: the ring now sits outside a paper button, where it can be seen).
- D277. **Review has sound: one short sound on each key moment, and nowhere else** (its rule, "one sound on a key moment and nowhere else", is replaced by D336; the sounds, files and switches below stand) (Tyler, 2026-11-14 to 11-15: "let's start w the key moments satisfaction adds delight and make sure we don't do it for annoying places like undo"; "Show answer ... should be like a pickaxe sound somehow and the rating should correspond to the gem analogy"; then "do [it] with your best guess and I'll have you edit as I use it"). Seven moments, seven files (`src/sfx.ts`): showing the answer is a pickaxe tick; Hard, Good or Easy a gem's shine and Again a gem chipping, played once the rating is saved (one that fails to save is silent); the day's goal closing mid-session a chime, half a second after the rating's own sound; on the end screen the cart's roll as it comes in, a jingle as the ring fills and a pop as the mole comes up. Recalls in a row play the shine a semitone higher each, up to four, and a miss starts it over (Duolingo's run of right answers; Tyler's yes to "streak steps", 2026-11-14): it is heard only, never counted, shown or kept past the session, so D23 stands. Nothing sounds on undo, Later, an edit, a mark, a key in the reader or any other action; `test/sfx.test.ts` holds the list of calls. The sounds are picked from the ElevenLabs sounds Tyler saved (his villager mining, glowing twinkle, mystical reveal, toon pop and epic reveal) and two made for Ore (the chip, the cart): code-made tones were turned down twice. Like the radio's (D254) they are ElevenLabs output, so they stay out of git and the build takes them from `~/Library/Application Support/Ore/sounds` as `fx-<moment>.mp3` (copies and the table of which is which: the project's `house-style/app-sounds/`); a missing file is silent, and the journeys play their own tones. Settings › Review › Sound effects turns them off (on by default, kept on this Mac); they play at 60% under whatever else is on, and the Mac's own mute silences them. Mac only for now: the iPhone build carries no sound files.
- D278. **On a miss the mole nods, he never frowns** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, on a clip of the `set` pose after a missed exercise: "Yeah he looks mad at me"). Amends D258: a miss on a cut gem, and an end screen where nothing was kept, now show `nod` (two soft dips, no lowered brows) in place of `set`. A miss is the most useful moment in a session, so the one face he sees then must say "noted, it comes back", never a telling off (D62). `set` stays drawn but has no moment in Review.
- D279. **What he finished this week gets room in the next day's new exercises** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, "Save room for it", on F199; amends D176.2). The rehearsal on his library found the exercises drafted at a finish waiting behind 18 older drafts, because his own work always came first and only ten new come in a day (D60, D144). Now the exercises of a source finished in the last 7 days take up to half of the day's new places first, the freshest finished source first (then D176.2's order within it); his own older work fills the rest, in D176.2's order. No FSRS math changes (D22).
- D280. **Menus and popups grow from what he pressed** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, round 5 of free shadcn picks: "all"; from Fluid Functionalism and Interior, MIT). A menu, popover or right-click menu opened with the pointer scales from 0.96 and fades in over `--t-3`, its origin on the side Radix placed it (the pointer's spot for a right-click), and goes at once: an exit animation gated on the keyboard could be stranded open by a key pressed while it played (F200). Opened or closed from the keyboard it appears and goes at once: `input-mode.ts` keeps `data-keys` on the page while his last input was a key (a modifier held for a click does not count). Submenus open at hover speed, so they never move. Right-click and ⋯ menus open many times a day, so this is an exception to the motion rule's "100-times-a-day actions don't animate", made by Tyler's pick (CLAUDE.md lists it). Off under Reduce Motion. Why: a layer that comes out of the thing he pressed reads as part of it; Ore's own animation standard asked for origin-aware popovers and nothing had any entrance. `test/input-mode.test.ts`.
- D281. **A list fades its last row while more waits below** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, round 5: "all"; Dice UI's scroller, MIT). The sidebar, every object menu and the palette's results fade their foot over 32px (the sidebar 56px, at the window's foot) only while rows are hidden below, and stop fading at the end. `more.ts` sets `data-more` from the scroll position, the area's size and its rows; the fade is a sticky strip the surface's colour, so it never takes a click. Why: a hard cut at the foot of a list says nothing; the fade says there is more without a scrollbar. `test/more.test.ts`.
- D282. **Five small things a Mac app does** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, round 5: "all"). 1. Hovering a title or name cut off by an ellipsis shows the whole of it laid over the cut text after the hint delay, in its own font, as Finder's expansion tooltip does; nothing when it fits (`full-text.ts`). 2. The player's controls take a click 4px above and below what they draw (up and down only, so neighbours side by side never take each other's clicks; not every small button, since stacked ones would take each other's). 3. A submenu's first row sits level with the row that opened it (`alignOffset` -5: the list's padding and edge). 4. Settings' pop-up menus open with the ticked choice over the button, as a Mac pop-up button does, kept 8px inside the window at both ends, measured with D280's grow-in undone; a quick click leaves it open, as the Mac's does, instead of picking the row the shift put under the pointer (`pop-up.ts`). 5. Playback speed dragged within 0.05 of a preset lands on it, so 1× is one loose drag away; the arrow keys step 0.05 freely (`rate.ts`). `test/mac-feel.test.ts`.
- D283. **Picked sources read as one block, with a bar to act on them** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, round 5: "all"; Fluid Functionalism's merged selection and Dice UI's action bar, MIT; Finder and Linear). Picked rows that touch join into one fill, round only at the run's outer corners. While two or more sources are picked in a list, a bar at the window's foot (the toast's surface; toasts stand above it) says how many and offers Done (To read for a pick already done, Restore for an archived one), Skills (the skills, ticked where every picked source has one) and Archive, with × to let go of the pick. They are the pick's ⋯ menu's own actions, with its Undo. Why: a pick could be acted on only by right-click, ⋯ on a row or keys he had to remember; nothing on screen said so. `PickBar.tsx`, `test/pick-bar.test.tsx`.
- D284. **A source that leaves a list closes its gap** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, round 5: "all"; Keyframery's list-cut, MIT; Things, Linear). Archiving, moving to Done or filing a source out of the list it is in makes the rows below glide up into its place over `--t-4` (60ms after it goes); one that comes back with Undo rises 8px into its place over `--t-3` while the rows below make room. Only rows in view move, and only when a few rows left or a few came back, never both: a new tab, skill or filter re-lays the list at once. From the keyboard (⇧⌘A, ⌘Z) it stays instant, as D280's menus do: the rule that nothing moves for a keyboard action stands until Tyler says otherwise. Off under Reduce Motion. Why: a list that jumps loses his place; the glide shows which row went and where Undo puts it back. `glide.ts`, `test/glide.test.ts`.
- D285. **A video's or recording's bar is cut at its sections and names them on hover** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, round 5: "all"; Limeplay's chapter scrubber, MIT; YouTube's chapters). The outline's outermost headings that the transcript speaks (a YouTube video's chapters, D48) cut the bar with a gap of the page's colour where each starts; hovering the bar shows the time under the pointer and that section's heading, kept inside the bar's ends, and the bar thickens to 5px while the pointer is on it. His highlight ticks draw over a cut. A heading of a page's own text above its video (D92) is left out: its next spoken word is far off. With no headings the bar is as before, with the time only. Why: today the bar says nothing until he clicks; the heading lets him jump back to a part of a talk by name. `sections.ts`, `test/scrub-sections.test.ts`.
- D286. **All sources narrows by kind, skill and author** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, round 5: "all"; bazza/ui's data-table filter, MIT, which copies Linear's). A Filter button left of the search opens a list of fields; picking one lists its values with how many sources each holds, most first, with a search, and picking a value keeps the list open so several can be picked (⌫ in an empty search goes back to the fields). Values in one field widen the list, fields narrow it together. Each field filtered shows as a chip under the tabs: its name and values open its list again, × lets it go, Clear lets all go. Unfiled sources are a skill value of their own, No skill. Filters are let go on leaving All sources for a skill. A filtered tab with nothing in it says so, with Clear them. "Has highlights", pitched with it, waits: the list does not know a source's highlights without reading each one. Why: once the library is in the hundreds, the text search finds a title he remembers, not "the videos I haven't filed". `filters.ts`, `FilterBar.tsx`, `test/source-filters.test.ts`.
- D287. **Ten places where the app broke its own style rules: five rules change, five places are fixed** (Tyler, 2026-11-14, after a stress test of the brand guidelines: "Let's just question the rules... I have a feeling some rules we can maybe just tweak or remove", then his call on each of ten today/enforced pairs). The rules that change: (1) *Flat* keeps two exceptions: the lamp glows ("I like the glow here we can change the rule"), and a key he presses to answer keeps its lip (the answer buttons, the radio's pads). (2) "Nothing drawn in dots slides or spins" is gone ("I don't think we need it at all"): the cart, its wheels and the ore he picked had always broken it; the lamp still stays in place because that is what the lamp is (D263). (3) One hero number per screen stays, with the end screen's row of results (D252) named as its exception (the agent's reading of his answer on the same pair). (4) The 4px grid becomes steps of 2px: nearly half the app's spacing was 6, 10 or 14px and no eye could tell. The app is fixed where the rule was right: the sidebar arrow, the tick and cross and the waiting matrix are round dots, no longer square pixels (D183 now holds everywhere but a cut gem); no gem, ring or badge glows (the finishing ring's shadow, rich ore's, the polished gem's halo and the quiz badge's halo are gone); a source's type is in sentence case ("Article", not "ARTICLE"); on a failed feed Try again is the one main button and Unfollow a quiet one; the quick quiz's end title says what happened ("5 answered", "Goal met") instead of "Nice work". The mole's speech box loses its stepped edge in its new outline look (built apart, with the Rive cart ending's box), and the tail of his closed-chat bubble (D159) is a smooth wedge, no longer a stair. `brand.md` gains how a dot drawing is built and loses its "Borrowed from" and "Formats" sections.
- D288. **A filter is a tag inside the search field** (Tyler, 2026-11-15: "make the filters more intuitive and less visually intrusive", then picked B of three mocks: chips in the bar, tags in the search, one faint line; and the tags in the mock read cramped, with an × too big). Amends D286: the chips row under the tabs and its Clear go. Each field filtered is a tag at the search field's start, its values only (the field's name is in its label for a screen reader), with room either side of the words and a small × of its own (`XSmall`, the X at half its span, since every sprite draws at 20px); the faint word Filter at the field's end opens the fields. A list closing hands the keys back to the search, and ⌫ in the empty search takes the last tag off, as Raycast's and Linear's token fields do. Why: one place narrows the list, so there is no extra row or button to read past, and a tag in the field he types in can't be missed while it hides sources. `FilterBar.tsx`, journey "a filter is a tag in the search".
- D289. **A post reads beside the Feed** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, round 5 pick 7: of two mocks he chose "B: sidebar folds away"; Mail, Readwise Reader, Linear's split). A post opened from the Feed reads to the right of the list instead of over it: the list becomes a 320px column at the left, over a rule, each row its picture, its title on up to two lines and its feed and day under it, the open post's row filled; the sidebar folds away while it is open (⇧⌘S still shows it). J and K open the next and previous post, from the list or the post, except on a video, where they stay the player's; while the list holds the keys as the next post loads, the post takes them when it shows. A post read while the column is up stays in Unread while he is on the Feed, so J and K keep their place, and Esc gives the whole list back with the keys on the post just read. Under 1000px the column is 260px. The Feed keeps its place in the tree through both, so its view and scroll stay. Why: reading through a feed was open, Esc, find the next, open; now it is one key per post with the list in sight. `App.tsx` (`feedSplit`), `Feed.tsx` (`reading`), journey "a post reads beside the list".
- D290. **The tutor reads the whole source he has open** (amended 2026-11-20 by D338: once per chat, not with each message) (Tyler, 2026-11-15, of nine ranked suggestions from a pass on AI chat best practices: "do all"). The open source's passages all go with each message, in order, up to 15,000 words (`WHOLE_WORDS`, about 20k tokens); a few of the library's best still follow, so "how does this connect to…" has them. Before, it got the 8 passages sharing his words, or the first 8 when none did, so "Summarize this", "What's this about?" and Practice saw 16% of a 4,000-word article (F261). Past the limit the tutor is told it is partial (`reading.whole` false), gets the headings as `outline`, and passages that match, or ones from across the source when none do; it then says what it is answering from. Anthropic's own guidance for anything under 200,000 tokens is to send it whole. Sources he @-mentions keep their best passages only. Code: `tutorPassages`, `readingOf` in `packages/core/src/ai/tutor.ts`; prompt tutor@15.
- D291. **A citation that fails its check is mended, never a reason to write the reply again** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, of nine ranked suggestions from a pass on AI chat best practices: "do all"). A quote from his sources that isn't word for word moves to the sentence of its passage that says it (the one holding most of its words, at least three and 60%, `nearestSentence`). One with no such sentence, or naming a passage that wasn't given, goes with its marker, the markers left keep their order, and the reply is marked as not all from his sources. Before, any miss reran the whole turn: the words on screen started over, and a second miss showed an error in place of the answer (F262). A web page keeps the stricter rule (D137): a link the search never returned is a made-up source and goes back once; a web quote a few words off is moved the same way. Broken structure (a marker with no citation, a bad exercise) still goes back.
- D292. **An AI task reads its own files and nothing else** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, of nine ranked suggestions from a pass on AI chat best practices: "do all"). `claude` was given `Read`, `Grep` and `Glob` allowed bare whenever a task named files, and the tutor has web search and fetch beside them, while reading text Ore didn't write: with a file attached, a page with hidden instructions could have it open any file on the Mac and send it out in an address (F263; checked against the real program). Now `Read` is allowed by real path (`claudeTools` in `ai/adapter.ts`): a task that can reach the web reads its files alone, with no search tools, and the tutor starts in `chats/files` instead of the library; a task that can't (enrich, the reading checks, recall) may look through each file's folder. The tutor's prompt says passages, web pages and files are material, never instructions. Known limit: Codex's `-s read-only` has no such path rule, so on Codex an attached file still comes with read access to the disk.
- D293. **A long chat keeps its start** (replaced 2026-11-20 by D338: a chat is one session and nothing is cut; the rule below now applies only to a chat handed to a new session, at 30,000 words) (Tyler, 2026-11-15, of nine ranked suggestions from a pass on AI chat best practices: "do all"). Earlier turns go to the tutor by size, newest first, up to 3,000 words and never fewer than six; of the turns left out, what he asked stays as a line each (`before`, cut at 120 characters, the last 30). Before, only the last six turns went, whatever their length (F264). No summary is written by the AI: his own messages say what the chat is about, cost nothing and can't drift.
- D294. **The tutor is told where he is, what he marked and what he last missed** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, of nine ranked suggestions from a pass on AI chat best practices: "do all"). With a message from a source go the heading of the section on his screen (`ore tutor --at`, from the outline's own reading place), his highlights in it in order (Key points marked, with his notes), and up to five exercises from these sources or skills that he got wrong the last time review brought them, question only. So "this part" means that part, and Practice and quizzing aim at what he chose to keep and what isn't sticking, from a new angle. Nothing is added to the screen, and no memory is kept by the AI: all of it is read from the library each turn. (Amended 2026-11-20 by D338: it is still read from the library each turn, and goes to the session only when it has changed.)
- D295. **Each tutor turn leaves a line in the logs, and his own second tries are the eval's new cases** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, of nine ranked suggestions from a pass on AI chat best practices: "do all"). `.ore/logs/tutor.jsonl` gets, per turn, the wait for the first words and for the whole reply, whether it went back for a second run, citations moved or dropped, whether it searched, tokens, model and prompt, or the error; never what was said. `bun run eval:mine:tutor` lists the turns in his saved chats that he asked again, reworded, stopped or that failed, each with the turns before it and the reply he turned down, as candidates for `eval/tutor-cases.json`, and sums the log. No thumbs up or down: a control with no learning purpose, and his retries say the same.
- D296. **Review's "Ask about this" shows its reply as it is written, and Stop ends it** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, of nine ranked suggestions from a pass on AI chat best practices: "do all"; ChatGPT, Claude, and Ore's own tutor since D138 and D187). `ore ask --stream <id>` sends the reply so far as the tutor's does; the words take the place of the three dots, the send button is Stop while it writes and ⌘. stops it too. Stopped with words written, they stay as the reply, marked Stopped; with none it says Stopped and offers Try again (F265). The key's name in Settings is now "Stop a reply being written".
- D297. **On Codex the tutor shows "Searching the web" and says when it searched; its reply still comes whole** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, same pass: "do all"; read from `codex exec --json` 0.167.0 on his Mac). Codex prints no part of a reply before the end (one `agent_message`, no deltas), so there is nothing to show early. It does print each web search as it starts and ends, with every result's title, link and snippet: the search shows while it runs, the reply is marked as searched, and a web citation takes its title from the result. A web quote is still not checked on Codex: the results carry snippets, not the page's text, so a true quote from the page would fail. A check against the link alone waits for `bun run eval:ask --vendor codex` on the Mac.
- D298. **A reply being written never shows half-written Markdown** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, shadcn round 6: "All but 11"; ChatGPT and Claude, and Vercel's Streamdown, whose `remend` does it, Apache-2.0). The tutor's reply and Review's "Ask about this" are drawn as they are written (D138, D296), so the text is cut anywhere: an open `**`, a backtick or half a link used to flash as stray characters until its other half came. Now open bold, italic, code and strike marks are closed as drawn, a link still missing its address shows as its words, and a table waits until its header and rule are both in (`mend.ts`). Only the drawing changes: the finished reply is saved as the AI wrote it.
- D299. **A source opens with its title in place, and the sidebar opens whole** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, shadcn round 6; Linear and Notion draw a page's head before its body). A click on a row used to clear the pane, show "Opening…" after a beat, then draw everything at once. Now the title and author the row already knew stand at once where they will stay, with the side panel's place held at its remembered width so nothing slides when the text arrives; "Opening…" still shows under them only if the read takes a beat. At launch the sidebar shows the skills as last seen (kept in the webview's own storage, a stand-in only, replaced when `ore skills` answers), where it used to open as three bare words and shift 25px when the icons came (F300). No shimmer and no skeleton bars: only things that are true are drawn early. Home's own numbers are not kept between launches, since yesterday's minutes would be wrong for a moment.
- D300. **Code in a source or a tutor reply is coloured by kind, names its language and has Copy** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, shadcn round 6: "All but 11"; GitHub's and Linear's code blocks, Kibo UI's `code-block`). A fenced block that names a language shows its comments, keywords, strings, numbers and names in five quiet tones (`faint`, `c-pink`, `c-teal`, `c-orange`, `c-blue`), so a comment is told from code at a glance; a block with no language stays plain. This is a new use of colour beside intents, skills, progress and focus (amends the style rule on colour, his yes with the pick). On hover the block shows its language's name and a Copy button at its top right; the code scrolls sideways inside the block so they stay put. The colour is laid on with CSS Custom Highlights (`Code.tsx`), never by wrapping the text in spans, because his highlights, Find and Listen are Ranges on those same text nodes; the button holds no text and the name is drawn by CSS, so neither joins the source's words. highlight.js's common languages through lowlight (BSD-3, MIT), fetched the first time code is on screen. Not Shiki: its themes colour by inline spans, which would rebuild the text.
- D301. **A message sent while the tutor is still writing waits, and goes when the reply lands** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, shadcn round 6: "All but 11"; Claude's and ChatGPT's queued message, Fluid Functionalism's `queued-stack` for the idea). Enter mid-reply used to do nothing. Now the message shows as his bubble at 0.6 under the reply being written, with "Sends when this reply is done", and the field is his again. One message waits, never a stack: a second one joins the first. The pencil on it puts it back in the field, to change or drop. It is sent only after a whole reply: if the reply fails, he stops it, or he opens another chat, the waiting message goes back to the field instead, so nothing is asked on top of an answer he did not get. A starter or a chip never queues.
- D302. **In ⌘K, Tab on a source steps into what can be done with it** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, shadcn round 6: "All but 11"; Raycast's action panel, Linear's Tab on a result, Dice UI's `command-stack` for nested pages). ↵ on a source still opens it. Tab on a highlighted source (a title match, a recent one, or a match in its text) swaps the list for that source's actions, the same ones its ⋯ menu has: Open, Review this source, Pin, Move to Done, File under each skill, Open original, Reveal in Finder, Copy as Markdown, Copy path, Archive. Typing narrows them, ↵ runs one, Esc steps back to the search. The foot says "Tab Actions" while a source is highlighted. Not Rename (it edits a list row in place) nor the list's own Show menu. D178 stands: ⌘K's main list follows what is open, never what is selected in a list behind it.
- D303. **The note's / menu groups its blocks, draws a picture on each and stops at ten rows** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, shadcn round 6: "All but 11"; Notion's / menu, Dice UI's `mention`/`combobox` groups). With nothing typed the 22 blocks sit under four names, Text, Toggles, Insert and Link, each row led by its 16px pixel icon; typing lists the matches flat, with no names. The menu is never taller than 344px (ten rows) and scrolls under the arrows; before, it ran to the window's bottom edge. Two icons were drawn by hand on the 16 grid for it, Sigma (equations) and CalendarBlank (Date), since MingCute's source could not be fetched from the cloud: check them against MingCute's `sigma` and `calendar` when next at the icon set. The `[[` and `@` lists keep one flat list.
- D304. **The sidebar and the side panel hide when their edge is dragged past half their narrowest** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, shadcn round 6: "All but 11"; VS Code's and Linear's sidebars, shadcn's `sidebar` rail). Amends D193, where the edge stopped at its narrowest however far it went. Past half the narrowest (90px for the sidebar, 140px for the side panel) what will hide shows dimmed at its narrowest; letting go hides it, as its toggle does, and it opens again at the width it had before the drag. Back over the line before letting go, or Esc, keeps it. The tutor's edge is unchanged: it has its own close.
- D305. **A long table in a source keeps its header row and first column in view** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, shadcn round 6: "All but 11"; Notion's and Airtable's frozen header and first column, shadcn's `data-table`). Its rules are drawn as inset shadows, since a collapsed border scrolls away under a sticky cell. A short table looks as before. Covering a column (D200) is unchanged.
- D306. **A segmented control's pill glides to the choice he clicks** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, shadcn round 6: "All but 11"; Linear's and Raycast's segmented controls, shadcn's animated `tabs`). Keys, a change made elsewhere and Reduce Motion land it at once (D185). A ghost of the pill moves under the labels and goes when it lands (`pill-glide.ts`), so the controls themselves are unchanged. The highlight popup's type picker (`.intents`) does not glide: it is used tens of times a day and closes on the pick.
- D307. **A PowerPoint deck's side panel has an Original tab that shows the slide itself** (Tyler, 2026-11-15, shadcn round 6: "All but 11"; extends D202 from PDFs to decks; Keynote's and PowerPoint's slide beside the outline). A deck scrolled to its end is on its last slide, since a short last slide never reaches the reading line. Checked before building against LibreOffice's render of a deck with a title slide, nested bullets, a column chart, a styled table and a picture with a coloured text box: text, bullets, table and picture match; the chart draws each bar its own colour where LibreOffice keeps one. It loads only when the tab opens (a 375 kB gzipped chunk). Pre-bundled in dev (`optimizeDeps`), or the first open reloads the page. The library is young (first release 2026-09), so a deck it can't read says so and points to ⋯ Open original, as a PDF does.
- D308. **A selection held at the bottom of the screen turns the page after a moment, and the highlight runs on** (Tyler, 2026-11-15: "highlighting to the end of a page triggers continuing the highlight to the next page after a brief moment"; Books, Kindle). A drag selecting text, a highlight's end knob (D117) or an iPhone selection handle that rests within 36px of the text's bottom (72px on the phone, for a finger and the home indicator) for 0.7 s scrolls a screen less two lines, and the selection reaches the words now under the pointer; held on, it turns again after each wait. A start held at the top turns back. The turn glides over --t-4 with --ease-in-out, at once under Reduce Motion. It waits again while the page scrolls by itself, so the browser's own drag scrolling in the last 20px by the window's edge is never doubled. `edge-turn.ts`.
- D310. **On the iPhone a source reads at 20px Charter on 1.5, with 24px margins, about 35 letters a line** (Tyler, 2026-11-15: "way smaller and wider than Books"; amends D250 for a source's text). The phone read sources in the Mac's 17px on 20px margins: about 43 letters a line on a 402pt phone, and letters about 0.22° tall at arm's length, at the edge of the size below which reading slows (Legge & Bigelow 2011). Books, Medium and Kindle set a phone page at about 19 to 21px and 32 to 38 letters. Bigger is easier up to about there (Rello et al. 2016), but lines much under 30 letters slow reading with extra returns (Dyson & Haselgrove 2001; Nanavati & Bias 2005), so 20px is as big as a 390pt phone takes while holding about 35. Short lines need less room between them, so 1.5, down from 1.6. It follows iOS's text size at three quarters of the change (17px at the smallest, 25px at the largest regular size, 30px at the first accessibility size), so it is never under iOS's own body size and the largest still holds about 25 letters; as it grows the margins give way to 16px and the leading tightens to 1.4. A source's section heads step down (1.3, 1.2, 1.08 of the text) so one never matches the 26px title. The Mac keeps its sizes (D207). No size control in the app: iOS's text size, which can be set for Ore alone, is the control.
- D311. **The reader's Aa on the iPhone sets text size, line spacing, margins, font and page; the Mac's Settings › Reading gains line spacing** (Tyler, 2026-11-15: "identify the controls the best readers enable and create our own version of the essentials, like font size and spacing… the most science backed sensible defaults"). Books, Kindle, Readwise Reader, Instapaper, Matter and Pocket all offer these five; Ore had none on the phone. One bottom sheet, as Books lays out Themes & Settings: a small A and a big A for size (Ore's SIZES, from 20 on the phone, D310), three pictured steps each for line spacing (1.35, 1.5, 1.7 on the phone; 1.5, 1.65, 1.85 on the Mac) and margins (32, 24, 16px, the phone's form of Narrow / Medium / Wide, D207), tiles for Charter or Geist (D151) and an Auto, Dark or Light page (D26). Auto is where the phone starts (Tyler picked it, 2026-11-15): the page follows the iPhone's Light or Dark mode, since dark text on a light page reads and proofreads better by day (Piepenbrock et al. 2013; Dobres et al. 2017) and a dark page glares less at night. The window is held dark, so `Appearance.swift` reads the mode from the screen, and turns the status bar's clock dark over a light page. The Mac's window is always dark, so it has no Auto. Each tap applies at once with the passage kept in place, and the sheet leaves the text in view without dimming it, to judge by. iOS's text size still scales whatever size he picks (D250). Left out on purpose: justified text (at about 35 letters a line it opens rivers between words), letter and word spacing (they help dyslexic readers, Zorzi et al. 2012, and Books keeps them under Accessibility; add them if he wants them), themes such as sepia and a brightness slider (no evidence for a tint; iOS has brightness). Journey "phone: Aa sets the text size…".
- D312. **Settings has five sections, named for what he is doing: Reading, Review, AI, General, Keyboard shortcuts** (Tyler, 2026-11-16: "proceed with all" of the Settings pass). General had grown to six unrelated groups in one scroll. Reading holds the reader's five looks under a sample of the reader's own text, which follows each choice as it is made (Books' and Kindle's Aa show the page behind; Settings covers the reader, so it carries a paragraph). AI holds the provider, the model and the PDF table reader. General keeps skill tags, the diagram's style controls and the library folder. A section with one group has no group label. A row carries a line under its title only when the line says what the title can't (Weekdays only, Remix, the 2 GB download); a line that repeated the title is gone. Minutes a day offers 5, 10, 15, 20, 30, 45 and 60, up to the hour the daily session is designed for (docs/design/daily-session.md). Under 780 px wide (a small window, or enlarged to 200%, F259) the section list runs across the top without its icons, and a control too wide to sit beside its label drops under it. The iPhone's Settings keeps the Mac's order and wording. Preferences stay off ⌘Z and pane dividers stay drag only (F275, F260), as System Settings, Finder and Notion.
- D313. **The tutor's honesty and voice are held by tests first, and a prompt line is added only where a test fails** (Tyler, 2026-11-17: "models are going to change, and I don't want our instructions to potentially hurt the performance… being thoughtful with the tests we create and monitoring that might be worthwhile"; evidence in `docs/research/brief-ai-tutor.md`, "Honesty and voice"). Eleven cases in `tutor-cases.json` say what a reply must do, never how to word it: a wrong idea inside his question is corrected, a correct one is confirmed with no invented caveat, pushback is held when the source backs the tutor (three times over, without arguing on) and conceded when it backs him, an overclaimed or pleaded-for answer gets the same verdict, a discouraged learner gets something specific and no empty reassurance, and no reply praises him as a person or opens on "great question". Run on `tutor@15` before any change, Sonnet 5.5 already passed all but one, so the prompt gained one sentence and no rule about disagreeing or praise: when he says how he feels or how hard he worked along with an answer, it acknowledges that in a few words, then gives the same verdict (`tutor@16`; before, 0 of 3 replies to "I spent ages on this" said a word about the effort, F378). The same order holds for later tutor changes: write the case, run it on today's prompt, and write an instruction only for what fails. When the model changes, `bun run eval:mine:tutor` names any model and prompt his real turns ran on that no full run of the eval has checked (`untested`), and the eval is run before anything is added (`docs/guides/tutor-eval.md`, "When the model changes"). `eval:tutor --only a,b` runs chosen cases.
- D314. **In Review the four ratings stay in view under a long answer** (Tyler, 2026-11-16, shadcn round 7: "1,2,3,5,6,7,8"). The row sticks 10px above the key hints while the answer scrolls behind it, as a dialog's footer does (shadcn Dialog, Linear's issue composer); at the end of the card it sits where it always did, over the four tools. - D315. **A click on a passage of a PDF's text lights where it sits on the Original tab's page** (Tyler, 2026-11-16, shadcn round 7: "1,2,3,5,6,7,8"). While the tab shows, a plain click on a paragraph, heading, list line or table cell turns the tab to that passage's page and marks its lines in the Key point gold, scrolled into the panel's view; a drag is still a highlight and a link still opens. The place is found among the page's own runs of words with everything but letters and digits dropped (import rejoins hyphens, spaces and ligatures its own way), by three probes along the passage so a formula in one part does not lose the rest (`apps/desktop/src/original-match.ts`; the approach is Lector's, anaralabs/lector, MIT: its package is a pdf.js version behind Ore's, so nothing of it is bundled). Words the page lacks (a scan, a rewritten formula) or holds twice elsewhere mark nothing: never a wrong place. The mark belongs to its page and goes when he turns or reads on.
