Guards: `apps/desktop/test/original-match.test.ts`, journey "original: a click on a passage of the text lights where it sits on the PDF's page".
- D316. **On the iPhone, Sections opens low and pulls up to the top of the screen** (Tyler, 2026-11-16, shadcn round 7: "1,2,3,5,6,7,8"). The sheet had one height, about 60% of the screen, over the text he was placing himself in, and a 30-section outline scrolled inside it. It opens with its list at 38% of the screen, so the text above still shows; a pull up on a list that holds more than it shows grows it under the finger to the status bar (an iOS sheet's medium and large detents, as Maps and Books do), and scrolls from there. From full height a short pull down brings it back, a long one or a flick closes it; a list scrolled down scrolls back to its top first, as before. A short outline has nothing to pull up to and keeps its height. Built on the phone's own sheet (`apps/desktop/src/phone/sheet.ts`, `pull`) instead of bringing in Base UI's Drawer: the five sheets already share that one hook, and the change is two heights on it. The other sheets (⋯ actions, What to review, Aa, Add a link) keep one height: their lists are short. Guard: journey "phone: Sections opens low with the text above it".
- D317. **On the iPhone a toast is swiped away** (Tyler, 2026-11-16, shadcn round 7). A toast over the row he wanted could only be waited out (up to 8 s) or closed by a small × that only errors carry. It follows the finger down or to a side, as an iOS banner does; let go past 48pt or with a flick it leaves, otherwise it comes back. Undo goes with it, as it does when the toast times out (⌘Z has no place on the phone; shake to undo still reaches it, D275). A wait's toast (an add in progress) stays, since swiping it away would hide work still going. Guard: journey "phone: a toast is swiped away".
- D318. **A term he looked up counts: it stays a plain highlight, and the plan weighs it as a term he didn't know** (Tyler, 2026-11-17, thread "Decide how Ore handles vocabulary and concepts": "Both" of the options, this one first). Explain (D97) wrote nothing, so the clearest sign of a gap, a term he had to look up, was lost unless he also pressed Key point. Now Explain from the highlight popup keeps the highlight it was opened on (a fresh one is no longer dropped as only a way to point), and ⌃⌘D on a word with no highlight makes one; either way the highlight is marked `looked_up` (`ore highlight --looked-up`, `ore annotate --looked-up`). Inside a passage he highlighted, no second highlight is stacked. It is a plain highlight like any other, so it waits for the finish and counts against `plain_cap`; enrich (`enrich@17`) never skips it as a stray word, and drafts its definition (a Fact, grouped with related terms; an Explain only when central) when the document leans on it or his skill's goal needs it, else skips it. - D319. **A textbook's margin glossary keeps its definitions** (Tyler, 2026-11-17, thread "Decide how Ore handles vocabulary and concepts": "Both", this one second; amends D119). D119 dropped all text set smaller than the body beside its column, which is right for a margin note that only repeats a word of the line beside it (AIMA's terms: the line already has the word, in bold) but also dropped a margin glossary, the term again with its definition, as psychology and biology textbooks set one: the author's own definition of a key term, the passage an expert picked (g 0.44, `research/brief-learning-science.md`), gone before he could read, highlight or be asked about it. Now a margin note of four words or more that the page's text doesn't already say is kept, its bold term too, as its own paragraph after the page's text and above its footnotes (F389), so the line it sat beside still reads on unbroken and its highlights never move. The mouse landing on the tutor's mole plays one friendly move at random, never the one before: six new (`hello`, `tip`, `flash`, `peekaboo`, `chuckle`, `dance`) and six he already had (`wink`, `happy`, `proud`, `shy`, `curious`, `nod`). Purpose: delight, the third priority, on the one character in the app; it tells him nothing. None starts while one plays, while he thinks or speaks, on a touch (a tap opens the tutor), on keyboard focus (he faces him and blinks, as before) or under reduced motion; no sound. - D321. **The tutor's reply is typed out at a steady pace, letter by letter behind its block** (Tyler, 2026-11-17: "make the chat streaming and waiting for reply more like ChatGPT and Claude"; of four mixes played side by side on a demo page with worst cases, his pick "Steady + typed"; amends D138 and D296). The model sends its words in clumps of one to eight with stops between, and a Codex reply comes whole (D297), so the words jumped onto the screen. ChatGPT and Claude even this out: Ore now types what has come at 70 letters a second, and each time more arrives sets a pace that puts it all on screen within half a second (`steady.ts`), so the words never fall further behind than that, a stall shows the block blinking where the next word goes, and a whole reply sweeps in within half a second. The block holds still while letters are typed, as a terminal's does, and blinks when they wait (D186). The landed reply keeps typing the last letters before its citations, copy and actions show; Stop shows all that was sent. When the model starts over (D138's retry) the letters that still match stay. Review's "Ask about this" types its reply the same way while it is written; there the last half second shows at once when it lands. A saved chat, a reply that landed with the tutor closed, and Reduce Motion show the words at once. Nothing is added to the screen: the wait, the scroll that follows the end (his question is not moved to the top: he passed on that in round 7, pick 4) and the words are as before. - D322. **The apply half gets its own pages: one try at a time on the skill page, a Forge of every try, and a page per try** (Tyler, 2026-11-17, thread "Define Ore's minimum set of exercises": shown the try as one fold on the skill page he said "too much text", asked for ADHD-friendly and the mining analogy, then "think a bit bigger instead of cramping it into this one UX", new pages welcome if the science backs them, and "Implement" on the three-screen sketch; amends D127 and D148, and he lifted the no-new-rewards rule of 2026-11-15 for this). **One at a time:** per skill one open try is the one he is trying now, the one he picked last, else the first he marked; plans work one at a time and several at once weaken each other (Dalton & Spiller 2012). The skill page's "Trying now" shows it in one line (a box to check, what he'll do, its cue) and the waiting ones as grey stones with their words on hover. **The Forge** (a page inside each skill, on Back and Forward like any view): Trying now, Waiting, Yours; "Try this now" swaps the slot; + adds a try with no passage behind it (its own snippet, kept out of the source lists as a typed question is). No dates, no counts, nothing owed (D23). **A try's page:** the plan as an implementation intention, "When… I'll…" (Gollwitzer & Sheeran 2006), with one line for what might get in the way (Oettingen's mental contrasting), the passage and section it came from, and every use with how it went (reflection after doing: Di Stefano 2015). **Uses:** "I did it" asks how it went; Again keeps it in the works (`ore tried --again`), and from the third use "It's mine" makes it his (status done). Three uses show he can do it, not that it is a habit. **The picture:** an idea is a stone of loose dots (grey waiting, green in use, its dots swelling with each use) and a pickaxe beside the skill's goal once it is his; a use that lands strikes the stone (sparks, the spring: a moment, D185), and nothing else animates. Left for later, each needing his yes: the mole's line on a reflection (D127.4 keeps the tutor out of tries), a check-in at the start of a session (D148.4), asking for a different situation on the next use, and the try's exercise shown on its page.
- D324. **The four pieces left out of the try pages: the mole says one line on a use, a session starts by asking if he did it, each use keeps its moment, and the try's exercise shows on its page** (Tyler, 2026-11-17, same thread: "Implement the rest", his yes for the bends named in D322; amends D127.4 and D148.4). **The mole's line:** once he writes how a use went, the tutor reads the plan, its cue, what might get in the way, every round and what he wrote, and says one or two sentences under that use: what worked or what to change, one concrete next thing, never a grade or a rating (D21). `ore tried <t> --line` (prompt `try-line`), stored with its vendor, model and prompt version on the round; it is dropped when he undoes the use or rewrites it, and thrown away if the try changed while the mole was thinking. A Mac job: the iPhone shows a line the Mac wrote and asks for none. A failure says nothing, since the line is extra. **The check-in:** a review session opens with "Did you try it?" for the one thing he is trying (in the skills he picked, the one touched longest ago), only when he last touched it before today, once a day, never for the quick quiz or a review of one source. Writing how it went is a use; "Not yet" (Esc) goes on, and nothing counts or scolds (D23). This is the reminder D148.4 held back: a plan is kept when its cue is met, and a look back at the start of the next sitting is the cheapest cue Ore owns. **A different situation:** no new element. Each use keeps the "When…" it was done under and shows it beside its day; after a use kept with Again the keys land in When with its words selected, so the same cue is one Tab away (a stable cue builds a habit) and a new one is one sentence (varied situations build transfer); the mole's line suggests a new situation when every round shared one. **The exercise:** the Explain exercise drafted from a use (D127) shows on the try's page under "Exercise", read only; it is edited where exercises are. `ore tries --now` lists what he is trying across skills.
- D323. **A reward glows the way the radio's lit pad does, in its own colour** (Tyler, 2026-11-17: "if you log a to-do or mouse over the goal or like achieve a goal ... we could use it for those moments or related to like reward or positive items rather than neutral ones", then "try all those areas, and I'll just tell you to remove where I feel like it's overdone or distracting"; amends D263 and D287's rule 7, which kept glow to the lamp). The day's goal met: Home's ring and the end screen's ring breathe a soft green glow in place (3.2 s, the halo only, so the glint and the dots' own states play as before). Hovering Home's ring lights its halo. A to-do ticked in a note: its box swells and glows once (900 ms) and settles; unticking, or a note opening on ticked boxes, stays still. Amber stays the lamp's alone (running right now); a reward glows green for the goal and the box's own colour for a to-do. Not on the 22 px ring above a review (it would glow through the whole session once met). Under Reduce Motion the met ring keeps a still glow and the tick does not move. Purpose: a positive moment reads as one at a glance, the way a lit pad reads as on. On trial: Tyler prunes any spot he finds overdone or distracting.
- D325. **The Dig: a skill explained whole from memory, checked against its key points, on a widening gap** (Tyler, 2026-11-17, thread "Define Ore's minimum set of exercises": "Implement the rest" of the set he was shown, Fact, Explain, Dig, Try; amends D102 and bends `research/brief-exercise-types.md` §9, which says recall-it-all is never scheduled). Nothing in Ore asked for the whole picture a second time. **What it is:** the skill goal's "Try it" becomes **Dig**. He does the goal from memory over the blurred page (typed or dictated), as before; his words still go in the skill note, now under `## Dig, <date>`. Then the mole reads them against the skill's key points (the newest 40, fixed before he writes, so the list is never fitted to his answer) and asks at most two questions, each a second chance at an idea he left out, never naming it (a cue before the reveal rescues what plain testing loses; explaining to a novice who asks why: Lachner 2020). Then the ideas show as stones in the key point's blue, a row per source: solid, it came on its own; loose, it came with the mole's question; grey, it didn't come, and a click opens its passage. Each stone says which on hover. **No grade:** no number, no rating, no exercise made, nothing in FSRS (D21, D23); `came` is feedback. **When:** a skill is ready to dig again 7, 14, 30, 60, then 90 days after its last dig, and only ever "ready": nothing is due, counted or shown as late. A finished session offers at most one, as one line on the end screen ("Dig · skill", a click opens it on the skill's page), for a skill with a goal and at least two key points, the one dug longest ago, and none in the week after any dig, so about one a week at most. From the skill page he can dig at any time. A skill with fewer than two key points keeps the dig as written, with no mole. **Why per skill, not per source:** three digs for every finished source would be a second backlog that grows with reading (daily-session.md), and the goal is the performance he wants (transfer-appropriate processing). **Stored** in `skills/<id>/digs.json`; `ore dig`, prompt `dig`. A Mac job: the iPhone saves the words in the note and the mole reads none there. Left out, each needing his yes: flipping a stone the mole got wrong, the last dig's stones shown again on the skill page, speaking it through a mic button (dictation works in the field).
- D326. **How far he has read is a line on the picture's bottom edge, on the Mac and the iPhone** (Tyler, 2026-11-17: the dots under a list picture "wasting a row", then "how do we know what doesn't clash with whatever the background is of the thumbnail?", and of four mocks, the dots under, the iPhone's dots on a shade, a line on the edge and a column beside the picture: "B. for both mac and iphone"; amends D262's dots on the picture and D184's 12 dots under a list thumbnail, and is the one bar D183's round dots give way to). A 3px white line as wide as his share of the source, on a track of white at 30%, over a 7px shade fading up from dark: the shade is what keeps it readable on a white page, a gold cover, a photo with a pale foot and the blank tile, and it hides a few pixels of the picture where the dots on a shade hid a third of a 54px one. In the Mac's source lists (All sources, a skill's lists) it replaces the 12 dots that hung under the picture, shown from 2% as before; on the iPhone's Today it replaces the ten dots, and New keeps its tag. Home's Continue reading on the Mac keeps its ten dots at the row's right, where they take no row. Mocks: `/mnt/project-files/thumb-progress-mac/`.
- D327. **Home's Continue reading on the Mac shows the reading line too** (Tyler, 2026-11-17, asked "Continue reading on the Mac's Home get the progress line?": "sure"; amends D326, which left the ten dots at the row's right). The line sits on the foot of the row's 64px picture, as in the lists and on the iPhone, so how far he is reads one way everywhere a source has a picture; hovering the picture still says "62% read". The ten dots are gone from the row; a source not opened yet still says New at the right, and the row keeps the ⋯'s room there so skill pills never run under it.
- D328. **What the tutor is pointed at sits inside the field, a row of chips over his words** (Tyler, 2026-11-17, of the chip beside the mole: "I feel like this could look better"; of three mocks, a tab on the field, inside the field and a "Looking at" label by the mole, he leaned to inside, asked what it costs and how other apps do it, then of two ways inside: "b2 go"; amends D186's chips beside the mole). Notion, Fabric, Copilot, Langdock and Plane all put the open page or file as a chip on its own row inside the input, above the text; none of twelve on Mobbin put it outside the field or on the typing line. So the skill page's or source's chip, files and the @ skills and sources still in effect sit on one row inside the field (it wraps when they don't fit), and the mole peeks over the field alone. A chip is 24px, `bg-active`, 13px `text`; its × shows on hover or keyboard focus, so the name leads and the × is no longer as loud as it. The field's frame moved from the textarea to its wrapper, so chips and words are one field, every edge where it was. The cost: the field is two lines tall whenever something is pointed at (about 28px less chat), and a filled chip in a framed field is a box in a box, an exception Tyler allowed here because every reference does it. Mocks: `/mnt/project-files/tutor-chip/`.
- D329. **Space separates, not lines** (Tyler, 2026-11-18, of the rules under every row and label on the iPhone's Today: "these lines are lazy design work. how can we better create natural separation without adding noise"; of three mocks, space only, short lines under the words and raised panels, he picked space only, then: "apply principle everywhere it would improve the ui, ios and macos"; amends the style rule of 2026-10-30, a section is a label over a hairline). A row's picture, icon or checkbox already marks where it starts, so a line under it says the same thing twice, and a line under a label a third time. Rows now sit close and sections far (36px above a label), as Spotify and Books lay out picture lists. Gone: on the iPhone, the lines on Today, Sources, the waiting links and the skill page's note row; on the Mac, the rule under Home's, Settings' and the shortcut list's labels and the Forge's band labels, the lines between a skill's questions, around the reader's prequestions and over a note's Linked from. Kept, because each does a job nothing else does: pane and floating-layer edges and the fields and footers inside them, the shelf under a folder tab, Settings' row lines (they carry the eye from label to control), and a source's own tables and rules. Not looked at, so left as they were: the lines in a chapter recall's feedback, Then and now, Ask about this, a tell-apart's cases and the Collected tabs. Mocks: `/mnt/project-files/today-dividers/`.
- D330. **The mole speaks aloud, on the few session ends that earn a word, on the Mac and the iPhone; his sounds and the sound effects each have a switch** (Tyler, 2026-11-18: "add appropriate mole sounds… in both the iOS and macOS versions… in tasteful places with tasteful dialect or dialogue, and don't overdo it, but we need a few hero moments… Options to turn off both gem sound effects and mascot sound effects separately in settings"; amends D277's one switch and its "Mac only for now"). He has one place to speak: Review's end screen, as he comes up and takes his pose (D258), a short exclamation in the old Scottish miner's voice Tyler picked (ElevenLabs "Dwarf Mole"), from the takes he already trusts. Five lines, one per pose: a skill's new level, "Hey-hey! Aye, aye! Wahoo!" (`cheer`); memories pulled back in time, "Hah! That's ma lad." (`proud`); a long gap recalled across, "Oh! Well, I never!" (`surprised`); then and now, "Hm? What's this, then?" (`curious`); and the session that met the day's goal, "Hah! Braw!" (`happy`), so once a day at most for the goal. Every other session ends without a word: an ordinary one, one that only cut gems, and a session of misses (D62, D278). Why so few: a voice heard every session is furniture by the third day, and one heard on a rare day marks it. He says nothing during a review (speech over a recall attempt competes with it), on the goal's mid-session note (its chime is the moment, and the end screen has the line), on the cart (two sessions in three would hear it), in the tutor, the Forge or the Dig, and never in the quick quiz, which has no mole. His written line in the speech box stays what it was; the voice is the reaction, not a reading of it. Settings › Review › Sound has two switches, both on by default and kept per device: Sound effects (the tick, the shine, the chip, the chime, the cart, the jingle) and Mole's voice (his five lines and the pop as he comes up). The iPhone build now carries Review's sounds and his voice (not the radio's loops) and its Settings has the same two switches; there the ringer switch silences them, as it does any app's effects. Files are `vo-<pose>.mp3` beside the `fx-` ones, out of git as D277 has it (trimmed and levelled copies: the project's `house-style/app-sounds/`); a missing one is silent. `endVoice` in `end-says.ts`, `sfx.ts`, test `sfx.test.ts`.
- D331. **The iPhone app moves the way iOS does: pages give at their ends, screens slide in, sheets rise, and the long-press menu grows from its row** (Tyler, 2026-11-19: "the ios app does not feel very native. like scrolling past bottom/tops, no menu pop up animations"; asked whether the web view was the cause, shown the five causes in our own code, he picked all five fixes). None of it was the stack: Obsidian's phone app runs in the same kind of web view. 1. **Bounce.** Every scrolling page on the phone gives at its top and bottom with iOS's own rubber band: the Mac's `overscroll-behavior: none` is lifted under `.phone` (the reader, Review, a note, lists), and a page shorter than the screen has one point to scroll (a zero-width float one point taller than `.phone-page` and Review's body), since iOS only bounces a scroller that has somewhere to go. The bars sit outside the scrollers and never move; a pull down on Today still opens Search. 2. **Push.** A screen he opens (a source, a skill, a course, Settings, Review) slides in from the right over `--t-4` on `--ease-out` while the one under it moves 30% left, as Books and Settings do; Back slides it out the same way, and the left-edge swipe carries the screen under with the finger. The screen a relaunch puts back is just there. A screen that replaces another in place (Review this source) and Review's own close still change at once. 3. **Sheets.** A bottom sheet (⋯, Aa, Sections, What to review, Add a link, a submenu) rises from the foot over `--t-4` as the page behind dims, and a tap outside lets it down as the swipe does; a pick still closes it at once, so the action is never held behind an animation (F200's reason). 4. **Menu.** The long-press menu grows from 0.8 at the row he pressed over `--t-3`, and that row stays lit while it is open; D280's 4% is a pointer's distance and could not be seen under a thumb. No grey flash on a tap. All on the motion tokens with no spring (D185), none under Reduce Motion. This sets aside "nothing moves for an action done a hundred times a day" for the phone's pushes and sheets: on iOS the slide is how a screen says where it came from and which edge takes it back, and its absence was what he noticed. 5. Haptics follow in their own change (a web view cannot tick the phone; a Tauri plugin does). Left as it is, since it would mean native views drawn over the page: Apple's blurred menu, native sheet stops, iOS 26's glass bars.
- D332. **The iPhone ticks under his finger on four moments for now** (amended 2026-11-20: the four are where it ticks today, not a limit; a tick is added wherever Apple's own apps give one) (Tyler, 2026-11-19, the fifth of D331's fixes: "All five"). A web view cannot make a haptic, so the phone's shell carries Tauri's haptics plugin (`tauri-plugin-haptics`, Apache-2.0 OR MIT, iOS's own feedback generators, which follow the phone's System Haptics switch, so Ore has no switch of its own) and the page asks it through `phone/haptics.ts`. The four, each where Apple's apps tick: a long press opening its menu (a medium impact, iOS's context menu); a swipe passing the point where letting go acts, on a source's row and on the pull down to Search (a light impact, Mail's rows and a pull to refresh; once per crossing, none for a swipe that springs back); a rating recorded in Review (a selection tick: the second press of D237's guard, never the first); and a highlight made (a light impact, Books). Nothing else ticks: not a tap, a tab, a screen opening, a sheet or a toggle, since a tick on everything means nothing, and none of it is a reward (D23, D62): each confirms that the phone took what his finger did. The Mac and a browser do nothing.
- D333. **The end screen says less, and on the iPhone it is laid out for a phone** (Tyler, 2026-11-19: "the wording in the circle feels cramped", "as a user idk what 'day gap recalled' means", of the try line "i cant tell if its telling me what to do or something i already did", then "actually fix it the design, text amount, spacing, and information"; shown the result he asked whether the next step belonged on a following page or nowhere and whether the skill reel could come back "below the green circle instead of inside it", rotating "in a loop" with several skills, and said "run it" to the answer; amends D252, D259, D267, D274, D148). Each thing on the screen was asked what he needs it for at a session's end: that the day is done, what it was worth, and what to do next. **The ring** holds the day's minutes over "min" and nothing else: the "+N" chip repeated the ring's own white-centred dots, and the skill line had no room there. **Under the ring** the skills the session kept memories in stand one line at a time: the skill's icon at 24px (D170) and name, "3 kept" (the old "+3" said neither what nor of what), and "level 2" when it went up one. A skill that went up leads, then the ones fed most, three at most; one skill stands still, several take turns in a loop, and Reduce Motion holds the first. **The row** is the days of memory the session added and the gems it cut: the memories kept are the lines above it, so no number is said twice, and a new level is on its skill's line. A long gap and memories pulled back in time needed a sentence to mean anything, so they are no longer numbers; the mole says them ("18 days gone and you still had it."), and the comet is gone with its number. **The mole** says "Goal met." in the session that met the day's goal (ten of ten session-end screens on Mobbin say what happened in plain words). Only two things lead it: a skill's new level, the rarest thing a session has and small on its line ("go" to his saying it again), and then and now, which points at what stands under the ring. **The try** is gone from this screen (D148's "one try at the end of a session"): the screen closes the session, a page after it would be one more tap before leaving every day, and a try has its place on the skill page ("Trying now") and in the Forge (D322). Where a try comes back to him unasked is open until he has used one. **On the iPhone** the mole's line stands over him, centred, whole and 15px, where the box beside his head had 118px to wrap in; a result is as wide as its words, so no label breaks beside one that doesn't; what the session won stands in the middle of the space over the buttons; and Done is as wide as the screen at the foot, under his thumb, with Keep going under it (Apple's own sheets).
- D334. **Each skill the session fed gets a row on the end screen, with its level and the way to the next** (Tyler, 2026-11-20, of the one line that took turns under the ring: "The 9 kept and the level just are very underwhelming. It almost looks like a subtitle rather than the reward. Think about this from first principles"; and of the level that only one skill showed: "It doesn't make sense intuitively"; shown three placements with a long skill name and a one-skill session, rows, tiles and one hero skill, he picked rows; amends D333). The reward of a session is what each skill gained, so it has the room of a result, not of a caption: under the ring a row per skill, its icon and name, its level as a figure of dots at the right, and under the name ten dots to the next level beside "9 kept". The dots this session added are in the skill's colour, so progress short of a level is seen too. Every row says its level, so a level is never something only one skill seems to have; a skill that went up has its new level and the whole bar it filled in its colour, and leads. (The first build put "+9" at the right and the level in small text; Tyler sent it to an independent critic, who saw only the recording: the level-up was "the quietest thing in its own row", "+9" had no unit, and the rows landed after the results under them. So the level took the figure's place, the count got its word, and the rows land first, one at a time, the results after.) Three rows at most, then "and 2 more skills"; nothing takes turns, so nothing is waited for. The bar counts solid memories (gems), as the skill tile on Home does (D63), while the figure counts memories kept; the mole still says a new level (D333). No streak, XP or badge is added (D23): the level and its bar are the ones the skill page already has. References: Life Reset, Uxcel and Hevy (a full-width row per category with its gain at the right), WHOOP and Google Maps (a bar to the next level).
- D335. **The mole's box on the end screen comes up as he speaks, and has a tail pointing at him** (Tyler, 2026-11-20: "the text box pops up for a long time before he speaks. That needs to be smoother", and "a little arrow are the bottom of the box like an actual dialogue box rn its not clear its him talking"; amends D266, which took the tail off, and D243). The box used to come up empty when he settled and wait for the results to land before a letter was typed; with the skills' rows landing first (D334) that wait grew. Now the box and his voice (D330) come together once the results are in, and the first letter is typed as it opens. The tail is a corner of the box's own hairline, 8px, from its left edge on the Mac and the middle of its foot on the iPhone, where he is under it.
- D336. **Sound is as satisfying as it can be made, and layered wherever that is better** (Tyler, 2026-11-20, of D277's "one short sound on each key moment, and nowhere else", after the end screen's video had three sounds over fourteen seconds of things landing: "Remove that rule. The new rule for sound is to make it as satisfying and as like Duolingo and other apps with rewarding sounds as possible. Not like Duolingo in the actual style of the sound, necessarily, but I think objectively, layered sound effects can be much better, so definitely should not limit that"). This replaces D277's rule for the whole app: the bar is the apps whose sounds reward (Duolingo's lesson end and the like) in how good it feels, in Ore's own materials, and several sounds may ring at once. What D277 says of the files, the switches and the 60% level stands, and an action that is not a moment (undo, Later, an edit) still has no sound of its own, because a sound there rewards nothing. First use, the end screen (`layer` in `src/sfx.ts`, which plays over what is sounding and is never cut, where a review's own sounds take turns): each minute the count takes is a tick, a semitone higher each up to an octave; each skill's row lands on the recall's shine, a step up a major scale each, and a skill that went up a level rings the goal's chime under its own; each result lands on a tick and its count ends on a shine, higher again, so the screen ends higher than it began; the mole's letters tick softly, one in three, when he says nothing aloud. Under Reduce Motion nothing lands, so only the jingle plays. All of it is the files D277 has, pitched: new sounds made for these moments are the next step, by his ear.
- D338. **A tutor chat is one session of Claude or Codex, so it remembers all of itself** (Tyler, 2026-11-20: "why?" of the 3,000-word limit, then, of T3 Code and HyperFrames Studio, which run a chat on his Claude plan by resuming a session: "continue and remove any dumb rules that stopped us from doing this in the first place"; replaces D293, amends D290 and D294). The tutor started a new run of the vendor's program for every message and sent it the open source and the last 3,000 words of the chat again. So in a long chat it could not see its own early replies, and it read the source from the start each time. Review's "Ask about this" never worked that way: it has kept one session per chat since D61. Now the tutor does the same.
  1. **Resume.** A chat's first message starts a session (`claude -p --session-id`, `codex exec`), and each later one picks it up (`--resume`, `exec resume`) and sends only what is new: his message, passages the session has not been given, where he is when he has moved, his highlights and missed exercises when they have changed, a source he has since opened or closed. A passage keeps the number it was first sent under for the whole chat, so a later reply can cite it, and a web page an earlier search returned can be cited again.
  2. **Seed.** With no session to pick up, one is started from the saved chat (`chats/<id>.json`, D172, still the record): its turns whole up to 30,000 words, then the oldest as what he asked, a line each. That one move covers a chat opened on another Mac, a session the program has cleared, a switch between Claude and Codex, an edited message, Try again or a flip back to another version of a reply (D201), a worked answer of which he has seen only some steps (D142: the session wrote them all, so the next message starts one that knows only the steps shown), a turn that failed or that he stopped (which leaves half of itself in the session), and a session grown past 150,000 tokens, where the vendor's program would otherwise sum up the oldest part, passages included. The session is matched on every turn as the chat shows it, his words and the replies; its size is what Claude says the model last had in front of it, and Codex, which reports only sums, is left to its own program.
  3. **Measured** (cloud, Claude Sonnet, the same 43-turn chat on a 6,900-word source both ways): the wait for the first words is the same, 2.0 s; the session quoted its own first and second replies where the old way answered "my earlier answers are gone"; and the usage the program reports for the whole chat was about a tenth ($0.47 against $4.62), since the source is read once.
  4. **Where it lives.** What this Mac's session has been sent is in `.ore/tutor/<chat>.json`, beside the library's other disposable state; the session itself is in the vendor program's own folder, as Review's is, and holds the source's text. Deleting a chat removes the first; the program clears its own old sessions.
  Nothing on screen changes. `ore tutor --chat <id>` names the chat; with none (an agent's one-off, the evals) nothing is kept and everything is sent, as before. Not built: rewinding a session in place for an edit (the program has an unlisted flag for it; a seed needs no flag and costs one resend). Code: `tutor` in `packages/core/src/ai/tutor.ts`, `session` in `ai/adapter.ts`; prompt tutor@17. Checked with the real program on Claude; Codex's thread resume is built as Review's is and checked only against a stand-in.
- D337. **Sources in one skill that disagree: nothing is built until two of his real sources do, and then it is a remix, not a new exercise** (Tyler, 2026-11-17: one Health source says seed oils are bad and another that they aren't, "that'd be something that I think we want to bring up to the user in a way that allows them to understand that confliction"; on 2026-11-20 he asked whether the idea was still the best one for the app and worth its cognitive and UI cost, and to the answer said "okay to set conflicting sources aside. Document it"). Evidence, the detection test and the plan: `docs/research/brief-conflicting-sources.md`. **What already protects him:** an exercise from an opinion piece or a contested claim names who says it (`enrich.md`, kept by remixes, checked by `eval/questions.ts`), the tutor says so and cites both when his source and another disagree (`tutor.md`), and the Dig (D325) sets a skill's key points side by side, a row per source. **Why not the version first proposed** (a two-passage exercise of its own, a label for the kind of disagreement, a saved line for where he lands, a memory of dismissed false alarms, a claims list per source): a lot of screen and two more AI calls a source for something rare, in an app that has been taking elements away (D329, D333), before he has met one conflict in his own library. **The plan that waits:** when a new source is drafted it is also checked against the skill's Explain exercises; one it contradicts on an idea central to both gets a "two sources" remix on the normal exercise screen, both passages with who is speaking, asking for the strongest case for each and why they differ (resolving a conflict beats understanding each text, Kobayashi 2015; readers side with what they read first, Richter & Maier 2017). The feedback he already gets says why they differ and, **his pick of 2026-11-17, where the wider evidence leans, with outside sources, after his answer and never in the question** (treating an outdated claim as an open debate is false balance; the wrong idea beside its correction beats the correction alone, Schroeder & Kucera 2022). It replaces that exercise's review, so the queue does not grow, and retiring it is the dismiss. Never a bare "these disagree" mark (conflict without an explanation breeds distrust of the whole field, Lee, Nagler & Wang 2017). Come back to this when two of his sources disagree; finding conflicts against existing exercises is untested.

- D339. **A chat he had with Claude comes in as a source, and the tutor carries it on** (Tyler, 2026-11-20, after a long chat with Claude's learn skill: "I wish I could somehow port this into Ore and continue where I left off"; of three shapes on a card he picked "Source, then tutor continues"). Why a source and not only a tutor chat: a tutor chat is never highlighted, searched, filed under a skill or reviewed, so what he learned in it would stay out of the loop.
- D340. **Home's Continue reading shows each picture whole, in its own shape** (Tyler, 2026-11-20, of the 64×36 pictures: "too small", then "make it look as good as possible, given what we need to convey"; amends D114 and D327's 64px picture on the Mac's Home, D77's one 16:9 tile there). The picture's job on this list is to tell him which source a row is before he reads its title. One 16:9 crop failed that for most of what he reads: a PDF's first page showed a strip of it, a show's square art lost its top and bottom, and a tall chart showed its empty middle. So the picture is drawn whole, as Finder and Books draw theirs: a page or a cover upright, a video's frame wide, a show's art square, each centred in one 96×72 slot so the titles still line up, on an 88px row. A frame in the slot is the source lists' 96×54 (D130). A shape narrower than a page or wider than a frame is cut to that, so nothing becomes a sliver. A source with no picture keeps its kind's icon, on a tile of its kind's shape (upright for a PDF or a book). The reading line (D326) stays on the picture's foot. The iPhone's Today and the source lists keep their 16:9 picture for now. Revisit if the mixed shapes read as untidy in daily use, or to bring the source lists in line. (Replaced 2026-11-21 by D341: he found the mixed shapes untidy the same day.)
- D341. **A Continue reading row is Readwise Reader's, with a wide picture** (Tyler, 2026-11-21, of D340's rows: "i dont like the fix. copy reader by readwise", then "the thumbnails are not good"; he picked B of three mocks: Reader's square, a wide picture, the picture on the right. Replaces D340; amends D244's New mark on the Mac). Why D340 failed: pictures of different shapes made a ragged column, a page shrunk whole was a bright block that said nothing, and a tall row held one line. Now every row has one 96×54 box, the source lists' (D130), and two lines beside it.
  1. **Picture.** A wide picture fills the box; a PDF's page or a tall capture fills it from the top, where its title is; a cover or a show's square art is shown whole on a blurred copy of itself, since a strip of a cover is no cover. Reader's own square was not copied: it cuts a wide share picture mid-word, and most of what he reads comes with a wide one. The reading line stays on the picture's foot (D327).
  2. **Under the title**, one quiet line of what he picks a source by: New (if not opened), how long is left, the site or the course, the author, then its skills. How long is left leads because it answers "does this fit the time I have" (the daily session is 10 minutes to an hour). What doesn't fit drops whole from the end; nothing is cut to a stub.
  3. **How long is left** is its words (`ore ls` counts them) past where he is, at 230 a minute read, 150 for a video or talk (its transcript's pace). A rough guide, in whole minutes; revisit with his own pace if the numbers feel off.
  Not built: a blank first page still shows as a blank box (telling one needs the picture's pixels at import); no summary line, since Ore has none and it would be filler; the iPhone's Today and the source lists keep their rows. Mocks: `/mnt/project-files/home-rows/`.
  1. **In.** The shared page (claude.ai/share/…) is built in the browser, so it comes from the Browser tab add; its link alone is refused with that next step (D96). What he asked is the outline, a heading each (a long message cut at a word, then quoted in full); under it the reply's own prose, its own headings two levels down so the outline stays what he asked. The page's buttons and "Ran 5 agents" lines are not content. Where a reply showed an interactive visual or quiz, a line says one was here: the shared copy leaves them out, and so the files Claude made; a file he attached to a message gets the same line. `chat: claude` in the source's meta; its day is the first message's, where he is.
  2. **Read already.** He was in it, so nothing is asked before it (no prequestion block or Reading for chip on the Mac; the block comes back only to say its questions couldn't be written, with Try again), a finish covers all of it however far he scrolled here, and the top of the reader offers its first step: "What do you remember from this chat?" with Recall, which opens the same closed-book finish as any source (D18). Its prequestions are still written, and asked there.
  3. **Carried on.** The tutor gets a chat source whole up to 30,000 words (D338's limit for a chat handed over) where another source goes in parts past 15,000 (D290): it is the history it continues from. Checked on his real chat (14,165 words, ending on a question to him): with no instruction added, the tutor took his answer to that last question, judged it, cited the chat and asked the next one, so the prompt is unchanged (his rule: an instruction only where a test fails).
  4. A message that long no longer fits as a command-line argument on Linux, so past 64 KB it goes to `claude` on stdin (F421, the Claude half).
  Not built, for now: ChatGPT's shared pages; a chat export file; the same on the iPhone's reader (it still shows the prequestion block; a phone change needs the simulator check); telling him when the tutor cites what an AI said and no source backs. Revisit the last if he finds himself trusting a chat's claim that review then repeats. Code: `chatMarkdown` in `packages/core/src/ingest/rendered.ts`, `wholeUpTo` in `ai/tutor.ts`, the line in `apps/desktop/src/Reader.tsx`.
- D342. **The Add box keeps what he typed and left** (Tyler, 2026-11-21, of the basic UX sweep's "Esc throws away what you typed": "your rec"). Esc or a click outside closes the box; the next ⌘N shows the words again, selected whole, so ↵ adds them and typing or a paste replaces them (Spotlight). They go once added, once he empties the box, and when Ore quits; Add and Follow each keep their own. Why: the tutor's field, a Finish recall and a Review answer already keep a draft, and a slip of Esc cost a pasted text. Revisit if stale words in the box get in his way more than a lost draft did. F449.
- D343. **In a source with the sidebar folded and the window under 1040px wide, the ambience radio sits small in the top bar, after Forward** (Tyler, 2026-11-21, of the radio over the first letters of a source's lines: "yeah move it"; bends D254's bottom left for this case only). Why: there the reading column starts at the window's edge and no bottom corner is free of text; the top bar is. It opens the same screen. 1040px is where the default side panel leaves the column a 76px gutter; a wider side panel can still put text under the radio in a wider window, and Home and the lists with the sidebar folded keep it at the bottom left over their left edge. Revisit if he meets either. F450.
- D344. **The iPhone's Collected page beside a source has the title "Collected"** (Tyler, 2026-11-21: "give it collected"), as Sources and Settings name themselves; scrolled, it shows small in the bar. The note page beside it stays bare: the note is the page. F451.
- D345. **A source needs no skill: the ones with none have a row in the Mac's sidebar, and a source can carry its own importance** (Tyler, 2026-11-22: he wanted to read a values guide, no skill fit and a "values" skill was not worth making; he floated hand-typed tags, then picked this after the survey in the project doc "How Ore should organize sources"). Why no tags: a skill is something he would set a goal for (D174), so the guide was not unfiled, it was a read with no goal behind it; given folders and tags side by side, people file under one label and rarely use tags to find things again (Bergman et al. 2013), and every praised library app has a named home for the unsorted instead (Zotero's Unfiled Items, Raindrop's Unsorted).
- D-new-paddle-pages. **A PDF's pages are read by PaddleOCR-VL on the Mac: its headings, tables and display formulas, on every PDF added** (Tyler, 2026-11-22: "yeah lets do your plan", after a race of five readers on 20 of his own PDFs). Why: Tyler asked for the simplest PDF reading as good as or better than Readwise Reader's. On 16 of his PDFs scored against answer keys (words found / clean / headings / tables / math, out of 100): today's reader 93/90/51/0/24, Docling 94/93/80/69/39, PaddleOCR-VL 93/94/87/77/67, GLM-OCR 93/94/87/78/70, Claude (Sonnet) repairing pages 99/97/93/88/48. The keys were written by Claude, which favours the Claude reader; scored against each PDF's own text the gap in words shrinks to about 4 points, Claude costs his usage on every page, took about 13 s a page and refused 10 pages of old copyrighted scans. A check that sends only "likely wrong" pages to a model was dropped: 47 of 83 pages needed help once a missed heading counts, and no cheap sign spots a missed heading. PaddleOCR-VL 1.6 (Apache-2.0, 0.96B parameters, 1.9 GB) with its layout model PP-DocLayoutV3 (Apache-2.0) took about 6 s a page on his M4 Pro through mlx-vlm (MIT), one request at a time (mlx-vlm 0.8.0 garbles pages read side by side); GLM-OCR scored the same but dropped about 160 words of one page and was slower. So it replaces Granite-Docling (D273) as the optional Mac download (`ore tables --install`, about 4 GB, into `~/Library/Application Support/Ore/paddle`; installing it deletes the old `docling` folder), and once it is there every PDF added is read in the background, not only one whose tables or formulas were spotted. The words stay pdf.js's own (D125): the model says where a heading, a table and a formula are, pdf.js's text fills them, so no word is the model's guess and highlights never move. On a page the model read, a heading is what it saw as one (a heading in the body's size is found, a large pull quote is not); a page it has not read keeps the size rule. No model picker: the only control is the download in Settings › AI. The model's blocks also give the order a page is read in, used where pdf.js drew it in another (a designed report's page: words found there rose from 69 and 76 to 83 and 87) but only where that order never climbs back up a column, since on two-column papers the model's order was sometimes scrambled and the PDF's own was right. Where the page has no text of its own the model's is used: a scanned page whole, and on a page with text a table, formula or caption that is a picture; prose inside a picture (a screenshot's words) stays in the picture. Built this way, the same 16 PDFs score 96/92/86/77/38 (Paddle's own Markdown: 93/94/87/77/67); the words that are not the page's own text (7 in 1,000) are formulas' LaTeX and the tables and captions read from pictures. Still open: inline math stays plain text (the intake grid's gap), which is the 38; a 900-page book takes about 95 minutes in the background; each PDF pays about 12 s to load the model; the iPhone gets the result through sync. Revisit if a page reader that scores higher on these PDFs, at a permissive licence and a size a Mac can run, appears, or if real use shows a kind of page Paddle gets wrong; `/mnt/project-files/pdf-race/` holds the samples' scores.
  1. **No skill** is a row under All sources, shown only while a live source has no skill. It opens All sources with the filter's own No skill tag in the search (D286, D288), so what hides the rest is in sight and its × lets it go; an open source with no skill sits under it, as a source sits under its skill.
  2. **A new source no longer opens the skill picker by itself** (amends D89 and D109, his "I think drop"). The picker opening on every source added with no skill read as "a skill is required". The byline's faint Skill (F454) and the row's + file it when he wants.
  3. **Importance on a source** (his "if we have importance in a note with no skill, I wonder if we should just have importance for all sources"): Importance ▸ in a source's menus on the Mac (its row, the reader's ⋯, ⌘K), the skill's three levels (D173), ticked at the one its exercises have now. `priority` in the source's frontmatter is written only when he sets it (`ore edit --priority`); set, it wins over its skills either way (a light read inside a Focus skill, a no-skill guide that matters); unset, its exercises take the highest of their skills', Normal with none (`cardPriority`). Read each time and never copied, so a skill's importance changing rewrites nothing. Once set on a source that has skills, a last row, Same as its skills, hands it back; with no skill, Normal is that.
  Not built: the iPhone's drawer row and menu item; the AI's topics shown as a Topic filter he can edit (the zero-upkeep tags). Revisit topics the first time he wants every source on a theme across skills and search does not get him there.

## Archive (2026-11-20)
History only: these set the first builds' order and scope and are done or overtaken (D122 covers what imports). They no longer steer new work.
- D12. Build order: core + CLI (v0) → Claude skill + Raycast extension (v0) → Tauri app (v1). Nothing in v0 is thrown away.
- D13. v1 formats: web articles, PDFs, YouTube transcripts, Markdown/text, pasted snippets, EPUB (moved up from v2 on 2026-10-21 so books can be read now). v2: screenshots with OCR, X threads. v3: newsletters, audio, Kindle. No Readwise/Kindle importer.
- D14. v1 entry points: global hotkey panel (URL, clipboard, selection, dropped file), drag and drop on the window, watched `inbox/` folder. No Share sheet (Tyler does not use it). Browser extension later. Raycast extension in v0 as the hotkey stand-in. (Amended 2026-10-28, D95: the hotkey panel and the watched `inbox/` folder are dropped; paste, drop, ⌘N and Raycast cover capture.)
- D16. System-wide capture (select anywhere → hotkey → same four intents, optional screenshot + OCR) is v2. v1 lays down: the same popup component, the same intents, a `snippet` item type with source app and URL.
